The Parliament
in the interest of protecting the inviolable and inalienable fundamental rights of human beings, as well as the independence, territorial integrity, economy of the country, and the national wealth,
taking into account Hungary's international legal and European Union obligations,
for the purpose of enforcing the exclusive punitive power of the state
enacts the following law:

## 1. §

(1) The criminal liability of the perpetrator may only be established for an act which – with the exception of acts punishable under generally recognized rules of international law – was prescribed by law to be punished at the time of commission.  
(2) No punishment may be imposed or measure applied for the commission of a criminal offense which was not provided for by law at the time of commission – or in the case of application of Section 2(2), at the time of adjudication.

## 2. §

(1) A criminal offense shall be adjudicated according to the criminal law in force at the time of its commission – with the exceptions set out in paragraphs (2)–(3).  
(2) If, according to the new criminal law in force at the time of adjudication of the act, the act is no longer a criminal offense, or is to be adjudicated more leniently, then the new criminal law shall be applied. *
(3) The new criminal law shall be applied with retroactive effect in the adjudication of an act punishable under generally recognized rules of international law, if it was not punishable under Hungarian criminal law at the time of commission.

## 3. § (1) Hungarian criminal law shall be applied to

a) a criminal offense committed in the territory of Hungary,
b) a criminal offense committed on a floating installation flying the Hungarian flag or on an aircraft bearing Hungarian markings located outside the territory of Hungary,
c) an act committed abroad by a Hungarian citizen which is a criminal offense under Hungarian law.  
(2) Hungarian criminal law shall also be applied to
a) an act committed abroad by a non-Hungarian citizen, if it
aa) is a criminal offense under Hungarian law and is also punishable under the law of the place of commission,
ab) * is a criminal offense against the state – except for espionage committed against allied armed forces and espionage against the institutions of the European Union – regardless of whether it is punishable under the law of the place of commission,
ac) is a criminal offense defined in Chapter XIII or XIV, or any other criminal offense the prosecution of which is prescribed by an international treaty promulgated by law,
b) an act committed abroad by a non-Hungarian citizen against a Hungarian citizen, a legal person established under Hungarian law, or other legal entity without legal personality, which is punishable under Hungarian law.  
(3) In the cases specified in paragraph (2), the initiation of criminal proceedings shall be ordered by the Chief Prosecutor.

## 4. §

(1) A criminal offense is an act committed intentionally or – if this law also prescribes punishment for negligent commission – negligently, which is dangerous to society, and for which this law prescribes the imposition of punishment. *
(2) An act dangerous to society is an activity or omission which violates or endangers the person or rights of others, or the social, economic, or state order of Hungary as defined by the Fundamental Law.

## 5. §

A criminal offense is either a felony or a misdemeanor. A felony is an intentionally committed criminal offense for which this law prescribes the imposition of a punishment more severe than two years' imprisonment; all other criminal offenses are misdemeanors.

## 6. §

(1) There is a concurrence of offenses when one or more acts of the perpetrator constitute multiple criminal offenses and they are adjudicated in a single proceeding.  
(2) * It is not a concurrence of offenses, but a criminal offense committed in continuation, when the perpetrator commits the same criminal offense multiple times within a short period of time, with a unified intent, against the same victim.  
(3) * If multiple or continuous acts of the perpetrator constitute one criminal offense, or multiple criminal offenses of the perpetrator constitute one criminal offense pursuant to a provision of this law, and charges have been brought against the perpetrator for this criminal offense, another similar criminal offense committed by the perpetrator after the bringing of charges shall be adjudicated as an independent criminal offense.

## 7. §

A criminal offense is committed intentionally by a person who desires the consequences of his or her act, or acquiesces in such consequences.

## 8. §

A criminal offense is committed negligently by a person who foresees the possible consequences of his or her act but recklessly relies on their non-occurrence, or does not foresee the possible consequences of his or her act because he or she fails to exercise the attention or care that can be expected of him or her.

## 9. §

More severe legal consequences attached to a result as a qualifying circumstance of a criminal offense may be applied if the perpetrator is at least negligent with respect to the result.

## 10. §

(1) A person is punishable for attempt who commences the commission of an intentional criminal offense but does not complete it.  
(2) The penalty scale for the completed criminal offense shall be applied to an attempt.  
(3) The punishment may be reduced without limitation or may also be waived if the attempt is committed on an unsuitable object, with an unsuitable instrument, or in an unsuitable manner.  
(4) A person is not punishable for attempt
a) whose voluntary withdrawal results in the non-completion of the criminal offense, or
b) who voluntarily prevents the occurrence of the result.  
(5) If in the cases specified in paragraph (4) the attempt in itself already constitutes another criminal offense, the perpetrator may be punished for that criminal offense.

## 11. §

(1) If this law specifically so provides, a person is punishable for preparation who, for the purpose of committing a criminal offense, secures the necessary conditions or conditions facilitating it, invites, offers, undertakes to commit it, or agrees to commit it jointly.  
(2) A person is not punishable for preparation
a) whose voluntary withdrawal results in the non-commencement of the commission of the criminal offense,
b) who, for the purpose of preventing the commission, withdraws his or her previous invitation, offer, or undertaking, or endeavors to have the other participants withdraw from the commission, provided that the commencement of the commission of the criminal offense does not occur for any reason, or
c) who reports the preparation to the authorities before the commencement of the commission of the criminal offense.  
(3) * If in the cases specified in paragraph (2) the preparation in itself already constitutes another criminal offense, the perpetrator may be punished for that criminal offense.

## 12. §

Perpetrators are the principal, the indirect principal and the co-principal (hereinafter together: principals), as well as the instigator and the accomplice (hereinafter together: accessories).

## 13. § (1) The principal is the person who realizes the statutory elements of the criminal offense.

(2) The indirect principal is the person who realizes the statutory elements of an intentional criminal offense by using a person who is not punishable for this act due to infancy, morbid mental state, duress or threat, or who is in error.  
(3) Co-principals are persons who, being aware of each other's activity, jointly realize the statutory elements of an intentional criminal offense.

## 14. § (1) The instigator is the person who intentionally induces another to commit a criminal offense.

(2) The accomplice is the person who intentionally provides assistance to another in the commission of a criminal offense.  
(3) The penalty scale established for the principals shall also be applied to the accessories.

## 15. §

The punishability of the perpetrator or the punishability of the act is excluded or limited by:
a) infancy,
b) morbid mental state,
c) duress and threat,
d) error,
e) self-defense,
f) necessity,
g) authorization by law,
h) other grounds specified by law.

## 16. §

* A person who has not reached the age of fourteen at the time of committing a punishable act is not punishable, except
a) for homicide [Section 160 (1)–(2)],
b) for homicide committed in a state of strong agitation (Section 161),
c) for bodily harm [Section 164 (8)],
d) for violence against an official [Section 310 (1)–(3)],
e) for violence against a person performing public duties [Section 311, if qualified according to Section 310 (1)–(3)],
f) for violence against a supporter of an official or a person performing public duties [Section 312, if qualified according to Section 310 (1)–(3)],
g) for an act of terrorism [Section 314 (1)–(2)],
h) for robbery [Section 365 (1)–(4)], and
i) for plundering [Section 366 (2)–(3)]
the perpetrator, if at the time of committing the crime he or she had reached the age of twelve and at the time of commission possessed the capacity necessary to recognise the consequences of the crime.

## 17. §

(1) A person who commits a punishable act in such a pathological state of mental functioning that renders him or her incapable of recognising the consequences of his or her act or of acting in accordance with such recognition is not punishable.  
(2) The punishment may be mitigated without limitation if the pathological state of mental functioning limits the perpetrator in recognising the consequences of the crime or in acting in accordance with such recognition.

## 18. §

Section 17 shall not apply to a person who commits the crime in a state of intoxication or stupor resulting from his or her own fault.

## 19. §

(1) A person who commits a punishable act under the influence of coercion or threat that renders him or her incapable of conduct in accordance with his or her will is not punishable.  
(2) The punishment may be mitigated without limitation if the coercion or threat limits the perpetrator of the crime in conduct in accordance with his or her will.

## 20. §

(1) The perpetrator is not punishable for a fact of which he or she was not aware at the time of commission.  
(2) A person who commits a punishable act in the mistaken belief that it is not dangerous to society and has reasonable grounds for such belief is not punishable.  
(3) Mistake does not exclude punishability if it is caused by negligence and this Act provides for the punishment of negligent commission as well.

## 21. §

The act of a person who causes harm to an unlawful attacker with a defensive device not capable of taking life, installed for the purpose of preventing an unlawful attack against his or her own or others' person or property, is not punishable, provided that the defender has done everything that could reasonably be expected in the given situation to ensure that the defensive device installed by him or her does not cause harm.

## 22. §

(1) An act that is necessary to repel an unlawful attack directed against or directly threatening his or her own or another person or persons, property or the public interest is not punishable.  
(2) An unlawful attack shall be regarded as if it were also directed at taking the life of the defender if
a) it is committed against a person
aa) at night,
ab) with a weapon,
ac) armed, or
ad) in a group,
b) it is an unlawful intrusion into a dwelling
ba) at night,
bb) with a weapon,
bc) armed, or
bd) in a group, or
c) it is an unlawful intrusion with a weapon into an enclosed area belonging to a dwelling.  
(3) A person who exceeds the necessary extent of defence out of fright or excusable agitation is not punishable.  
(4) The person attacked is not obliged to evade the unlawful attack.

## 23. §

(1) The act of a person who saves his or her own or another person or property from immediate danger that cannot otherwise be averted, or acts in this way for the protection of the public interest, is not punishable, provided that the act does not cause greater harm than that which he or she sought to avert.  
(2) A person who causes greater harm than that which he or she sought to avert because, out of fright or excusable agitation, he or she does not recognise the magnitude of the harm is not punishable.  
(3) Necessity cannot be established in favour of a person to whom the causing of the danger is attributable or for whom the assumption of the danger is a duty by virtue of his or her occupation.

## 24. §

An act that is permitted or declared unpunishable by law is not punishable.

## 25. § Punishability is terminated by

a) the death of the perpetrator,
b) limitation,
c) pardon,
d) active repentance,
e) other grounds specified by law.

## 26. §

(1) Punishability – with the exception of those specified in paragraphs (2)–(3) and in the absence of a different provision in the Act on the exclusion of limitation for certain crimes – is subject to limitation after the expiry of a period corresponding to the upper limit of the sentencing range, but at least five years.  
(2) * Punishability for crimes specified in Chapter XXVII is subject to limitation after twelve years.  
(3) * Punishability is not subject to limitation for
a) crimes specified in Chapters XIII and XIV,
b) crimes punishable by life imprisonment, and
c) crimes specified in Chapter XIX punishable more severely than five years' imprisonment if the victim of the crime had not reached the age of eighteen at the time of the commission of the crime.

## 27. § The starting date of limitation is

a) in the case of a completed crime, the day on which the statutory elements are fulfilled,
b) in the case of attempt and preparation, the day on which the act constituting them ends,
c) in the case of a crime that is committed solely by the omission of the performance of a duty, the day on which the perpetrator could still fulfil his or her duty without the consequences established in this Act,
d) in the case of a crime consisting in the maintenance of an unlawful state, the day on which this state ceases.

## 28. §

(1) * Limitation is interrupted by a criminal procedural act taken by the court, the prosecution service, the investigating authority or, in cases with an international dimension, by the minister responsible for justice or a foreign authority against the perpetrator on account of the crime. On the date of interruption, the limitation period begins again.  
(1a) * If the victim of homicide committed in a state of strong agitation, intentional grievous bodily harm punishable more severely than three years' imprisonment, kidnapping, human trafficking and forced labour, violation of personal liberty or – with the exception provided in Section 26 (3) (c) – a crime against the freedom of sexual life and sexual morality had not reached the age of eighteen at the time of the commission of the crime, the period during which he or she has not reached or would not have reached the age of twenty-one is not included in the limitation period.  
(2) * If criminal proceedings are suspended, the duration of the suspension is not included in the limitation period. This provision shall not apply if the criminal proceedings are suspended because the identity of the perpetrator could not be established in the investigation, he or she is at an unknown location, or has become mentally ill, or if the accused at liberty is at a known location abroad and the criminal proceedings cannot be conducted in his or her absence.  
(3) * The period during which criminal proceedings could not be initiated or continued on the basis of immunity arising from the right of immunity because the right of immunity guaranteed by law was not suspended by the person entitled to decide is not included in the limitation period. This provision shall not apply to crimes punishable on private motion in respect of which the prosecution is represented by the private prosecutor.  
(4) * In the case of probation, the duration of the probation period and the duration of reparation work are not included in the limitation period.

## 29. §

(1) A person who, until the indictment is brought, confessed to the commission of a misdemeanour or a felony punishable by no more than three years' imprisonment against life, bodily integrity and health, against human freedom, against human dignity and certain fundamental rights, traffic-related, against property, or against intellectual property rights, and within the framework of a mediation procedure – or prior to it, but approved in an agreement concluded within the framework of the mediation procedure – made reparation for the harm caused by the criminal offence in a manner and to an extent accepted by the victim, shall not be punishable. This provision shall also apply if, among the criminal offences in concurrence, the criminal offence against life, bodily integrity and health, against human freedom, against human dignity and certain fundamental rights, traffic-related, against property or against intellectual property rights is the determining one.  
(2) The punishment may be mitigated without limitation if, in the case of the criminal offences specified in paragraph (1), the perpetrator confessed to the commission of a felony punishable by no more than five years' imprisonment until the indictment is brought, and within the framework of a mediation procedure – or prior to it, but approved in an agreement concluded within the framework of the mediation procedure – made reparation for the harm caused by the criminal offence in a manner and to an extent accepted by the victim. This provision shall also apply if, among the criminal offences in concurrence, the criminal offence against life, bodily integrity and health, against human freedom, against human dignity and certain fundamental rights, traffic-related, against property or against intellectual property rights is the determining one.  
(3) Paragraphs (1)–(2) shall not apply if the perpetrator
a) is a multiple or special recidivist,
b) committed the criminal offence in a criminal organisation,
c) his or her criminal offence caused death,
d) * committed the intentional criminal offence during the probationary period of the suspension of imprisonment or after being sentenced to imprisonment to be executed for an intentional criminal offence, before the completion of the execution of the imprisonment, or during the period of probation or conditional prosecutorial suspension, or
e) * previously participated in a mediation procedure for an intentional criminal offence, and as a result paragraphs (1) or (2) were applied to him or her, provided that two years have not yet elapsed from the date of the decision not subject to further legal remedy until the commission of the further intentional criminal offence.

## 30. § Criminal prosecution is precluded by

a) the lack of a private motion,
b) the lack of a complaint.

## 31. §

(1) In the cases specified in this Act, the perpetrator of a criminal offence may be punished only upon a private motion.  
(2) The victim is entitled to submit the private motion.  
(3) * In the cases specified in Section 69 (5) of Act XC of 2017 on Criminal Procedure, the private motion may be submitted by the legal representative of the victim. The private motion may also be submitted independently by the victim who is a minor with limited legal capacity, and his or her legal representative is also entitled to do so. In these cases, the guardianship authority is also entitled to submit the private motion. In case of conflict of interest, the provisions of the Civil Code shall apply.  
(4) If the victim entitled to submit the private motion dies, his or her relative is entitled to submit the private motion.  
(5) A private motion submitted against any perpetrator is effective against all perpetrators.  
(6) The private motion may not be withdrawn.

## 32. §

In the cases specified in this Act, the perpetrator of a criminal offence may be punished only upon a complaint made by the person entitled to do so.

## 33. § (1) Punishments are

a) imprisonment,
b) confinement,
c) community service work,
d) fine,
e) disqualification from occupation,
f) disqualification from driving,
g) expulsion,
h) disqualification from attending sports events,
i) expulsion from the territory of Hungary.  
(2) Disqualification from public affairs is an accessory punishment.  
(3) Punishments – with the exceptions set out in paragraphs (5) and (6) – may also be imposed in combination.  
(4) * If the lower limit of the sentencing range of the criminal offence does not reach one year of imprisonment, instead of imprisonment confinement, community service work, fine, disqualification from occupation, disqualification from driving, expulsion, disqualification from attending sports events or expulsion from the territory of Hungary, or several of these punishments may be imposed.  
(5) If this Act provides for a criminal offence to be punished by confinement, instead of or in addition to this punishment community service work, fine, disqualification from occupation, disqualification from driving, expulsion, disqualification from attending sports events or expulsion from the territory of Hungary, or several of these punishments may be imposed.  
(6) The following may not be imposed:
a) confinement or community service work in addition to imprisonment,
b) community service work or fine in addition to expulsion from the territory of Hungary,
c) * fine in addition to life imprisonment.

## 34. § Imprisonment is for a definite period or for life

## 35. §

(1) If the court imposes imprisonment, it shall order its execution in a prison, correctional facility or penitentiary.  
(2) Having regard to the circumstances relevant to the imposition of the punishment, a degree of execution one degree more lenient or one degree more severe than that specified by law may be determined. This provision shall not apply to life imprisonment imposed pursuant to Section 44 (1)–(2) and Section 90 (2).

## 36. §

The shortest duration of imprisonment for a definite period is three months, the longest duration is twenty years; in case of commission in a criminal organisation, as a special or multiple recidivist, or in case of concurrence or cumulative punishment, twenty-five years.

## 37. §

(1) The degree of execution of the punishment of imprisonment is prison if it was imposed for a misdemeanour, except if the convicted person is a recidivist.  
(2) The degree of execution of the punishment of imprisonment is correctional facility if it was imposed
a) for a felony, or
b) for a misdemeanour and the convicted person is a recidivist.  
(3) The degree of execution of the punishment of imprisonment is penitentiary if
a) imprisonment of three years or longer duration was imposed for
aa) a criminal offence specified in Chapter XIII, XIV or XXIV,
ab) a military criminal offence also punishable by life imprisonment,
ac) * act of terrorism [Section 314 (1)–(2), Sections 315–316/A], financing of terrorism [Sections 318 and 318/A], hijacking of a vehicle [Section 320 (1)–(3)], participation in a criminal organisation [Section 321 (1)], misuse of explosives or explosive devices (Section 324), misuse of firearms or ammunition [Section 325 (1)–(3)], misuse of weapons prohibited by international treaty [Section 326 (1)–(6)], misuse of defence technology products or services [Section 329 (1)–(4)], misuse of dual-use items [Section 330 (1)–(3)],
ad) * more severely qualified cases of homicide, drug trafficking, possession of drugs, kidnapping, trafficking in human beings and forced labour, sexual violence, causing public danger, violation of international economic prohibition or robbery [Section 160 (2), Section 176 (2)–(3), Section 177 (1)–(2), Section 178 (2), Section 179 (2), Section 190 (2)–(4), Section 192 (1)–(6), Section 197 (2)–(4), Section 322 (2)–(3), Section 327 (3), Section 365 (3)–(4)]
or
b) * the imprisonment is of two years or longer duration, and
ba) the convicted person is a multiple recidivist, or
bb) the criminal offence was committed in a criminal organisation.

## 38. §

(1) * In the case of imposing imprisonment for a definite period, the court shall establish in its decision on the merits the earliest date of conditional release, or – in the cases specified in paragraph (4) – that the possibility of conditional release is excluded.
(2) If the possibility of conditional release is not excluded, its earliest date is the day following the completion of
a) two-thirds of the sentence,
b) three-quarters in the case of a recidivist,
but at least three months.
(3) * In the case of imposing imprisonment not exceeding five years – in a case deserving special consideration – the court may order in its decision on the merits that the convicted person may be conditionally released after serving half of the sentence. This provision shall not apply if the convicted person is a multiple recidivist.
(4) The following may not be conditionally released:
a) a multiple recidivist, if the imprisonment is to be executed in a penitentiary degree,
b) a violent multiple recidivist,
c) a person who committed the criminal offence in a criminal organisation,
d) a person who was sentenced to imprisonment for an intentional criminal offence committed after being sentenced to imprisonment for a definite period to be executed, before the completion of the execution or the termination of enforceability,
e) * a person who was sentenced as a principal to imprisonment to be executed for the following criminal offences:
ea) genocide, crime against humanity, apartheid, if committed by intentionally causing death [Section 142 (1) point a), Section 143 (1) point a), Section 144 (1) point a)],
eb) homicide [Section 160 (1) and (2)],
ec) kidnapping [Section 190 (4)],
ed) more severely qualified cases of prisoner mutiny, terrorist act, seizure of a vehicle and mutiny, if committed by intentionally causing death [Section 284 (4), Section 314 (1), Section 320 (2), Section 442 (4)], or
ee) violence against a superior or service officer [Section 445 (5) point a)].
(5) * The following may also not be conditionally released:
a) a person sentenced to imprisonment to be executed
aa) for preparation,
ab) as an accomplice, or
ac) with the application of unlimited mitigation,
in the case of committing a criminal offence specified in paragraph (4) point e),
b) for a violent criminal offence against a person committed to the detriment of their relative, punishable by imprisonment of eight years or more severe, or
c) for a criminal offence against sexual freedom and sexual morality committed to the detriment of a person who has not reached the age of eighteen, punishable by imprisonment of eight years or more severe.
(6) * The court may rule in its decision on the merits on the eligibility for conditional release of the offender specified in paragraph (5), if, having regard to the circumstances of the commission, the degree of danger to society inherent in the offender's person or other sentencing circumstances, the protection of society and the prevention of the offender committing a new criminal offence can be achieved by ordering probation supervision and determining a specific rule of conduct.
(7) * For the purposes of paragraph (5) point b), relative shall also mean the parent of the offender's child, former spouse, former cohabitant, guardian, ward, legal guardian or ward.

## 39. §

(1) * In the case of imprisonment for a definite period, the duration of conditional release is equal to the remaining part of the imprisonment, but at least one year. In the application of Section 38 (3), the court may order in its decision on the merits that the duration of conditional release is extended by at least one and at most three years.
(2) If the remaining part of the imprisonment is shorter than one year and its execution has not been ordered, the sentence shall be considered completed – after the expiry of the conditional release – on the last day of the remaining part.

## 40. § (1) * The court shall terminate the conditional release if the convicted person is sentenced to imprisonment to be executed

a) for a criminal offence committed during the period of conditional release after the decision on the merits has become final, or
b) for a criminal offence committed during the period of conditional release.
(2) The court may terminate the conditional release if the convicted person is sentenced to a punishment other than those specified in paragraph (1).
(3) In the case of termination of conditional release, the time spent on conditional release shall not be counted towards the imprisonment.
(4) * If, during the period of conditional release, imprisonment imposed on the convicted person for a criminal offence committed before the earlier decision on the merits became final is to be executed, the execution of the imprisonment interrupts the conditional release, and the court postpones the earliest date of continuation of the conditional release
a) until the date of conditional release granted from the subsequently imposed imprisonment, or
b) – if the possibility of conditional release is excluded in the case of the subsequently imposed imprisonment – for the duration of the execution of the imprisonment.

## 41. §

(1) * Life imprisonment may be imposed on a person who has reached the age of twenty at the time of committing the criminal offence. This provision also applies to life imprisonment that may be imposed pursuant to Section 90 (2).
(2) The degree of execution of life imprisonment is penitentiary.

## 42. §

* In the case of imposing life imprisonment, the court shall determine in its decision on the merits the earliest date of conditional release, or exclude the possibility of conditional release.

## 43. §

(1) If, in the case of imposing life imprisonment, the court does not exclude the possibility of conditional release, it shall establish its earliest date at a minimum of twenty-five and a maximum of forty years. The earliest date of conditional release shall be determined in years.
(2) In the case of life imprisonment, the duration of conditional release is at least fifteen years.

## 44. §

(1) In the case of imposing life imprisonment, the court may exclude the possibility of conditional release only for the following criminal offences:
a) genocide [Section 142 (1)],
b) crime against humanity [Section 143 (1)],
c) apartheid [Section 144 (1) and (3)],
d) more severely qualified case of violence against a war envoy [Section 148 (2)],
e) violence against protected persons [Section 149 (1)–(2)],
f) use of weapons prohibited by international treaty [Section 155 (1)],
g) other war crime (Section 158),
h) more severely qualified case of homicide [Section 160 (2)],
i) more severely qualified case of kidnapping [Section 190 (3)–(4)],
j) * more severely qualified case of trafficking in human beings and forced labour [Section 192 (6)],
k) violent change of the constitutional order [Section 254 (1)],
l) more severely qualified case of sabotage [Section 257 (2)],
m) more severely qualified case of prisoner mutiny [Section 284 (4)],
n) terrorist act [Section 314 (1)],
o) more severely qualified case of seizure of a vehicle [Section 320 (2)],
p) more severely qualified case of causing public danger [Section 322 (3)],
q) more severely qualified case of mutiny [Section 442 (4)],
r) more severely qualified case of violence against a superior or service officer [Section 445 (5)],
if committed with violence against a person or against property.
(2) The possibility of conditional release must be excluded if the offender
a) is a violent multiple recidivist, or
b) committed the criminal offence specified in paragraph (1) in a criminal organisation.

## 45. §

(1) If the convicted person is sentenced to a determinate term of imprisonment to be executed during the execution of life imprisonment for a criminal offence committed before being sentenced to life imprisonment, the court shall postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the determinate term of imprisonment to be executed.  
(2) If the convicted person is sentenced to a determinate term of imprisonment to be executed during conditional release from life imprisonment for a criminal offence committed before being sentenced to life imprisonment, the court shall revoke the conditional release and postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the determinate term of imprisonment to be executed.  
(3) If the convicted person is sentenced to a determinate term of imprisonment during the execution of life imprisonment for a criminal offence committed during the execution of life imprisonment, the court shall postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the determinate term of imprisonment, but for a period of at least five and at most twenty years.  
(4) If the convicted person is sentenced to a determinate term of imprisonment during conditional release from life imprisonment for a criminal offence committed during the execution of life imprisonment, the court shall revoke the conditional release and postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the determinate term of imprisonment, but for a period of at least five and at most twenty years.  
(5) If the convicted person is sentenced to a determinate term of imprisonment for a criminal offence committed during conditional release from life imprisonment, the court shall revoke the conditional release and postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the determinate term of imprisonment, but for a period of at least five and at most twenty years.  
(6) * If the earliest date of conditional release from life imprisonment is postponed due to a determinate term of imprisonment pursuant to paragraphs (1), (2), (4) and (5), the earliest date of conditional release shall be determined taking into account the time of pre-trial detention and criminal supervision credited to the determinate term of imprisonment.  
(7) * The convicted person may not be conditionally released if he or she is repeatedly sentenced to life imprisonment. If the previous life imprisonment has not yet been executed, the repeatedly imposed life imprisonment may not be executed.  
(8) * The court shall revoke the conditional release if the convicted person is sentenced to repeated life imprisonment
a) during conditional release from life imprisonment, or
b) for a criminal offence committed during conditional release from life imprisonment.

## 46. §

(1) The duration of detention shall be determined in days, its shortest duration being five days and its longest duration ninety days.  
(2) Detention shall be executed in a penal institution.

## 47. §

(1) The duration of community service work shall be determined in hours, its minimum being forty-eight hours and its maximum three hundred and twelve hours.  
(2) * The convicted person shall perform community service work, unless otherwise provided by law, at least one day per week – on the weekly rest day or in his or her free time – without remuneration.  
(3) *
(4) A person sentenced to community service work is obliged to perform the work assigned to him or her. The offender may be obliged to perform work which – having regard to his or her state of health and qualifications – he or she can foreseeably perform.

## 48. §

(1) If the convicted person fails to perform the work assigned to him or her through his or her own fault, the community service work or the remaining part thereof shall be converted into imprisonment.  
(2) The imprisonment replacing community service work shall be executed in a penal institution, in prison grade.  
(3) The imprisonment replacing community service work may be shorter than three months.

## 49. §

If the convicted person is sentenced to imprisonment to be executed for a criminal offence committed after being sentenced to community service work, and the community service work sentence has not yet been executed, the community service work or the remaining part thereof shall be converted into imprisonment such that four hours of community service work corresponds to one day of imprisonment. After the conversion, the remaining community service work corresponds to one day of imprisonment.

## 50. §

(1) A fine shall be imposed by determining – having regard to the objective gravity of the criminal offence – the number of day-fines, and – in proportion to the offender's property, income, personal circumstances and way of life – the amount corresponding to one day-fine.  
(2) A person who is sentenced to a determinate term of imprisonment for a criminal offence committed for the purpose of gain shall also be sentenced to a fine if he or she has adequate income or property.  
(3) * The minimum amount of a fine is thirty day-fines and the maximum is five hundred and forty day-fines. The amount of one day-fine shall be determined at a minimum of one thousand three hundred forints and a maximum of six hundred and fifty thousand forints.  
(4) * The court may, in its decision on the merits – having regard to the offender's property and income circumstances – order that the offender may pay the fine in monthly instalments within a maximum of two years.

## 51. §

(1) If the convicted person does not pay the fine, or in the case of authorisation of payment in instalments fails to pay one monthly instalment, the fine or the unpaid part thereof shall be converted into imprisonment.  
(2) If the fine was imposed in addition to imprisonment to be executed, or the execution of suspended imprisonment was ordered, the execution grade of the imprisonment replacing the fine shall be governed by the grade of the imprisonment. In other cases, the imprisonment replacing the fine shall be executed in prison grade.  
(3) The imprisonment replacing the fine may be shorter than three months.

## 52. §

(1) A person may be disqualified from practicing an occupation if he or she commits the criminal offence
a) by violating the rules of his or her occupation requiring professional qualifications, or
b) by using his or her occupation, intentionally.  
(2) Paragraph (1) point a) may also be applied against a person who, at the time of committing the criminal offence, did not practice the activity as his or her occupation, but possesses the professional qualifications necessary for practicing the occupation by violating the rules of which he or she committed the criminal offence.  
(3) * A perpetrator of a criminal offence against the freedom of sexual life and sexual morality, if he or she committed the criminal offence against a person who has not reached the age of eighteen, or a perpetrator of child pornography, must be disqualified with permanent effect from practicing any occupation or other activity in the context of which he or she educates, supervises, cares for, or provides medical treatment to a person who has not reached the age of eighteen, or is in any other relationship of power or influence with such person.  
(4) * A perpetrator of the criminal offence of endangering a minor must be disqualified from practicing any occupation or other activity in the context of which he or she educates, supervises, cares for, or provides medical treatment to a person who has not reached the age of eighteen, or is in any other relationship of power or influence with such person. In cases deserving special consideration, the mandatory application of disqualification from occupation may be dispensed with.  
(5) * A perpetrator of the criminal offence of unlawful influencing of the will of voters must be disqualified from being a responsible person in any civil organisation and a leading officer in a party. In cases deserving special consideration, the mandatory application of disqualification from occupation may be dispensed with.

## 53. §

(1) Disqualification from occupation is for a definite period or has permanent effect.  
(2) The shortest duration of disqualification for a definite period is one year, the longest duration is ten years. A person who is unsuitable or unworthy to practice the occupation may be disqualified with permanent effect.  
(3) * The duration of disqualification from occupation begins when the decision on the merits becomes final. If disqualification from occupation is imposed in addition to imprisonment, the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or evades the execution of the imprisonment shall not be included in its duration. If conditional release is not revoked, the time spent on conditional release must be included in the duration of disqualification from occupation.  
(4) The court may, upon request, exempt the disqualified person from permanent disqualification if ten years have elapsed since the disqualification and the disqualified person has become suitable for practicing the occupation, or – if the disqualification was applied on grounds of unworthiness – has become worthy. In the latter case, a person who committed the criminal offence in a criminal organisation may not be exempted.

## 54. § For the purposes of this subsection, occupation also includes where the perpetrator is

a) a member of the body exercising general management of a business organisation or its sole manager,
b) a member of the supervisory board of a business association or cooperative,
c) a member of an individual firm,
d) an individual entrepreneur, or
e) a leading officer of a civil organisation as defined in the Act on civil organisations.

## 55. § (1) A person may be disqualified from driving a vehicle if he or she

a) commits the criminal offence by violating the rules of driving a vehicle subject to authorisation, or
b) uses a vehicle to commit criminal offences.  
(2) A person who commits the criminal offence of driving a vehicle while intoxicated or driving a vehicle while under the influence of drugs must be disqualified from driving a vehicle. In cases deserving special consideration, the mandatory application of disqualification from driving a vehicle may be dispensed with.  
(3) Disqualification from driving a vehicle may also relate to a specific type (air, rail, water or road) and category of vehicle.

## 56. §

(1) Disqualification from driving a vehicle is for a definite period or has permanent effect.  
(2) * The time for which the perpetrator's driving licence was – prior to his or her being sentenced to disqualification from driving a vehicle – confiscated at the scene in connection with the criminal offence, he or she surrendered it to the authority, or the immediate suspension of the driving entitlement was registered in the licence register at the scene, must be included in the duration of disqualification from driving a vehicle.  
(3) The shortest duration of disqualification for a definite period is one month, the longest duration is ten years. Disqualification from driving a vehicle must be determined in months or years, or in years and months.  
(4) * The duration of disqualification from driving a vehicle begins when the decision on the merits becomes final. If disqualification from driving a vehicle is imposed in addition to imprisonment, the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or evades the execution of the imprisonment shall not be included in its duration. If conditional release is not revoked, the time spent on conditional release must be included in the duration of disqualification from driving a vehicle.  
(5) A person who is unsuitable for driving a vehicle may be disqualified with permanent effect. The court may, upon request, exempt the disqualified person from permanent disqualification if ten years have elapsed since the disqualification and the disqualified person has become suitable for driving a vehicle.

## 57. §

(1) In cases specified in this Act, a person whose presence there endangers the public interest may be banned from one or more settlements, or from a settlement or a specified part of the country.  
(2) The shortest duration of the ban is one year, the longest duration is five years.  
(3) * The duration of the ban begins when the decision on the merits becomes final. If the ban is imposed in addition to imprisonment, the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or evades the execution of the imprisonment shall not be included in its duration. If conditional release is not revoked, the time spent on conditional release must be included in the duration of the ban.

## 58. §

(1) The court may disqualify the perpetrator from
a) attending any sporting event held in the competition system of any sports federation, or
b) entering any sports facility when it serves as the venue for a sporting event held in the competition system of any sports federation,
on account of a criminal offence committed in connection with a sporting event during attendance at, travel to or departure from the sporting event.  
(2) The shortest duration of the disqualification is one year, the longest duration is five years.  
(3) * The duration of the disqualification begins when the decision on the merits becomes final. If disqualification from attending sporting events is imposed in addition to imprisonment, the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or evades the execution of the imprisonment shall not be included in its duration. If conditional release is not revoked, the time spent on conditional release must be included in the duration of disqualification from attending sporting events.

## 59. §

(1) A perpetrator who is not a Hungarian citizen and whose stay in the country is undesirable must be expelled from the territory of Hungary. The expelled person is obliged to leave the territory of the country and may not return during the period of expulsion.  
(2) * A person to whom Hungary grants asylum may not be expelled.  
(3) * Expulsion of a person with the right of free movement and residence and with the right of long-term residence in the territory of Hungary may only take place on account of committing a criminal offence punishable by imprisonment of five years or more.  
(4) Against a person
a) who has been lawfully residing in the territory of Hungary for at least ten years, or
b) * who is lawfully residing in the territory of Hungary and whose right to respect for family life would be violated,
expulsion may only take place upon the imposition of imprisonment of ten years or more, provided that the perpetrator's stay in the country would significantly endanger public security.

## 60. § (1) Expulsion shall be for a definite period or permanent

(2) The shortest duration of expulsion for a definite period is one year, the longest duration is ten years.  
(2a) * In the case of illegal crossing of the border barrier (Section 352/A), damaging the border barrier (Section 352/B), obstructing construction work related to the border barrier (Section 352/C), and human smuggling (Section 353), expulsion imposed alongside imprisonment – with the exception of cases under Section 59 (2) and (4) – may not be dispensed with. In the case of expulsion for a definite period, its duration shall be twice the duration of the imposed imprisonment, but at least two years. The duration of expulsion may be determined in years, months and days. When this paragraph is applied, Section 50 (2) shall not apply.  
(3) A person sentenced to imprisonment of ten years or more may be expelled permanently if – having regard to the exceptional gravity of the offence, the nature of its commission, and the offender's connections – his or her stay in the country would significantly endanger public security. A person enjoying the right of free movement and residence may not be expelled permanently.  
(4) * The duration of expulsion shall begin on the day when the convicted person leaves the territory of Hungary – a convicted third-country national who does not have a right of residence in another European Union Member State leaves the territory of the European Union – as specified in the decision on the enforcement of expulsion adopted under immigration law, or if that date is not known, the deadline designated for this purpose in the decision. If the court imposes expulsion on an accused person staying abroad or absent, the duration of expulsion shall begin on the day the final decision becomes final.  
(5) The court may exempt the expelled person from permanent expulsion upon his or her request if ten years have elapsed since the expulsion and the expelled person is worthy of it.

## 61. §

(1) A person sentenced to custodial imprisonment for committing an intentional criminal offence and who is unworthy to participate in their exercise shall be disqualified from the exercise of public affairs.  
(2) A person disqualified from public affairs
a) shall not have the right to vote, may not participate in a referendum or popular initiative,
b) may not be an official,
c) may not be a member of the body or committee of a representative body, may not participate in their work,
d) may not be delegated to the assembly or body of an organisation established by an international treaty promulgated by law,
e) may not attain military rank,
f) may not receive a domestic decoration or permission to accept a foreign decoration,
g) may not be a defence counsel or legal representative in official proceedings,
h) may not hold office in a public body or public foundation, and
i) may not be a leading officer of a civil organisation as specified in the Act on civil organisations.  
(3) * A person disqualified from public affairs shall lose, upon the final decision becoming final, all membership, position, office, military rank, mandate and decoration the attainment of which is precluded by paragraph (2), as well as his or her honorary rank.

## 62. §

(1) Disqualification from public affairs shall be for a definite period, its shortest duration is one year, its longest duration is ten years.  
(2) * The duration of disqualification from public affairs shall begin when the final decision becomes final. The duration of disqualification from public affairs shall not include the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or during which he or she evades the execution of the imprisonment. If conditional release is not revoked, the time spent on conditional release shall be counted towards the duration of disqualification from public affairs.

## 63. § (1) Measures

a) reprimand,
b) probation,
c) reparation work,
d) probation supervision,
e) confiscation,
f) confiscation of assets,
g) making electronic data permanently inaccessible,
h) compulsory treatment,
i) measures under the Act on criminal law measures applicable to legal persons.  
(2) Reprimand, probation and reparation work may be applied independently, in place of punishment.  
(3) Probation supervision may be applied alongside punishment or a measure. Probation supervision may not be ordered alongside expulsion.  
(4) Confiscation, confiscation of assets and making electronic data permanently inaccessible may be applied independently and alongside punishment or a measure.

## 64. §

(1) A person whose act is no longer dangerous at the time of adjudication, or is dangerous to society to such a slight degree that the imposition of the minimum punishment applicable under this Act or the application of another measure – not including confiscation, confiscation of assets and making electronic data permanently inaccessible – is unnecessary, shall be given a reprimand.  
(2) * By a reprimand, the court or the prosecution expresses its disapproval of the unlawful act and calls upon the offender to refrain from committing criminal offences in the future.

## 65. §

(1) The court may defer the imposition of punishment for a probationary period for a misdemeanour and for a felony punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years if it can reasonably be assumed that the purpose of punishment can also be achieved by applying a measure.  
(2) The following may not be placed on probation:
a) a recidivist,
b) a person who committed the criminal offence in a criminal organisation,
c) a person who committed the intentional criminal offence after being sentenced to custodial imprisonment, before the completion of execution, or
d) a person who committed the intentional criminal offence during the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment.  
(3) The probationary period may range from one to three years; the duration shall be determined in years, or in years and months.  
(4) A person placed on probation may be placed under probation supervision. If the person placed on probation violates the rules of conduct of probation supervision, the probationary period may be extended once, by a maximum of one year.

## 66. §

(1) Probation shall be revoked and punishment shall be imposed if
a) the person placed on probation is convicted during the probationary period of a criminal offence committed before being placed on probation,
b) the person placed on probation is convicted of a criminal offence committed during the probationary period, or
c) the person placed on probation seriously violates the rules of conduct of probation supervision.  
(2) Apart from the cases specified in paragraph (1), upon expiry of the probationary period the offender's criminal liability shall cease.

## 67. §

(1) The court may defer the imposition of punishment for one year for a misdemeanour and for a felony punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years, and prescribe the performance of reparation work, if it can reasonably be assumed that the purpose of punishment can be achieved in this way. Probation supervision may also be ordered alongside the prescription of reparation work.  
(2) Reparation work may not be prescribed for a person who
a) is a recidivist,
b) committed the criminal offence in a criminal organisation,
c) committed the intentional criminal offence after being sentenced to custodial imprisonment, before the completion of execution, or
d) committed the intentional criminal offence during the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment.  
(3) * The offender may – at his or her choice – perform the reparation work at an institution maintained by the state or a local government, at a civil organisation with public benefit status, at an ecclesiastical legal person or for them.  
(4) The duration of reparation work shall be determined in hours; its minimum extent is twenty-four hours, its maximum extent is one hundred and fifty hours.

## 68. §

(1) If the offender duly proves the performance of reparation work within one year, his or her criminal liability shall cease.  
(2) * If the offender does not prove the performance of reparation work or seriously violates the rules of probation supervision, the court shall impose punishment. If the offender is unable to prove the performance of reparation work for health reasons, the deadline for proving the performance of reparation work may be extended once by a maximum of one year.

## 69. § (1) * Probation supervision may be ordered

a) for the duration of conditional prosecutorial suspension,
b) for the duration of conditional release,
c) for the probation period of probation,
d) in conjunction with the prescription of reparation work,
e) for the probation period of suspension of imprisonment,
if regular monitoring of the offender is necessary for its successful completion.  
(2) * The following are subject to probation supervision:
a) a person released on conditional release from life imprisonment,
b) a recidivist who has been released on conditional release or against whom the execution of imprisonment has been suspended,
c) an offender under Section 38(5) who has been released on conditional release by application of Section 38(6),
d) for the probation period of suspension of imprisonment, a person sentenced to imprisonment for a violent offence against a person committed against his or her relative, or
e) for the probation period of suspension of imprisonment, a person sentenced to imprisonment for an offence against sexual freedom and sexual morality committed against a person under eighteen years of age.  
(3) * For the purposes of point d) of paragraph (2), relative shall also include the parent of the offender's child, former spouse, former cohabitant, guardian, ward, legal guardian or ward.

## 70. § (1) * The duration of probation supervision is the same as

a) the duration of conditional release,
b) the probation period of probation,
c) the probation period of suspension of imprisonment,
d) the duration of conditional prosecutorial suspension,
but not more than five years, or in the case of conditional release granted from life imprisonment, not more than fifteen years.  
(2) Probation supervision ordered in conjunction with the prescription of reparation work shall last until the person obliged to perform reparation work proves the completion of the reparation work, but not more than one year.  
(3) In the case of Section 69(1), after half of the probation supervision has elapsed, but at least one year, the probation officer may propose the termination of probation supervision if its necessity no longer exists.

## 71. § (1) The probationer is obliged as a general rule of conduct to

a) comply with the rules of conduct prescribed by law and in the decision,
b) maintain regular contact with the probation officer, and
c) provide the probation officer with the information necessary for supervision.  
(2) * The court, or in the case of conditional prosecutorial suspension the prosecution service, may prescribe obligations and prohibitions as special rules of conduct in its decision to promote the purpose of probation supervision. The court or the prosecution service may order that the probationer
a) not maintain contact with a specified person who participated in the commission of the offence,
b) * stay away from the victim of the offence and, with regard to the victim, from his or her relative or other person
(hereinafter together referred to as: affected person), from the affected person's dwelling or other property serving his or her habitual residence – including child welfare and child protection institutions –, workplace, institutions or other places regularly visited by the affected person, in particular educational, educational-training or health institutions visited for the purpose of medical treatment, buildings visited in the course of religious practice,
c) * not visit public places and public events of a specified nature, as well as assemblies, specified public areas,
d) not consume alcoholic beverages in public places,
e) report at a specified place and at specified intervals to a specified authority or person,
f) contact the state employment service or register for public employment at the local government,
g) pursue specified studies,
h) – with his or her consent – undergo specified medical treatment or therapeutic procedure,
i) participate in group sessions organised by the probation officer or other sessions according to the programme of the community employment service of the probation service.  
(3) * The court or the prosecution service may prescribe rules of conduct other than those listed in paragraph (2), with particular regard to the nature of the offence, the damage caused and the enhancement of the offender's chances of social integration.  
(4) * Against an offender under Section 69(2) points c) and d), the special rule of conduct under point b) of paragraph (2) shall be ordered in the interest of protecting the victim or, with regard to the victim, his or her relative. In the case under Section 69(2) point c), the court shall order the use of a technical device tracking the movement of the obliged person to monitor compliance with this rule – except where the technical conditions for its application are not available.  
(5) * The court may deviate from the application of paragraph (4) against an offender under Section 69(2) point d), having regard to the circumstances of the commission, the degree of danger to society inherent in the offender's person, the relationship between the offender and the victim of the offence or other person endangered in connection with the offence, or other sentencing circumstances.  
(6) * The ordering of restraint under point b) of paragraph (2) does not preclude the offender – if entitled to contact – from maintaining contact with his or her minor child within the framework of supervised contact.

## 72. § (1) The following shall be confiscated:

a) a thing used or intended as an instrument for the commission of the offence,
b) a thing created by the commission of the offence,
c) a thing against which the offence was committed, or which was used for the purpose of transporting this thing after the completion of the offence,
d) a thing the possession of which endangers public safety or is contrary to law.  
(2) A press product in which the offence is committed shall be confiscated.  
(3) In the cases of points a) and c) of paragraph (1) – provided that the owner did not have prior knowledge of the commission – confiscation may not be ordered if the thing is not the property of the offender, except where the omission of confiscation is precluded by an international legal obligation.  
(4) * Confiscation shall also be ordered if
a) the offender is not punishable due to childhood, morbid mental state or a ground for terminating punishability specified by law,
b) * the offender has been reprimanded,
c) it cannot be executed during the period of special protection specified in the Act on the special protection of loaned cultural property.  
(5) Confiscation may not be ordered of a thing to which forfeiture of assets extends.  
(6) The ownership of the confiscated thing passes to the state unless otherwise provided by law.  
(7) Confiscation shall not take place after the expiry of the period established for the limitation of punishability of the act, but at least five years.

## 73. §

Confiscation may be exceptionally waived in the cases specified in Section 72(1) points a) and c) if it would cause a disproportionate disadvantage to the perpetrator or the owner in relation to the gravity of the offence, except where
a) an international legal obligation precludes it,
b) the perpetrator committed the offence in a criminal organisation,
c) * in cases of drug trafficking, drug possession, facilitating the preparation of drugs, misuse of substances necessary for drug production, misuse of new psychoactive substances, misuse of performance-enhancing substances, falsification of medicines, falsification of medical devices, misuse of poison, misuse of harmful consumer goods, environmental damage, damage to nature, animal cruelty, illegal fishing, illegal hunting, organisation of prohibited animal fights, violation of waste management regulations, misuse of ozone-depleting substances, misuse of radioactive materials, misuse in the operation of nuclear facilities, misuse of explosives or explosive devices, misuse of firearms or ammunition, misuse of weapons prohibited by international treaty, misuse of military technology products or services, misuse of dual-use products or violation of obligations related to the keeping of dangerous dogs.

## 74. § (1) Confiscation of assets shall be ordered on

a) assets derived from the commission of the offence, which the perpetrator obtained during or in connection with the commission of the offence,
b)–c) *
d) assets that replaced assets derived from the commission of the offence, obtained during or in connection with the commission of the offence,
e) assets that were provided or intended for the purpose of committing the offence to ensure the necessary conditions or to facilitate it,
f) assets that were the subject of the pecuniary advantage given or promised.  
(2) Confiscation of assets shall also be ordered on assets derived from the commission of the offence, obtained during or in connection with the commission of the offence, with which another person was enriched. If a business organisation was enriched with such assets, the confiscation of assets shall be ordered against the business organisation.  
(3) * If the perpetrator or the person enriched pursuant to paragraph (2) has died, or the business organisation has been transformed, the confiscation of assets shall be ordered against the legal successor on the assets pursuant to paragraph (1) or Section 74/A. to which the succession occurred.  
(4) *
(5) Confiscation of assets may not be ordered
a) on assets that serve as security for a civil law claim asserted during the criminal proceedings,
b) on assets that were acquired in good faith for consideration,
c) * on assets pursuant to paragraph (1) points a) and d) or paragraph (2), if in relation to them, due to the same facts as the offence that is the subject of the criminal proceedings, an administrative authority or state tax and customs authority has established a payment obligation in a final decision or a court has established it in a final judgment in administrative proceedings, up to the amount of the payment obligation.  
74/A. § * (1) Until proven otherwise, assets shall be considered subject to confiscation, and confiscation of assets shall be ordered on assets that the perpetrator acquired during the time of
a) his or her participation in a criminal organisation,
b) the commission of placing drugs on the market or trafficking in them,
c) the commission of human smuggling committed in a business-like manner or in a criminal association.  
(2) Until proven otherwise, assets shall also be considered subject to confiscation, and confiscation of assets shall be ordered on assets that were acquired through
a) placing drugs on the market or trafficking in them [Section 176(1)–(4) and (6), Section 177(1) point b), point c) subpoint cb) and (2)–(3) and (5)], cultivation, production, importation into, exportation from, transit through the territory of the country of drugs [Section 178(1)–(5) and Section 179], facilitating the preparation of drugs [Section 182(1)–(4)], placing drug precursors on the market, transporting them, conducting intermediary activities with them [Section 183(1) point a)],
b) production, importation into, exportation from, transit through the territory of the country, placing on the market or trafficking in new psychoactive substances [Section 184(1)–(3) and (5), Section 184/A(1) point b) and point c) subpoint cb), (2)–(3) and (5), Section 184/B(1) point a), (2)–(5), Section 184/C(1) point a) subpoint aa), point b) subpoint ba), (2)–(5)],
c) * trafficking in human beings and forced labour [Section 192(1)–(7)],
d) * procuring (Section 200), facilitating prostitution (Section 201), living off the earnings of prostitution (Section 202), exploitation of child prostitution [Section 203(1) and (3)–(4)], offering, transferring, making available, producing, placing on the market, trafficking in or making available to the general public pornographic recordings depicting persons under eighteen years of age [Section 204(1) points b)–c), (2)–(4), (5) point b) and (6)], having a person or persons under eighteen years of age perform in a pornographic show or organising such a pornographic show [Section 204/A(1) point b) and (2)–(5)],
e) child labour (Section 209), illegal employment of third-country nationals [Section 356(1)–(2)],
f) misuse of personal data committed for profit [Section 219(1) and (3)–(4)],
g) bribery, acceptance of bribery, official bribery, acceptance of official bribery (Sections 290–294),
h) * terrorist act [Section 314, Section 315(1)–(2), Section 316 and Section 316/A], financing of terrorism [Section 318 and Section 318/A],
i) participation in a criminal organisation [Section 321(1)],
j) * human smuggling (Section 353), facilitating illegal residence (Section 354),
k) extortion (Section 367),
l) embezzlement committed for particularly large or particularly significant value, in a criminal organisation [Section 372(5) point a) and (6)], fraud causing particularly large or particularly significant damage, committed in a criminal organisation [Section 373(5) point a) and (6)], economic fraud causing particularly large or particularly significant pecuniary disadvantage, committed in a criminal organisation [Section 374(5) point a) and (6)], breach of fiduciary duty causing particularly large or particularly significant pecuniary disadvantage, committed in a criminal organisation [Section 376(5) point a) and (6)],
m) fraud committed using an information system [Section 375(1)–(5)],
n) counterfeiting of money [Section 389(1)–(3)], facilitating counterfeiting of money (Section 390), counterfeiting of cash substitute payment instruments [Section 392(1)–(2)], misuse of cash substitute payment instruments [Section 393(1)–(2)], facilitating counterfeiting of cash substitute payment instruments (Section 394),
o) budget fraud committed in a criminal organisation causing particularly large or particularly significant pecuniary disadvantage [Section 396(4)–(5)],
p) money laundering (Section 399),
q) unlawful acquisition of data committed in a criminal association or in a business-like manner [Section 422(4) points b) and c)], circumvention of technical measures ensuring the protection of an information system [Section 424(1)] by the perpetrator in the five years preceding the initiation of criminal proceedings, if the assets or the perpetrator's lifestyle is particularly disproportionate to his or her verifiable income circumstances and personal circumstances.  
(3) Confiscation of assets may not be ordered in the cases of paragraphs (1) and (2) if the perpetrator proves that the assets do not originate from a criminal offence.

## 75. § (1) Confiscation of assets shall be ordered in a monetary amount, *

a) * if the assets subject to confiscation can no longer be found,
b) * if the assets subject to confiscation cannot be separated from other assets, or their separation would cause disproportionate difficulty,
c) in the case specified in Section 74 (5) point b).  
(2) * Confiscation of assets shall also be ordered if
a) the offender is not punishable due to childhood, morbid mental state or a ground for termination of punishability defined by law,
b) * the offender was given a reprimand,
c) it cannot be executed during the period of special protection defined in the Act on the special protection of loaned cultural property.  
(3) In the absence of a contrary provision of law, confiscated assets shall pass to the state.

## 76. §

For the purposes of this subsection, assets shall also be understood to include the benefit thereof, rights of pecuniary value, claims, and any advantage having a value expressible in money.

## 77. §

(1) Data published on an electronic communications network shall be made permanently inaccessible if
a) making it accessible or publishing it constitutes a criminal offence,
b) it was used as an instrument for the commission of a criminal offence, or
c) it was created through the commission of a criminal offence.  
(2) The permanent rendering inaccessible of electronic data shall also be ordered if the offender is not punishable due to childhood, morbid mental state, or a ground for termination of punishability defined by law, or if the offender was given a reprimand.

## 78. §

(1) Compulsory medical treatment shall be ordered for the perpetrator of a punishable act involving violence against a person or causing public danger if he or she is not punishable due to a morbid state of mental functioning, and there is reason to fear that he or she will commit a similar act, provided that if he or she were punishable, a punishment more severe than one year's imprisonment would have to be imposed.  
(2) Compulsory medical treatment shall be terminated if its necessity no longer exists.

## 79. §

The purpose of punishment is to prevent both the offender and others from committing criminal offences in the interest of protecting society.

## 80. §

(1) Punishment shall be imposed within the limits defined in this Act, with its purpose in mind, so that it corresponds to the objective gravity of the criminal offence, the degree of culpability, the dangerousness of the offender to society, and other mitigating and aggravating circumstances.  
(2) When imposing imprisonment for a definite period, the middle measure of the sentencing range shall be decisive. The middle measure is half of the sum of the lower and upper limits of the sentencing range.  
(3) If this Act prescribes the increase of the sentencing ranges defined in the Special Part when imposing punishment, the calculation specified in paragraph (2) shall be performed with regard to the increased sentencing ranges.  
(4) If the court imposes imprisonment, it shall determine the measure of punishment disregarding the possibility of suspension of execution or conditional release.

## 81. § (1) In case of concurrence of offences, one punishment shall be imposed

(2) The cumulative punishment shall be imposed on the basis of the most severe of the types of punishment or sentencing ranges established for the criminal offences in concurrence.  
(3) If at least two of the criminal offences in concurrence are punishable by imprisonment for a definite period, the upper limit of the sentencing range shall be increased by half of the highest sentencing range, but may not reach the combined duration of the upper limits of the sentencing ranges established for the individual criminal offences.  
(4) * If the conditions therefor exist in respect of the given criminal offence, the sentencing range for the criminal offence in concurrence shall be established on the basis of the provisions on special, multiple and violent multiple recidivists, as well as on commission in a criminal organisation, prior to the application of paragraphs (2) and (3).  
(5) An accessory punishment in case of cumulative punishment may not exceed the highest measure or duration defined by law.

## 82. §

(1) A punishment more lenient than the sentencing range may be imposed if its minimum measure would be too severe having regard to the principles of imposing punishment.  
(2) On the basis of paragraph (1), if the lower limit of the sentencing range is
a) ten years' imprisonment, at least five years',
b) five years' imprisonment, at least two years',
c) two years' imprisonment, at least one year's,
d) one year's imprisonment, imprisonment of shorter duration
may be imposed instead.  
(3) In the case of paragraph (2) point d), instead of imprisonment, detention, community service work or fine, or these punishments may also be imposed alongside each other.  
(4) In the case of attempt or aiding and abetting, if the punishment imposable under paragraph (2) would also be too severe, the punishment may be imposed on the basis of the next point of paragraph (2).  
(5) If this Act permits unlimited mitigation, the minimum measure of any type of punishment may also be imposed.

## 83. §

(1) * In proceedings under Chapter XCIX of Act XC of 2017 on Criminal Procedure, when imposing punishment upon approval of an agreement (hereinafter: agreement), the lower limit of the more lenient sentencing ranges defined in Section 82 (2) shall be taken as a basis.  
(2) * If the agreement is approved in the case of a defendant who, contributing to the proof of the case or another criminal case, cooperated with the prosecution or the investigating authority to a significant extent, the punishment may also be imposed on the basis of the next point of Section 82 (2).  
(3) *

## 84. § *

## 85. §

(1) The execution of imprisonment not exceeding two years may be suspended for a probationary period if – having regard in particular to the personal circumstances of the offender – it can reasonably be assumed that the purpose of the punishment can be achieved without its execution.  
(1a) * In the case of illegal crossing of the border barrier (Section 352/A of the Criminal Code), damaging the border barrier (Section 352/B of the Criminal Code), and obstruction of construction work related to the border barrier (Section 352/C of the Criminal Code), the execution of imprisonment not exceeding five years may be suspended for a probationary period.  
(2) The duration of the probationary period – unless this Act provides otherwise – may range from one year to five years, but may not be shorter than the imposed imprisonment. The probationary period shall be determined in years, or in years and months.  
(2a) * In the case of illegal crossing of the border barrier (Section 352/A of the Criminal Code), damaging the border barrier (Section 352/B of the Criminal Code), and obstruction of construction work related to the border barrier (Section 352/C of the Criminal Code), the duration of the probationary period may range from two years to ten years, but may not be shorter than the duration of expulsion imposed for these criminal offences.

## 86. §

(1) The execution of imprisonment may not be suspended against a person who
a) is a multiple recidivist,
b) committed the criminal offence in a criminal organisation, or
c) committed the intentional criminal offence before the completion of the execution of imprisonment or during the probationary period of its suspension.  
(2) If imprisonment is being executed on the offender for which the execution of the suspended imprisonment cannot be ordered, then the probationary period shall be extended by the duration of the imprisonment.  
(3) The provision of paragraph (2) shall also apply in the case of imprisonment replacing community service work and fine.  
(4) In the cases of paragraphs (2) and (3), the duration of the probationary period may exceed five years.  
(5) If the offender is sentenced to suspended imprisonment on multiple occasions and the probationary periods of the imprisonments have not yet elapsed, all probationary periods shall run concurrently.  
(6) Simultaneously with the suspension of the execution of imprisonment, the offender may be placed under probation supervision. If the offender is a recidivist, he or she shall be under probation supervision.

## 87. § The suspended imprisonment sentence must be executed if

a) during the probationary period it is established that the execution of the imprisonment sentence was suspended despite the exclusionary ground set out in Section 86(1),
b) the offender is sentenced to imprisonment to be executed for a criminal offence committed during the probationary period, or
c) the offender seriously violates the conduct rules of probation supervision,
d) * the offender expelled pursuant to Section 60(2a) unlawfully returns to the territory of Hungary during the probationary period of the suspended imprisonment sentence.

## 88. §

In the case of suspension of the execution of a sentence by way of pardon, the provisions on the execution of a suspended sentence shall apply mutatis mutandis to the ordering of execution.

## 89. §

(1) * In the case of a special recidivist and a multiple recidivist, the upper limit of the sentencing range for the new criminal offence shall be increased by half in the case of imprisonment, but may not exceed twenty-five years.
(2) In the case of a special recidivist and a multiple recidivist, the sentence may be mitigated pursuant to Section 82(1) only in cases deserving special consideration.
