(3) The more severe legal consequences set out in paragraph (1) may not be applied if the Special Part of this Act provides for commission as a special recidivist as a more severely qualified case of the criminal offence.

## 90. §

(1) Section 33(4) shall not be applicable to a violent multiple recidivist.
(2) * In the case of a violent multiple recidivist, the upper limit of the sentencing range for the criminal offence establishing the qualification as a violent multiple recidivist shall be doubled in the case of imprisonment. If the upper limit of the sentencing range thus increased would exceed twenty years, or if the criminal offence is punishable by life imprisonment under the law, life imprisonment shall be imposed on the offender.
(3) In the case of a violent multiple recidivist, the sentence
a) may not be mitigated pursuant to Section 82(1),
b) may be mitigated without limitation – if the General Part of this Act so permits.

## 91. §

(1) * In the case of a person who committed an intentional criminal offence in a criminal organisation, the upper limit of the sentencing range for the criminal offence shall be doubled, but may not exceed twenty-five years.
(2) In the case of a person who committed a criminal offence in a criminal organisation, expulsion may also be imposed.
(3) Where the commission of a criminal offence in a criminal organisation is established, the legal consequences provided for in this Act for the commission of a criminal offence in complicity may not be applied.

## 92. §

(1) * The entire period of pre-trial detention and of criminal supervision during which the court ordered the accused not to leave a dwelling, other premises, institution, or the fenced area belonging thereto without permission shall be credited towards the imprisonment, detention, community service work and fine imposed.
(2) For the purposes of crediting, one day of pre-trial detention shall correspond to one day of imprisonment, one day of detention, one day-unit of fine, or four hours of community service work, respectively.
(3) * For the purposes of crediting
a) one day of imprisonment shall correspond to five days in the case of fortress grade, four days in the case of prison grade, three days in the case of jail grade,
b) one day of detention shall correspond to two days,
c) four hours of community service work shall correspond to one day,
d) one day-unit of fine shall correspond to one day
of time spent in criminal supervision as defined in paragraph (1).
(4) * The period remaining after crediting pursuant to paragraphs (2) and (3) shall be credited as one day of imprisonment.
92/A. § * (1) * The entire duration of detention and community service work imposed and executed in misdemeanour proceedings, as well as on-the-spot fines and pecuniary fines, shall be credited – following a prosecutorial notice, a review or reopening procedure conducted pursuant to the Act on misdemeanours, in respect of the act affected by the notice, review or reopening – towards the imprisonment, detention, community service work, fine and reformatory education imposed in criminal proceedings.
(2) For the purposes of crediting, one day of misdemeanour detention shall correspond to one day of imprisonment or detention, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine, and one day of reformatory education, respectively.
(3) * An on-the-spot fine and a misdemeanour pecuniary fine shall correspond, per five thousand forints, to one day of imprisonment or detention, four hours of community service work, the same value of a fine, or one day of reformatory education, respectively.
(4) Community service work imposed in misdemeanour proceedings shall correspond to the same amount of community service work, and furthermore, community service work imposed in misdemeanour proceedings shall correspond, per six hours, to one day of imprisonment or detention, one day-unit of fine, and one day of reformatory education, respectively.
92/B. § * (1) * If the judgment rendered as a result of reopening, review, remedy lodged in the interest of legality, or repeated proceedings modifies the decision rendered in the underlying case, the sentence or measure imposed and executed in the underlying case shall be credited. If the type of sentence or measure differs, the provisions of paragraph (2) shall apply to crediting.
(2) For the purposes of crediting
a) one day of imprisonment shall correspond to one day of detention, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine, six hours of reparation work or one day of reformatory education,
b) one day of detention shall correspond to one day of imprisonment, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine, six hours of reparation work or one day of reformatory education,
c) four hours of community service work shall correspond to one day of imprisonment, one day of detention, one day-unit of fine, six hours of reparation work or one day of reformatory education,
d) one day-unit of fine shall correspond to one day of imprisonment, one day of detention, four hours of community service work, six hours of reparation work or one day of reformatory education,
e) one day of reformatory education shall correspond to one day of imprisonment, one day of detention, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine or six hours of reparation work,
f) six hours of reparation work shall correspond to one day of imprisonment, one day of detention, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine or one day of reformatory education,
respectively.

## 93. §

* (1) * If the offender is sentenced to multiple fixed-term imprisonment sentences, the sentences imposed with final effect shall be consolidated into a combined sentence – as provided by law – if the offender committed all the criminal offences before the announcement of the earliest first-instance decision on the merits.
(2) Only imprisonment sentences to be executed that have not yet been executed at the time of consolidation into a combined sentence, or that are being executed continuously, may be consolidated into a combined sentence.
(3) If a suspended imprisonment sentence is to be executed subsequently, it shall be considered an imprisonment sentence to be executed for the purposes of consolidation into a combined sentence.
(4) The following may not be consolidated into a combined sentence:
a) a sentence previously consolidated into a combined sentence,
b) imprisonment replacing a fine or community service work.

## 94. §

* The term of the combined sentence shall be determined as if a cumulative sentence were being imposed. However, the term of the combined sentence must reach the term calculated as the sum of the most severe sentence and one-third of the shorter sentence or sentences, but may not exceed the aggregate term of the sentences.

## 95. §

* (1) In the case of combining imprisonment sentences to be executed at different levels into a cumulative sentence, the strictest level shall be determined as the execution level. If the measure of the cumulative sentence is three years or more, or in the case of a multiple recidivist two years or more, the execution level of the cumulative sentence shall be determined taking this into account.  
(2) If the execution level to be determined by applying paragraph (1) would result in an unfair disadvantage for the convicted person, a level one degree more lenient may be determined.  
(3) * If the possibility of conditional release is not excluded, its earliest date shall be determined on the basis of the strictest provision. The convicted person may not be conditionally released from the cumulative sentence if the possibility of conditional release is excluded in respect of any of the imprisonment sentences.  
(4) * If the imprisonment sentence in connection with which the convicted person was excluded from the possibility of conditional release due to his own fault is subsequently combined into a cumulative sentence, then the convicted person may not be conditionally released from the cumulative sentence either.

## 96. §

* (1) If disqualification from an occupation, disqualification from driving, expulsion, disqualification from attending sporting events or expulsion from the country was imposed in addition to imprisonment, and the imprisonment sentences were combined into a cumulative sentence, then among several disqualifications from an occupation, disqualifications from driving, expulsions, disqualifications from attending sporting events or expulsions from the country with identical content, the one that is more disadvantageous to the convicted person shall be executed.  
(2) The accessory punishment of disqualification from public affairs may not be combined into a cumulative sentence. Among several accessory punishments of disqualification from public affairs, the one with the longest duration shall be executed.

## 97. §

(1) Adverse legal consequences attached to the establishment of criminal liability, the imposition of punishment or the application of a measure may be established by law.  
(2) * Data relating to the establishment of criminal liability, the imposed punishment and the applied measure shall be contained in an authentic official register until the date specified by law, after which no adverse legal consequence may be established against the convicted person on account of the conviction. Recidivism and the adverse legal consequences attached to it as defined in this Act shall be established pursuant to Section 389 of Act XC of 2017 on Criminal Procedure, on the basis of data obtained from the criminal record at the time closest to the commission of the new criminal offence, even if these data are subsequently deleted.

## 98. §

(1) As a result of rehabilitation – in the absence of a contrary provision of law – the convicted person is relieved of the adverse legal consequences attached to a criminal record.  
(2) A rehabilitated person shall be considered to have a clean criminal record and – in the absence of a contrary provision of law – is not required to give an account of any conviction in respect of which he has been rehabilitated.  
(3) In the event of the commission of a new criminal offence, rehabilitation does not extend to those criminal law consequences which this Act attaches to the previous conviction.

## 99. § A convicted person may be granted rehabilitation

a) by operation of this Act,
b) on the basis of a court decision, or
c) by way of pardon.

## 100. § (1) Rehabilitation occurs by operation of this Act

a) * in the case of detention, fine and community service work, on the day the decision on the merits becomes final,
b) * in the case of disqualification from an occupation, disqualification from driving, expulsion and disqualification from attending sporting events, on the day the decision on the merits becomes final,
c) in the case of expulsion from the country, on the day of completion of the execution of the punishment or the termination of its enforceability,
d) in the case of suspended imprisonment, on the day of expiry of the probationary period,
e) in the case of imprisonment imposed for a negligent misdemeanour, on the day of completion of the punishment or the termination of its enforceability,
f) in the case of imprisonment not exceeding one year imposed for an intentional criminal offence, upon the expiry of three years following the completion of the punishment or the termination of its enforceability,
g) in the case of imprisonment exceeding one year but not longer than five years imposed for an intentional criminal offence, upon the expiry of five years following the completion of the punishment or the termination of its enforceability,
h) in the case of imprisonment exceeding five years but not longer than ten years imposed for an intentional criminal offence, upon the expiry of eight years following the completion of the punishment or the termination of its enforceability,
i) in the case of imprisonment exceeding ten years for a definite period imposed for an intentional criminal offence, upon the expiry of ten years following the completion of the punishment or the termination of its enforceability.  
(2) In the case of paragraph (1) point b), rehabilitation does not extend to
a) in the case of disqualification from an occupation, the right to practise the occupation,
b) in the case of disqualification from driving, the right to practise driving,
c) in the case of expulsion, the right to stay in the settlement specified in the judgment or in a specified part of the country,
d) in the case of disqualification from attending sporting events, the convicted person's right to attend sporting events.  
(3) * In the case of paragraph (1) point d), rehabilitation does not occur if the execution of the imprisonment is ordered. In such a case, the rehabilitation rules applicable to enforceable imprisonment apply to rehabilitation.

## 101. §

* (1) In the case of enforceable imprisonment, the court may grant the convicted person subsequent rehabilitation upon request if he is worthy of it and half of the time specified in Section 100 paragraph (1) points f)–i) has already elapsed since the completion of the imprisonment or the termination of its enforceability.  
(2) In the case of suspended imprisonment, the court may grant the convicted person subsequent rehabilitation upon request if he is worthy of it and half of the probationary period, but at least one year, has already elapsed.  
(3) * In assessing worthiness, the lifestyle of the convicted person since the completion of the punishment, or in the case of suspended imprisonment since the decision on the merits became final, shall be taken into account, as well as whether – if he had the opportunity to do so – he made reparation for the harm caused by the criminal offence.  
(4) In the case of suspended imprisonment, subsequent rehabilitation loses its effect if the execution of the suspended imprisonment is ordered.

## 102. §

(1) * In the case of suspension of the execution of imprisonment, the court may grant the convicted person preliminary rehabilitation in the decision on the merits if he is worthy of it.  
(2) Preliminary rehabilitation loses its effect if the execution of the suspended imprisonment is ordered.

## 103. §

In the case of the application of an accessory punishment, the convicted person is not relieved of, and may not be relieved of, the adverse legal consequences attached to the conviction until the execution of the accessory punishment has been completed or its enforceability has terminated.

## 104. §

(1) The person exercising the right of pardon may grant the convicted person rehabilitation by way of pardon even if there is otherwise no ground for it under this Act.  
(2) A person granted pardon rehabilitation shall be considered to have a clean criminal record for the purposes of adverse legal consequences outside criminal law.

## 105. §

(1) A juvenile is a person who has reached the age of twelve but not eighteen at the time of committing the criminal offence.  
(2) The provisions of this Act shall apply to juveniles with the exceptions set out in this Chapter.

## 106. §

(1) The purpose of a penalty imposed on or measure applied to a juvenile is primarily to ensure that the juvenile develops in the right direction and becomes a useful member of society; accordingly, when choosing the measure or penalty, the education and protection of the juvenile must be kept in mind.  
(2) A penalty shall be imposed on a juvenile if the application of a measure is not expedient. Against a person who had not reached the age of fourteen at the time of committing the offence, only a measure may be applied.  
(3) A measure involving deprivation of liberty may be applied or a penalty involving deprivation of liberty may be imposed on a juvenile only if the purpose of the measure or penalty cannot be achieved by other means.

## 107. §

If the offender is a juvenile, active repentance may also take place in the case of a misdemeanour defined in Section 29(1) or a felony punishable by imprisonment not exceeding five years.

## 108. §

(1) Reformatory education may also be applied as a measure against a juvenile.  
(2) Imprisonment, detention or community service work may not be imposed in addition to reformatory education.

## 109. §

(1) The shortest term of imprisonment that may be imposed on a juvenile is one month for any offence.  
(2) The longest term of imprisonment that may be imposed on a juvenile who had not reached the age of sixteen at the time of committing the offence is
a) ten years in the case of committing an offence punishable by life imprisonment,
b) five years in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding five years.  
(3) The longest term of imprisonment that may be imposed on a juvenile who had reached the age of sixteen at the time of committing the offence is
a) fifteen years in the case of committing an offence punishable by life imprisonment,
b) ten years in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding ten years,
c) five years in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding five years.  
(4) For the purposes of calculating the limitation period for criminal liability and the provisions on recidivists, the time periods specified in paragraphs (2)-(3) shall be decisive.  
(5) * Against a juvenile, the possibility of conditional release may be excluded pursuant to Section 38(4)(e) and (5) if the juvenile had reached the age of sixteen at the time of committing the offence and is sentenced to imprisonment of ten years or more.

## 110. § (1) The enforcement level of imprisonment is juvenile prison if

a) the juvenile is sentenced to imprisonment of two years or longer for a felony,
b) the juvenile sentenced to imprisonment of one year or longer is a recidivist, or
c) the juvenile sentenced to imprisonment of one year or longer was sentenced to reformatory education for an intentional offence within three years preceding the commission of the intentional offence.  
(2) In cases other than those specified in paragraph (1), the enforcement level of imprisonment is juvenile jail.

## 111. §

The shortest term of detention that may be imposed on a juvenile is three days, the longest term is thirty days.

## 112. §

* Community service work may be imposed on a juvenile if the juvenile had reached the age of sixteen at the time the decision on the merits is made.

## 113. §

* (1) A fine may be imposed on a juvenile if the juvenile has independent earnings, income or adequate assets.  
(2) * In the case of a juvenile, the minimum amount of the fine is fifteen day-fines, the maximum amount is two hundred and fifty day-fines; the amount of one day-fine shall be determined at a minimum of six hundred and fifty forints and a maximum of sixty-five thousand forints.  
(3) In the case of a juvenile, if the fine is unenforceable, it shall be converted into
a) community service work if Section 112 permits, or
b) imprisonment.  
(4) The community service work replacing the fine shall be determined so that two hours of community service work replace one day-fine. Otherwise, the provisions of Section 47 shall apply to the community service work replacing the fine, with the proviso that its duration may differ from those specified in Section 47(1).  
(5) If the juvenile does not voluntarily fulfil the work obligation, the community service work or the remaining part thereof shall be converted into imprisonment. Otherwise, the provisions of Section 48 shall apply.  
113/A. § * Against a juvenile, the application of Section 52(3) may be dispensed with in cases deserving special consideration.

## 114. § Expulsion may take place against a juvenile if

a) imprisonment of ten years or more has been imposed on the juvenile,
b) the juvenile's stay in the country would significantly endanger public security, and
c) the juvenile's right to respect for family life is not violated.

## 115. §

A juvenile may be disqualified from public affairs only if imprisonment exceeding one year is imposed.

## 116. §

(1) Probation may take place against a juvenile for any offence.  
(2) The probationary period may range from one year to two years.  
(3) In the cases specified in Section 66(1), the court shall order reformatory education or impose a penalty.

## 117. §

* Reparation work may be prescribed against a juvenile if the juvenile had reached the age of sixteen at the time the decision on the merits is made.

## 118. §

A juvenile with an appropriate family environment may not be banned from the settlement where the juvenile's family lives.

## 119. § (1) * The juvenile

a) during the period of conditional release,
b) during the probationary period of probation,
c) during the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment,
d) during the period of temporary release from the reformatory,
e) during the period of conditional prosecutorial suspension
is under probation supervision.  
(2) Probation supervision shall also be ordered against a juvenile in addition to the prescription of reparation work.  
(3) * Section 71(4) shall not apply to a juvenile.

## 120. §

(1) * The court shall order reformatory education if institutional placement is necessary for the effective education of the juvenile. Reformatory education may not be ordered against a person who had reached the age of twenty at the time the decision on the merits is made.  
(2) The duration of reformatory education may range from one year to four years.

## 121. §

(1) In the case of ordering reformatory education, the court shall establish that the juvenile may be temporarily released from the institution after serving half of the reformatory education if
a) the juvenile has spent at least one year in the institution, and
b) it can reasonably be assumed that the purpose of the measure can be achieved without further reformatory education.  
(2) The duration of temporary release is equal to the remaining part of the reformatory education, but at least one year.  
(3) The court shall terminate the temporary release if the juvenile is sentenced to imprisonment during the temporary release – except for the case specified in Section 122 – or reformatory education is ordered against the juvenile. If the court imposes another penalty on or applies another measure to the juvenile, the court may terminate the temporary release.  
(4) In the case of termination of temporary release, the time spent on temporary release may not be credited towards the reformatory education.

## 122. §

If the juvenile is sentenced to enforceable imprisonment for an offence committed after the sentencing to reformatory education, during the reformatory education or temporary release, the imprisonment penalty shall be enforced. In this case, the remaining part of the reformatory education shall be converted into imprisonment so that one day of imprisonment replaces two days of reformatory education.

## 123. § (1) In the case of a juvenile, the cumulative sentence and the aggregate sentence may not exceed

a) in the case of Section 109(3)(a), twenty years,
b) in the case of Section 109(2)(a) and (3)(b), fifteen years,
c) in the case of Section 109(2)(b) and (3)(c), seven years and six months
of imprisonment.  
(2) * The imprisonment that may be imposed on a juvenile may not exceed the limits specified in (1)(a) and (b) even when applying Section 90(2).  
(3) In the case of a combination of reformatory education and imprisonment, the imprisonment shall be executed as an aggregate sentence. The court may extend its duration by a maximum of one year if this is necessary to achieve the objective specified in Section 106. However, the duration of the extension may not reach the remaining part of the reformatory education.

## 124. §

(1) If the court has ordered multiple reformatory educations against a juvenile that have not yet been executed at the time of ordering the unified measure, or that are being executed continuously, the court shall order reformatory education as a unified measure against the juvenile.  
(2) The duration of the reformatory education ordered as a unified measure shall be determined so that it is not shorter than the longest reformatory education, but does not exceed the combined duration of the ordered reformatory educations or four years.  
124/A. § * (1) * If the approval of the agreement takes place in the case of a juvenile who had not reached the age of sixteen at the time of committing the offence and who, by contributing to the proof of the case or another criminal case, cooperated significantly with the prosecutor or the investigating authority, the measure of imprisonment that may be imposed
a) in the case of committing an offence punishable by life imprisonment, may not exceed eight years,
b) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding five years, may not exceed three years,
c) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding three years, may not exceed two years,
d) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years, may not exceed six months.  
(2) * If the approval of the agreement takes place in the case of a juvenile who had reached the age of sixteen at the time of committing the offence and who, by contributing to the proof of the case or another criminal case, cooperated significantly with the prosecutor or the investigating authority, the measure of imprisonment that may be imposed
a) in the case of committing an offence punishable by life imprisonment, may not exceed ten years,
b) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding ten years, may not exceed eight years,
c) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding five years, may not exceed three years,
d) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding three years, may not exceed two years,
e) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years, may not exceed six months.  
(3) In imposing the sentence
a) in the case of concurrence of offences,
b) against a special, multiple or violent multiple recidivist, and
c) in the case of committing an offence in a criminal organisation
the penalty range according to (1) and (2) shall be taken as a basis.

## 125. §

* (1) The full period of pre-trial detention and of probationary supervision during which the court prescribed that the accused may not leave a dwelling, other premises, institution or the fenced area belonging to it without permission shall be credited towards the ordered reformatory education.  
(2) For the purposes of crediting, one day of reformatory education corresponds to
a) one day of pre-trial detention and
b) three days of probationary supervision as specified in (1).  
(3) The duration remaining after crediting shall be credited as one day of reformatory education.

## 126. § (1) A convicted juvenile is released by operation of law

a) * on the day the decision on the merits becomes final, if the execution of the imposed imprisonment is suspended,
b) on the day of completion of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability, if sentenced to imprisonment not exceeding one year for an intentional offence,
c) upon the expiry of three years from the day of completion of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability, if sentenced to imprisonment exceeding one year but not longer than five years to be executed for an intentional offence.  
(2) After completion of imprisonment exceeding one year imposed for an intentional offence, the court may grant release to the juvenile upon request if he or she is worthy of it.

## 127. §

(1) * For the purposes of this Act, a soldier is a member of the professional staff of the armed forces, the police, the Parliamentary Guard, the penitentiary organisation, the professional disaster management body, and the civilian national security services.  
(2) The provisions of this Act shall apply to soldiers with the exceptions set out in this Chapter.  
(3) A military offence as a principal offender may only be committed by a soldier.

## 128. §

(1) A soldier who commits a military offence punishable under Chapter XLV
a) against a soldier of an allied armed force, or
b) against a soldier of another state in a foreign theatre of operations during humanitarian activity, peacekeeping or humanitarian operation, or during other foreign service
during the joint performance of service duties shall be punishable.  
(2) For the purposes of (1)(a), a soldier of an allied armed force includes a member of the police or civil defence service of the state of the allied armed force; for the purposes of (1)(b), a soldier of another state includes a member of the police or civil defence service of another state.

## 129. §

A soldier who commits a military offence punishable under Chapter XLV
a) during service in allied armed forces,
b) in a foreign theatre of operations during humanitarian activity, peacekeeping or humanitarian operation
shall also be punishable.

## 130. §

(1) A soldier shall not be punishable for an act performed pursuant to an order, except if he or she knew that by executing the order he or she would commit an offence.  
(2) For an offence committed pursuant to an order, the person who gave the order shall also be liable as a principal offender if the soldier knew that by executing the order he or she would commit an offence; otherwise, the person who gave the order shall be liable as an indirect principal offender.

## 131. §

Except in the cases specified in Section 66(1), the perpetrator shall not be punishable for a military misdemeanour if one year has elapsed since the termination of his or her service relationship.

## 132. §

(1) If the convicted person may be retained in service, the court shall order the execution of imprisonment not exceeding one year or detention imposed for a misdemeanour in a military detention facility, except if the convicted person is a recidivist.  
(2) If the convicted person's service relationship has been terminated, the degree of execution of the sentence or its remaining part shall be prison.

## 133. §

Section 132 shall apply to the execution of imprisonment imposed as an aggregate sentence.

## 134. §

Community service may not be imposed on a soldier during his or her service relationship.

## 135. § (1) Against a soldier

a) demotion or
b) termination of service relationship
may be imposed in addition to another sentence, or if the upper limit of the penalty range for the offence is not more severe than three years' imprisonment, also independently.  
(2) There is no place for the application of a military sentence if the soldier is disqualified from public affairs.  
(3) * The sentence of termination of service relationship may not be applied against a person liable for military service.

## 136. § (1) Against a soldier

a) reduction in rank or
b) extension of waiting period
may be imposed in addition to a sentence.  
(2) There is no place for the application of a military accessory sentence in addition to a military sentence, or if the soldier is disqualified from public affairs.

## 137. §

(1) * Upon demotion, the soldier loses his rank and honorary rank.  
(2) Demotion shall be applied if the offender has become unworthy of the rank.

## 138. §

Termination of service relationship shall take place if the offender has become unworthy of service.

## 139. §

(1) In case of reduction in rank, the soldier shall be placed in a rank one level lower than the one he holds at the time of adjudication of the criminal offense.  
(2) Reduction in rank shall be applied if the criminal offense involves harm to the prestige of the rank, but demotion is not necessary.  
(3) Simultaneously with the reduction in rank, the time to be spent in the lower rank shall be determined for a period ranging from one year to two years.

## 140. §

(1) * In case of extension of the waiting period, the waiting period prescribed for promotion of the servicemember to the next rank shall be extended. The extension shall be determined in years, and its duration may not exceed half of the waiting period prescribed for the rank.  
(1a) * In case of extension of the waiting period, for professional members of the police, the Parliamentary Guard, the penitentiary organization, the professional disaster management body and the civilian national security services, the waiting period prescribed for advancement to the next pay grade shall be extended. The extension shall be determined in years, and its duration may not exceed half of the waiting period assigned to the pay grade.  
(2) * The waiting period shall be extended if the soldier must earn the promotion or advancement by completing a longer waiting period.  
(3) * In the case of servicemembers, the waiting period specified in paragraphs (1) and (2) shall mean the mandatory waiting period.

## 141. §

* (1) * Rehabilitation occurs by operation of law in case of demotion and termination of service relationship on the day the decision on the merits becomes final.  
(2) * The court may grant the convicted person advance rehabilitation from the disadvantages attached to criminal record in the decision on the merits if it orders the imprisonment to be executed in a military detention facility. This rehabilitation takes effect on the day of completion of the sentence or cessation of its enforceability.  
(3) The application of a military accessory punishment does not prevent the rehabilitation of the convicted person.

## 142. §

(1) Whoever, with the intent to destroy, in whole or in part, a national, ethnic, racial or religious group,
a) kills members of the group,
b) causes serious bodily or mental harm to members of the group,
c) forces the group into living conditions that threaten it or its individual members with destruction,
d) takes measures aimed at preventing births within the group,
e) abducts children belonging to the group to another group,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or by life imprisonment.  
(2) Whoever commits preparation for genocide shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.

## 143. § (1) Whoever, as part of a widespread or systematic attack against the civilian population,

a) commits homicide,
b) forces the population or a part thereof into living conditions that threaten it or its individual members with destruction,
c) displaces the population or a part thereof from its lawful place of residence,
d) * commits human trafficking and forced labor,
e) deprives another of personal liberty or unlawfully maintains his detention,
f) forces another to sexual violence or to tolerate it, to prostitution, to carry a fetus to term or to abortion,
g) causes serious bodily or mental harm to another,
h) deprives a group defined on the basis of political, national, ethnic, cultural, religious, gender or other characteristics, or a member thereof, of fundamental rights on account of belonging to the group,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or by life imprisonment.  
(2) Whoever commits preparation for a crime against humanity shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(3) For the purposes of this Section, a widespread or systematic attack against the civilian population means any conduct involving the serial commission of the acts described in paragraph (1) against the civilian population, in furtherance of or to promote a policy of a state or organization.

## 144. §

(1) Whoever, for the purpose of establishing and maintaining domination by one racial group of persons over any other racial group of persons and systematically oppressing the other racial group,
a) kills members of a racial group or groups,
b) forces a racial group or groups into living conditions aimed at bringing about the total or partial physical destruction of the group or groups,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or by life imprisonment.  
(2) Whoever commits another crime of apartheid shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment if the other crime of apartheid leads to serious consequences.  
(4) Whoever commits preparation for apartheid,
a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), from five to ten years,
b) in the case specified in paragraph (2), from two to eight years,
shall be punishable by imprisonment.  
(5) For the purposes of paragraphs (2)–(3), other crimes of apartheid means the crimes of apartheid defined in Article II, points a)/(ii), a)/(iii), c), d), e) and f) of the International Convention on the Suppression and Punishment of the Crime of Apartheid adopted by the General Assembly of the United Nations in New York on 30 November 1973, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 27 of 1976.

## 145. § The following shall be held liable in the same manner as the perpetrator of a criminal offense defined in this Chapter:

a) a military superior or a person actually acting as a military superior (hereinafter together referred to as: superior, for the purposes of this Section) if a person under his actual subordination and control or actual authority and control committed a criminal offense defined in this Chapter, and the superior knew or, under the circumstances, should have known about the commission of this criminal offense or its preparation, and did not take the measures within his competence necessary to prevent the criminal offense, or did not file a report without delay after becoming aware of the commission of the criminal offense;  
b) an official or foreign official in a leading position not falling under point a) (hereinafter referred to as: official leader, for the purposes of this Section) if a person under his actual authority and control (hereinafter referred to as: subordinate, for the purposes of this Section) committed a criminal offense defined in this Chapter, as a result of his failure to exercise proper control over him, if
ba) he knew that his subordinate committed or was preparing such a criminal offense, or consciously disregarded circumstances clearly indicating this,
bb) the criminal offense concerned activities falling within his actual duties or competence, and
bc) he did not take all necessary and reasonable measures within his competence to prevent its commission, or did not file a report without delay after becoming aware of the commission of the criminal offense.

## 146. §

(1) Any person who, in the territory of Hungary, recruits for military service or other service of military interest in a foreign armed organisation – other than allied armed forces – or acts as an intermediary for contractors for such service, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) A Hungarian citizen who voluntarily joins, offers to join, or participates in training in a foreign armed organisation – other than allied armed forces – taking part in an international or non-international armed conflict, shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).  
(3) If a person recruited or intermediated in the territory of Hungary for military service or other service of military interest in a foreign armed organisation has not reached the age of eighteen, the recruiter or intermediary shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.

## 147. §

(1) Any person who violates the conditions of an armistice shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to ten years if the violation of the armistice leads to particularly serious consequences.

## 148. §

(1) Any person who assaults, unlawfully detains, or uses other violence against the enemy's military envoy or their escort, if a more serious crime is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment up to three years.  
(2) Any person who kills the military envoy or their escort shall be punishable by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment.

## 149. § (1) Any person who in time of war

a) directs an attack against protected persons, or
b) directs an attack which may cause death or serious injury to protected persons, knowing that it is clearly excessive in relation to the anticipated direct military advantage,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment.  
(2) Any person who in time of war
a) kills a protected person,
b) subjects a protected person to conditions of life that threaten them with destruction,
c) coerces a protected person into sexual violence or tolerating it, prostitution, carrying a foetus to term or abortion,
d) unlawfully deprives a protected person of their personal liberty,
e) displaces or deports a protected person from their lawful place of residence, except where justified by an important military necessity,
shall be punishable by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment.  
(3) Any person who in time of war violates the right of a protected person to self-determination in healthcare, or causes serious physical or mental harm to a protected person, shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.  
(4) Any person who in time of war deprives a protected person or a group of protected persons of the right to a fair and regular trial shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(5) Any person who commits preparation for the offence defined in paragraph (1) or (2) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.

## 150. §

Any person who in time of war gives an order or instruction to their subordinate to leave no survivors, if a more serious crime is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.

## 151. §

(1) Any person who uses a protected person to shield a particular area or military forces from enemy military operations shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.  
(2) Any person who commits preparation for the offence defined in paragraph (1) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.

## 152. § (1) Any person who

a) coerces a protected person to serve in the armed forces or group of a power hostile to them,
b) coerces a national of the enemy power to participate in military operations against their own state,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.  
(2) Any person who commits preparation for the offence defined in paragraph (1) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(3) Any person who conscripts, enlists a person who has not reached the age of eighteen into armed forces or a group, or induces them to participate in war, provides assistance for this, or ensures the conditions necessary for this, shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.

## 153. §

(1) Any person who in time of war launches or continues an attack against an installation that does not qualify as a military objective and is militarily undefended, or launches or continues an attack which may cause damage in a militarily undefended installation or widespread, long-term and severe damage to the natural environment which is clearly excessive in relation to the anticipated direct military advantage, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to ten years if the attack is directed against
a) a hospital, a place for the treatment or accommodation of the sick and wounded, or
b) cultural property protected by international treaty.  
(3) Any person who uses or employs cultural property protected by international treaty or their immediate surroundings for military purposes, or appropriates, plunders, damages or destroys such property, shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (2).  
(4) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to fifteen years if the offence defined in paragraph (2) point b) or paragraph (3) is committed against cultural property under special or enhanced protection according to international treaty, or their immediate surroundings.  
(5) For the purposes of this Section
a) cultural property: cultural property as defined in Article 1 of the International Convention for the Protection of Cultural Property in the Event of Armed Conflict, concluded at The Hague on 14 May 1954, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 14 of 1957,
b) cultural property under special protection: cultural property as defined in Article 8 of the Convention referred to in point a),
c) cultural property under enhanced protection: cultural property as defined in Article 10 of the Second Protocol to the Convention referred to in point a), promulgated by Act XXIX of 2006.

## 154. § (1) Any person who in time of war in an operational or occupied territory *

a) plunders civilian property,
b) deprives the population of property necessary for their subsistence by extorting services or by other means, or destroys such property, except where justified by an important military necessity,
if a more serious crime is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) Any person who plunders the fallen, the wounded or the sick on the battlefield shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.

## 155. §

(1) * Any person who in time of war in an operational or occupied territory employs or causes to be employed a weapon or means of warfare prohibited by international treaty shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment.  
(2) Any person who commits preparation for the offence defined in paragraph (1) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.

## 156. §

Any person who in time of war directs an attack against the personnel, equipment, material, units or vehicles of a humanitarian, relief or peacekeeping organisation operating in accordance with the Charter of the United Nations, where they are entitled to the protection afforded to protected persons or installations by the international law of armed conflict, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.

## 157. §

Any person who in time of war misuses the emblem of the red cross, the red crescent or the red crystal, or other emblem or marking serving a similar purpose and protected by international law, if a more serious crime is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.


This excerpt sits under: A jogszabály mai napon ( 2024.02.14. ) hatályos állapota.

## 158. §

Provisions on other war crimes and the penalties that may be imposed for their commission are contained in Decree No. 81/1945. (II. 5.) ME, elevated to the force of law by Act VII of 1945, as amended and supplemented by Decree No. 1440/1945. (V. 1.) ME.

## 159. § A person shall be liable in the same manner as the perpetrator of an offence defined in this Chapter

a) if a military superior or a person acting as a military commander (hereinafter together referred to as: superior, for the purposes of this §) fails to take action, where an offence defined in this Chapter was committed by a person under his or her effective subordination and control, or effective authority and control, and the superior knew or, under the circumstances, should have known about the commission of such offence or its preparation, and did not take the necessary measures within his or her competence to prevent the offence, or did not file a complaint without delay after becoming aware of the commission of the offence;  
b) an official in a leading position or a foreign official in a leading position not falling under point a) (hereinafter referred to as: official leader, for the purposes of this §) shall also be liable if an offence defined in this Chapter was committed by a person under his or her effective authority and control (hereinafter referred to as: subordinate, for the purposes of this §), as a result of his or her failure to exercise proper control over that person, if
ba) he or she knew that his or her subordinate committed or was preparing such an offence, or consciously disregarded circumstances clearly indicating this,
bb) the offence concerned activities falling within his or her effective duties or competence, and
bc) he or she did not take all necessary and reasonable measures within his or her competence to prevent its commission, or did not file a complaint without delay after becoming aware of the commission of the offence.

## 160. §

(1) A person who kills another person shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.  
(2) The penalty shall be imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment if the homicide is committed
a) with premeditation,
b) for gain,
c) from base motives or for base purposes,
d) with particular cruelty,
e) against an official or a foreign official during or because of his or her official proceedings, against a person performing a public duty during the performance of such duty, or against a person who came to the support or defence of the official, foreign official or person performing a public duty,
f) against multiple persons,
g) endangering the lives of multiple persons,
h) as a special recidivist,
i) against a person who has not attained the age of fourteen,
j) * against a person incapable of defence,
k) * against a person with limited capacity to avert the offence due to his or her old age or disability, or
l) * after an indictment has been brought against the perpetrator for homicide.  
(3) A person who commits preparation for homicide shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(4) A person who commits homicide by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(5) A person who induces a person who has not attained the age of fourteen or a person incapable of expressing his or her will to commit suicide shall be punishable under subsection (1) if the suicide is committed.  
(6) For the purposes of point h) of subsection (2), an offence of a similar nature for the purposes of special recidivism shall be
a) genocide [§ 142 (1) point a)], homicide committed in a state of strong emotion (§ 161),
b) the more severely qualified cases of kidnapping and violence against a superior or service member [§ 190 (4), § 445 (5) point a)],
c) the more severely qualified cases of terrorist act, seizure of control of a vehicle, and mutiny, if committed by intentionally causing death [§ 314 (1), § 320 (2), §  442 (4)].  
(7) * Point l) of subsection (2) may be applied if the perpetrator
a) is convicted in the same proceedings for the homicide committed after the indictment and for the homicide committed earlier, or
b) has been convicted with final effect for the homicide committed earlier.

## 161. §

A person who kills another person in a state of strong emotion arising from excusable cause shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.

## 162. §

(1) A person who induces another person to commit suicide or provides assistance for its commission shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years if the suicide is attempted or committed.  
(2) A person who has attained the age of eighteen and who induces a person who has not attained the age of eighteen to commit suicide or provides assistance for its commission shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years if the suicide is attempted or committed.

## 163. §

(1) A person who aborts the foetus of another person shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(2) The penalty shall be imprisonment from one to five years if the abortion is committed
a) as a business,
b) without the consent of the pregnant woman, or
c) causing serious bodily harm or danger to life.  
(3) The penalty shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if the abortion causes the death of the pregnant woman.  
(4) A woman who aborts or has her foetus aborted shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

## 164. § (1) A person who injures the bodily integrity or health of another person commits bodily harm

(2) If the injury or illness caused by the bodily harm heals within eight days, the perpetrator shall be punishable for the misdemeanour of minor bodily harm by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(3) If the injury or illness caused by the bodily harm heals after more than eight days, the perpetrator shall be punishable for the felony of serious bodily harm by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(4) The penalty shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the minor bodily harm is committed
a) from base motives or for base purposes,
b) against a person incapable of defence or of expressing his or her will, or
c) against a person with limited capacity to avert the offence due to his or her old age or disability.  
(5) The penalty shall be imprisonment from one to five years if the minor bodily harm causes permanent disability or serious deterioration of health.  
(6) The penalty shall be imprisonment from one to five years if the serious bodily harm is committed
a) from base motives or for base purposes,
b) against a person incapable of defence or of expressing his or her will,
c) against a person with limited capacity to avert the offence due to his or her old age or disability,
d) causing permanent disability or serious deterioration of health,
e) with particular cruelty.  
(7) A person who commits preparation for an offence defined in subsection (3) or (6) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(8) The penalty shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if the bodily harm causes danger to life or death.  
(9) A person who commits serious bodily harm by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment
a) not exceeding one year in the case defined in subsection (3),
b) not exceeding three years in the cases defined in points b)–c) of subsection (6),
c) from one to five years in the case of causing life-threatening injury.  
(10) The offence defined in subsection (2) shall be punishable only upon private motion.

## 165. §

(1) A person who, by violating an occupational rule, negligently exposes the life, physical integrity or health of another person or other persons to direct danger, or causes bodily harm, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(2) The punishment shall be
a) imprisonment not exceeding three years, if the offense causes permanent disability, serious deterioration of health or mass disaster,
b) imprisonment from one year to five years, if the offense causes death,
c) imprisonment from two years to eight years, if the offense causes the death of more than two persons, or causes a fatal mass disaster.  
(3) If the perpetrator causes the direct danger intentionally, for a felony in the case specified in paragraph (1) imprisonment not exceeding three years, in the case specified in paragraph (2) – according to the distinction made therein – imprisonment from one year to five years, from two years to eight years, or from five years to ten years shall be imposed.  
(4) * For the purposes of this Section, occupational rule shall also mean the rule relating to the use and handling of functional firearms, explosives and explosive materials.

## 166. §

(1) A person who does not provide assistance that can be expected from him to an injured person or a person whose life or physical integrity is in direct danger shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the victim dies and assistance could have saved his life.  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony in the case of paragraph (1), and imprisonment from one year to five years in the case of paragraph (2), if the dangerous situation was caused by the perpetrator, or if he is otherwise obliged to provide assistance.  
(4) The last clause of paragraph (3) shall not be applicable against a person who is obliged to provide assistance under traffic rules.

## 167. §

A person who fails to fulfill his care obligation towards a person who, due to his condition or old age, is unable to care for himself, and thereby endangers the life, physical integrity or health of the person in need of care, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 168. §

(1) A person who performs an intervention on the human or fetal genome, or on the genome of a human embryo, aimed at altering it, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years if the intervention specified in paragraph (1) causes alteration of the genome.

## 169. §

(1) A person who uses germ cells derived from a deceased person or a deceased fetus for a special procedure aimed at human reproduction shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(2) A person who commits preparation for the prohibited use of human germ cells shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

## 170. §

A person who performs an intervention aimed at selecting the sex of the child to be born shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.

## 171. §

A person who conducts medical research on a human being without authorization or in deviation from the authorization shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.

## 172. §

(1) A person who conducts medical research on human germ cells or human embryos without authorization or in deviation from the authorization, or creates a human embryo for research purposes, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(2) A person who
a) transplants a human embryo into an animal organism,
b) fertilizes human and animal germ cells with each other,
c) implants into a human organism a human embryo on which research has been conducted,
d) uses human germ cells used for research for human reproduction,
e) uses non-human germ cells or embryos for human fertilization or embryo implantation,
f) uses a human embryo to create multiple human embryos or animal embryos,
shall be punishable by imprisonment from two years to eight years.  
(3) A person who commits preparation for the offense specified in paragraph (2) shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(4) For the purposes of this Section, human germ cell shall also mean fetal germ cell.

## 173. §

(1) A person who conducts research on a human embryo aimed at altering the genome of the embryo shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(2) A person who
a) uses a human embryo to create an individual with characteristics different from or additional to those formed at conception, or
b) separates the cells of a human embryo,
shall be punishable by imprisonment from two years to eight years.  
(3) A person who commits preparation for the offense specified in paragraph (2) shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

## 174. §

(1) A person who creates genetically identical human individuals in the course of medical research or intervention shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five years to ten years.  
(2) A person who commits preparation for the offense specified in paragraph (1) shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 175. §

(1) A person who unlawfully obtains human genes, cells, germ cells, embryos, organs, tissues, the body of a deceased person or parts thereof, or a deceased fetus, places them in circulation for pecuniary gain, or trades in them, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if the prohibited use of the human body is committed by an employee of a healthcare provider in the course of his occupation.  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years in the case specified in paragraph (1), and imprisonment from two years to eight years in the case specified in paragraph (2), if the prohibited use of the human body is committed
a) to the detriment of a person under eighteen years of age,
b) as a business, or
c) in a criminal organization.  
(4) A person who commits preparation for the prohibited use of the human body shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year in the case specified in paragraph (1), and by imprisonment not exceeding two years in the cases specified in paragraphs (2) and (3).  
(5) For the purposes of this Section, embryo shall also mean an embryo removed from the mother's body, as well as an embryo created or produced during a special procedure aimed at human reproduction and not implanted in the uterus.

## 176. §

(1) A person who offers, transfers, places in circulation, or trades in narcotic drugs shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two years to eight years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to ten years if the offense is committed
a) in a criminal organization,
b) as an official or a person performing a public duty, using this capacity, or
c) in the premises of the Hungarian Defence Forces, law enforcement agencies or the National Tax and Customs Administration.  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to twenty years or life imprisonment if the offense is committed with respect to a significant quantity of narcotic drugs.  
(4) A person who provides material resources for the commission of the offense specified in paragraphs (1)–(3) shall be punishable according to paragraphs (1)–(3).  
(5) A person who offers or transfers a small quantity of narcotic drugs shall be punishable
a) in the case specified in paragraph (1) for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years,
b) in the case specified in paragraph (2) points b)–c) by imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(6) A person who
a) commits preparation for the offense specified in paragraph (1) or (2) shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years,
b) commits preparation for the offense specified in paragraph (3) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one year to five years.

## 177. § (1) A person who has reached the age of eighteen and who

a) offers or transfers narcotics to a person who has not reached the age of eighteen,
b) places narcotics on the market or trades in them using a person who has not reached the age of eighteen,
c) in the premises of a building designated for educational, public education, child welfare or child protection tasks, or in its immediate vicinity
ca) offers or transfers narcotics,
cb) places narcotics on the market or trades in them,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to twenty years or life imprisonment if the offence is committed
a) in respect of a significant quantity of narcotics,
b) in a criminal association,
c) as a person performing official or public duties, by exploiting such capacity.  
(3) A person who provides material resources for the commission of the offence specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(2).  
(4) A person who commits the offence specified in paragraph (1) point a) or ca) in respect of a small quantity of narcotics shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years, or from two to eight years if committed as a person performing official or public duties, by exploiting such capacity.  
(5) A person who commits preparatory acts for the commission of the offence specified in paragraph (1) or (2) shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 178. §

(1) A person who cultivates, produces, obtains, possesses, imports into the territory of the country, exports from it, or transports through it narcotics shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) The punishment shall be
a) imprisonment from two to eight years if the offence is committed
aa) as a business,
ab) in a criminal association,
ac) as a person performing official or public duties, by exploiting such capacity,
b) imprisonment from five to ten years if committed in respect of a significant quantity of narcotics,
c) imprisonment from five to fifteen years if committed in respect of a particularly significant quantity of narcotics.  
(3) A person who provides material resources for the commission of the offence specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(2).  
(4) A person who commits preparatory acts for the commission of the offence specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(5) If the offence is committed in respect of a small quantity of narcotics, the punishment shall be
a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanour,
b) in the case specified in paragraph (2) points aa) and ac), imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(6) A person who consumes narcotics, or obtains or possesses a small quantity of narcotics for the purpose of consumption, if a more serious offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

## 179. § (1) A person who has reached the age of eighteen and who

a) using a person who has not reached the age of eighteen, or
b) in the premises of a building designated for educational, public education, child welfare or child protection tasks, or in its immediate vicinity
cultivates, produces, obtains or possesses narcotics shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(2) A person who has reached the age of eighteen and who imports into the territory of the country, exports from it, or transports through it narcotics using a person who has not reached the age of eighteen shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).  
(3) The punishment shall be
a) imprisonment from five to ten years if the offence is committed
aa) in a criminal association,
ab) as a business,
ac) as a person performing official or public duties, by exploiting such capacity,
b) imprisonment from five to fifteen years if committed in respect of a significant quantity of narcotics,
c) imprisonment from five to twenty years or life imprisonment if committed in respect of a particularly significant quantity of narcotics.  
(4) A person who provides material resources for the commission of the offence specified in paragraphs (1)–(3) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(3).  
(5) A person who commits preparatory acts for the commission of the offence specified in paragraphs (1)–(3) shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(6) * If the offence is committed in respect of a small quantity of narcotics, the punishment shall be for a felony
a) in the case specified in paragraphs (1) and (2), imprisonment not exceeding three years,
b) in the case specified in paragraph (3) points ab) and ac), imprisonment from one to five years.

## 180. §

(1) * A person who cultivates, produces, obtains or possesses a small quantity of narcotics for personal use, or who consumes narcotics, shall not be punishable if he or she confesses to the commission of the offence and proves by document by the time of the first-instance decision on the merits that he or she has received continuous treatment for narcotic addiction, other care treating narcotic use, or has participated in preventive-educational services for at least six months.  
(2) * Paragraph (1) shall not be applicable if within two years preceding the commission of the offence
a) the proceedings against the perpetrator were suspended on account of the ground for terminating punishability specified in paragraph (1), or
b) the perpetrator's criminal liability was established for narcotic trafficking or narcotic possession.  
(3) * In the case of § 178 paragraphs (1) and (5)–(6), and § 179 paragraphs (1)–(2) and (6) – if paragraph (1) is not applicable – the punishment may be mitigated without limitation if the perpetrator makes it possible to establish the identity of the person selling the narcotics by the time of the indictment. This provision shall not be applicable if the offence is committed in a criminal organisation.

## 181. § (1) A person who has reached the age of eighteen and who

a) induces or attempts to induce a person who has not reached the age of eighteen to the morbid enjoyment of an intoxicating substance or means not classified as a narcotic, or
b) attempts to induce a person who has not reached the age of eighteen to consume narcotics,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(2) If a more serious offence is not committed, a person who has reached the age of eighteen and who provides assistance to a person who has not reached the age of eighteen for the morbid enjoyment of an intoxicating substance or means not classified as a narcotic shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).

## 182. §

(1) A person who, for the purpose of cultivating or producing narcotics,
a) manufactures, obtains, imports into the territory of the country, exports from it, transports through it, or transfers the substance, equipment or apparatus necessary for this purpose,
b) places on the market or trades in the substance, equipment or apparatus necessary for this purpose,
if a more serious offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) A person who provides material resources for the commission of the offence shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if
a) the offence specified in paragraph (1) is committed in a criminal association, or
b) the offence specified in paragraph (1) point a) is committed as a business.  
(4) A person who commits preparatory acts for the offence specified in paragraph (1) or (3) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(5) A person shall not be punishable who – before his or her activity facilitating the preparation of narcotics came to the knowledge of the authorities – discloses his or her act to the authorities, hands over the substance, equipment or apparatus in his or her possession to the authorities, and makes it possible to establish the identity of other persons participating in facilitating the preparation of narcotics.

## 183. §

(1) Whoever, in relation to a drug precursor defined in a legal act of the European Union,
a) possesses, places on the market, transports, or carries out intermediary activity with it without authorisation or exceeding the limits of the authorisation,
b) obtains it by making a declaration with false content for that purpose,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(2) Whoever violates the notification obligation in connection with the placing on the market, transport, or intermediary activity carried out with a drug precursor defined in a legal act of the European Union shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(3) For the purposes of this Section,
a) legal act of the European Union means Regulation (EC) No 273/2004 on drug precursors (hereinafter in this paragraph: Regulation (EC) No 273/2004), and Regulation (EC) No 111/2005 laying down rules for the monitoring of trade between the Community and third countries in drug precursors (hereinafter in this paragraph: Regulation (EC) No 111/2005),
b) placing on the market means the concept defined in point (c) of Article 2 of Regulation (EC) No 273/2004,
c) transport means the concept defined in points (c) and (d) of Article 2 of Regulation (EC) No 111/2005,
d) intermediary activity means the concept defined in point (e) of Article 2 of Regulation (EC) No 111/2005,
e) notification obligation means the obligation defined in Article 8(1) of Regulation (EC) No 273/2004 and in Article 9(1) of Regulation (EC) No 111/2005.

## 184. §

* (1) Whoever offers, transfers, places on the market or trades in a new psychoactive substance shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offence is committed
a) in a criminal organisation,
b) as a person performing official or public duties, using that capacity, or
c) in a facility of the Hungarian Defence Forces, law enforcement agencies or the National Tax and Customs Administration.  
(2a) * The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to ten years if the offence is committed in relation to a significant quantity of new psychoactive substance.  
(3) * Whoever provides material means for the commission of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2a) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(2a).  
(4) Whoever offers or transfers a small quantity of new psychoactive substance shall be punishable
a) in the case defined in paragraph (1) for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year,
b) in the case defined in points (b)–(c) of paragraph (2) for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(5) * Whoever commits preparation for the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2a) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
184/A. § * (1) A person who has attained the age of eighteen years and who
a) offers or transfers a new psychoactive substance to a person who has not attained the age of eighteen years,
b) places on the market or trades in a new psychoactive substance using a person who has not attained the age of eighteen years,
c) in the premises of a building designated for the performance of educational, public education, child welfare or child protection tasks, or in its immediate vicinity,
ca) offers or transfers a new psychoactive substance,
cb) places on the market or trades in a new psychoactive substance,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two years to eight years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to ten years if the offence is committed
a) in a criminal organisation,
b) as a person performing official or public duties, using that capacity,
c) * in relation to a significant quantity of new psychoactive substance.  
(3) Whoever provides material means for the commission of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(2).  
(4) Whoever commits the offence defined in point (a) of paragraph (1) or in sub-point (ca) of point (c) of paragraph (1) in relation to a small quantity of new psychoactive substance shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years, or in the case of commission as a person performing official or public duties, using that capacity, by imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(5) Whoever commits preparation for the offence defined in paragraph (1) or (2) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
184/B. § * (1) Whoever
a) produces, imports into, exports from or transports through the territory of the country a new psychoactive substance,
b) obtains or possesses a new psychoactive substance exceeding a small quantity,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if the offence is committed
a) as a business,
b) in a criminal organisation,
c) as a person performing official or public duties, using that capacity.  
(2a) * The punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offence is committed in relation to a significant quantity of new psychoactive substance.  
(2b) * The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to ten years if the offence is committed in relation to a particularly significant quantity of new psychoactive substance.  
(3) * Whoever provides material means for the commission of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2b) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(2b).  
(4) * Whoever commits preparation for the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2b) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(5) If the offence is committed in relation to a small quantity of new psychoactive substance, the punishment shall be
a) in the case defined in point (a) of paragraph (1), for a misdemeanour, imprisonment not exceeding one year,
b) in the case defined in points (a) and (c) of paragraph (2), for a misdemeanour, imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
184/C. § * (1) A person who has attained the age of eighteen years and who
a) using a person who has not attained the age of eighteen years,
aa) produces, imports into, exports from or transports through the territory of the country a new psychoactive substance,
ab) obtains or possesses a new psychoactive substance exceeding a small quantity,
b) in the premises of a building designated for the performance of educational, public education, child welfare or child protection tasks, or in its immediate vicinity,
ba) produces a new psychoactive substance,
bb) obtains or possesses a new psychoactive substance exceeding a small quantity,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offence is committed
a) in a criminal organisation,
b) as a business,
c) as a person performing official or public duties, using that capacity.  
(2a) * The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to ten years if the offence is committed in relation to a significant quantity of new psychoactive substance.  
(2b) * The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to fifteen years if the offence is committed in relation to a particularly significant quantity of new psychoactive substance.  
(3) * Whoever provides material means for the commission of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2b) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(2b).  
(4) * Whoever commits preparation for the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2b) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(5) If the offence is committed in relation to a small quantity of new psychoactive substance, the punishment shall be
a) in the case defined in sub-point (aa) of point (a) of paragraph (1) and in sub-point (ba) of point (b) of paragraph (1), for a misdemeanour, imprisonment not exceeding two years,
b) in the case defined in points (b) and (c) of paragraph (2), for a felony, imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
184/D. § *

## 185. §

(1) Anyone who, for the purpose of enhancing sports performance, with regard to a prohibited performance-enhancing substance
a) manufactures,
b) offers, transfers, places on the market,
c) prescribes on a medical prescription or veterinary prescription,
