(1) Whoever conducts intelligence activity against Hungary for a foreign power or foreign organisation shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(2) Whoever commits the espionage defined in paragraph (1) by delivering strictly confidential classified data shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.  
(3) Whoever commits preparation for espionage shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(4) The person who – before conducting any other intelligence activity – reports their offer or undertaking to the authority or the competent body of the state and fully discloses their foreign contact shall not be punishable for offering or undertaking intelligence activity.  
261/A. § * The person who conducts intelligence activity against the European Parliament, the European Commission or the Council of the European Union for a third state outside the European Union shall be punishable according to § 261.

## 262. §

The person who commits espionage on the territory of a state allied with Hungary according to a valid international treaty containing an obligation of mutual military assistance, or against allied armed forces, shall be punishable according to § 261.

## 263. §

(1) * Whoever obtains credible knowledge that violent change of the constitutional order, conspiracy against the constitutional order, insurrection, sabotage, treason, disloyalty, supporting the enemy, espionage, espionage committed against allied armed forces, espionage against the institutions of the European Union is being prepared, or such an offence not yet detected has been committed, and does not report it to the authority or the competent body of the state as soon as possible, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment up to two years.  
(2) A relative of the perpetrator shall not be punishable for failure to report an offence against the state.

## 264. §

In the case of the offences defined in this chapter, expulsion may also be imposed on the perpetrator.

## 265. § (1) Whoever, with respect to classified information,

a) unlawfully obtains or uses it,
b) makes it accessible to an unauthorised person or inaccessible to an authorised person,
commits misuse of classified information.  
(2) The punishment is
a) detention for a misdemeanour, if it is restricted distribution,
b) imprisonment not exceeding one year, if it is confidential,
c) imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony, if it is secret,
d) imprisonment from one year to five years, if it is top secret
classified information with respect to which the offence is committed.  
(3) A person authorised by law to use classified information who commits misuse of classified information with respect to restricted distribution, confidential, secret or top secret classified information shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding one year, not exceeding two years, from one year to five years, or from two years to eight years, respectively, according to the distinction set out in paragraph (2).  
(4) Whoever commits preparation for misuse of classified information as defined in paragraph (2) points c)–d) shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanour, or not exceeding three years for a felony, respectively, according to the distinction made therein.  
(5) * A person authorised by law to use classified information who commits preparation for misuse of classified information as defined in paragraph (2) points c)–d) shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years, or from one year to five years, respectively, for a felony according to the distinction made therein.  
(6) A person authorised by law to use classified information who commits the offence by negligence shall be punishable by detention for a misdemeanour, or by imprisonment not exceeding one year, not exceeding two years, or not exceeding three years, respectively, according to the distinction set out in paragraph (2).

## 266. §

(1) Criminal law protection extends for thirty days from the initiation of classification to data for which classification has been initiated but the classification procedure has not yet been completed at the time of commission of the offence, and the perpetrator is aware of this.  
(2) *

## 267. § * (1) Whoever

a) makes data managed in a state register forming part of the national data assets inaccessible to the data controller, or
b) unlawfully or in breach of the limits of their authorisation obstructs the operation of a state register forming part of the national data assets,
if a more serious offence is not committed, shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony.  
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if
a) the offence causes significant harm to interests, or
b) the offence is committed for gain.  
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years if, as a consequence of the offence, all data managed in a state register forming part of the national data assets become permanently inaccessible to the data controller.

## 268. § (1) Whoever

a) falsely accuses another person before an authority of committing an offence,
b) brings to the attention of an authority fabricated evidence relating to an offence against another person,
shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony.  
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if criminal proceedings are instituted against the person concerned on the basis of the false accusation.  
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years if
a) the accused is convicted on the basis of the false accusation,
b) the false accusation relates to an offence for which the law also threatens the perpetrator with life imprisonment.  
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to ten years if the accused is convicted on the basis of a false accusation relating to an offence punishable by life imprisonment.  
(5) Whoever falsely accuses another person before an authority of committing an offence because, by negligence, they are unaware that their statement of fact is false or the evidence is false, shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanour.

## 269. § Whoever

a) falsely accuses another person before an authority of a petty offence or an infringement punishable by administrative fine,
b) falsely accuses another person before an authority or a person exercising disciplinary jurisdiction of a disciplinary offence,
c) brings to the attention of an authority or a person exercising disciplinary jurisdiction fabricated evidence relating to a petty offence, an infringement punishable by administrative fine or a disciplinary offence against another person,
shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding one year for a misdemeanour.

## 270. §

(1) If proceedings have been instituted as a result of a false accusation, criminal proceedings for false accusation may be instituted only on the basis of a report by the authority conducting the proceedings in that principal case until the conclusion of that principal case. Except in the case of such a report, the limitation period for the punishability of false accusation begins on the day of the conclusion of the principal case.  
(2) The punishment of the perpetrator of false accusation may be reduced without limit, and in cases deserving special leniency may be dispensed with, if they disclose the falsity of the accusation to the authority conducting the proceedings before the conclusion of the principal case.

## 271. §

(1) Whoever makes a report to an authority which they know to be false and which serves as the basis for criminal proceedings – and the case of § 268 does not apply – shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanour.  
(2) Whoever makes a report to an authority or to a person exercising disciplinary jurisdiction which they know to be false and which serves as the basis for petty offence or disciplinary proceedings – and the case of § 269 does not apply – shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(3) Whoever makes a report to an authority which they know to be false and which serves as the basis for other official proceedings – and the case of § 269 does not apply – shall be punishable by detention.

## 272. §

(1) A witness who gives false testimony or conceals the truth before an authority concerning a material circumstance of the case commits perjury.  
(2) The provisions on perjury shall apply to a person who
a) as an expert gives a false expert opinion or as an expert adviser gives false information,
b) as an interpreter or translator translates falsely,
c) provides a false document or false physical evidence in criminal or civil proceedings, except in the case of § 268 paragraph (1) point b).  
(3) * The accused in a criminal case may not be punished under paragraph (2) point c).  
(4) * Whoever commits perjury in criminal proceedings shall be punishable by imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony. If the perjury relates to an offence punishable by life imprisonment, the punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years.  
(5) Whoever commits perjury in civil proceedings shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years. If the subject matter of the civil case is of particularly high pecuniary value or of particularly significant other interest, the punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(6) Whoever commits perjury by negligence shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding one year for a misdemeanour.

## 273. §

Whoever commits perjury in petty offence or other official proceedings, or in disciplinary proceedings, shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding one year for a misdemeanour.

## 274. §

Criminal proceedings for perjury may be instituted only on the basis of a report by the authority conducting the proceedings in the principal case in which the perjury was committed, until that principal case is concluded. Except in the case of such a report, the limitation period for the punishability of perjury begins on the day of the conclusion of the principal case.

## 275. § (1) The following may not be punished for perjury:

a) a person who, if the truth were revealed, would accuse themselves or a relative of committing an offence,
b) a person who may refuse to give testimony on other grounds but was not cautioned of this before being heard, or
c) a person whose hearing is excluded by law.  
(2) * The punishment may be reduced without limit, and in cases deserving special leniency may be dispensed with, in respect of a person who, before the final or definitive conclusion of the principal case, reports to the authority conducting the proceedings the false nature of the evidence provided by them.

## 276. §

(1) * Whoever attempts to induce another person to commit false testimony is punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if committed in criminal proceedings, or by imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanor if committed in civil proceedings.  
(2) Whoever commits the offense defined in paragraph (1) in proceedings pending before a misdemeanor or other authority, or before a person exercising disciplinary jurisdiction, is punishable by imprisonment not exceeding one year for a misdemeanor.

## 277. §

* A witness who unlawfully refuses to testify before a court in criminal proceedings after being cautioned about the duty to testify is punishable by confinement for a misdemeanor.

## 278. §

(1) Whoever compels another person by force or threat not to exercise their lawful rights or not to fulfill their obligations in official proceedings commits the offense of coercion in official proceedings.  
(2) * Whoever commits the offense in criminal proceedings is punishable by imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony. If the offense is committed in criminal proceedings concerning an offense punishable by life imprisonment, the punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years.  
(3) Whoever commits the offense in civil proceedings is punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years. If the subject matter of the civil case is property of particularly high value or a particularly significant other interest, the punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(4) If the offense is committed in proceedings pending before a misdemeanor or other authority, or before a person exercising disciplinary jurisdiction, the punishment is imprisonment not exceeding one year for a misdemeanor.

## 279. §

(1) * Whoever engages in provocatively anti-social or violent conduct that disrupts, obstructs or frustrates the proceedings of an authority is punishable by imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanor, if a more serious offense is not committed.  
(2) * The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the offense defined in paragraph (1) is committed in court proceedings.  
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if the offense defined in paragraph (1) is committed in a group, while armed or in an armed state.

## 280. §

(1) Whoever discloses a fact, data or circumstance that came to their knowledge as an official witness without being released from this obligation by the person authorized to do so is punishable by imprisonment not exceeding one year for a misdemeanor.  
(2) If a more serious offense is not committed, whoever discloses what was said at a closed court hearing without the permission of the person authorized to do so is punishable in accordance with paragraph (1). *
(3) Whoever discloses a fact or data that was said at a court deliberation or contained in the minutes of the deliberation or its annexes and is not to be made public is punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).

## 281. §

(1) Whoever fails to communicate to a person subject to criminal proceedings, their defense counsel or the authority a fact upon which the termination of proceedings against the person subject to criminal proceedings or the acquittal of the person subject to proceedings may depend is punishable by imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony.  
(2) If the concealment of an exculpatory circumstance relates to an offense punishable by life imprisonment, the punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years.  
(3) Whoever fails to communicate to a person subject to misdemeanor proceedings, administrative proceedings for violations punishable by administrative fine, or disciplinary proceedings, their legal representative or the authority a fact upon which the termination of proceedings against the person subject to proceedings may depend is punishable by confinement for a misdemeanor.  
(4) The following are not punishable for concealment of an exculpatory circumstance:
a) a person who by communicating the fact would accuse themselves or their relative
aa) of an offense in criminal proceedings or official proceedings,
ab) of an offense or misdemeanor in misdemeanor proceedings,
ac) of an offense, misdemeanor, violation punishable by administrative fine or disciplinary offense in disciplinary proceedings,
b) a person whose examination as a witness is excluded by law.

## 282. §

(1) Whoever, without having agreed with the perpetrator of the offense before its commission,
a) provides assistance so that the perpetrator may escape from pursuit by the authority,
b) attempts to frustrate the success of criminal proceedings, or
c) participates in securing the benefit derived from the offense,
is punishable by imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanor.  
(2) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the harboring of a criminal is committed for profit.  
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years if the harboring of a criminal is committed
a) in connection with an offense defined in Chapter XIII, XIV or XXIV [except failure to report an offense against the state [Section 263(1)]],
b) * in connection with homicide [Section 160(1)–(3) and (5)], kidnapping [Section 190(1)–(4)], trafficking in human beings and forced labor [Section 192(1)–(6)], act of terrorism [Section 314(1)–(2)], financing of terrorism [Sections 318 and 318/A], hijacking of a vehicle [Section 320(1)–(2)],
c) in connection with an offense punishable by life imprisonment not listed in points a)–b), or
d) by an official or foreign official in the course of official proceedings in breach of official duty, or by a person performing a public function in the course of their proceedings.  
(4) Except for paragraph (2) and paragraph (3) point d), a person who commits the harboring of a criminal defined in paragraph (1) point a) in the interest of their relative is not punishable.

## 283. §

(1) Whoever escapes from the custody of the authority during criminal proceedings or during the execution of imprisonment or confinement is punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony.  
(2) * Whoever, for the purpose of evading criminal proceedings conducted against them, leaves during the period of criminal supervision the area, dwelling, other premises, institution, or enclosed area belonging thereto designated for them in violation of the rules of the coercive measure is punishable by imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanor.  
(2a) * Whoever, for the purpose of evading extradition or surrender proceedings conducted against them, leaves during the period of extradition, provisional extradition, provisional surrender, or provisional enforcement criminal supervision the area, dwelling, other premises, institution, or enclosed area belonging thereto designated for them in violation of the rules of the coercive measure is punishable in accordance with paragraph (2).  
(3) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding one year if the convicted person during the execution of imprisonment does not return after the expiration of the interruption of sentence, leave, short-term leave or absence authorized for them, for the purpose of evading the execution of the sentence.  
(3a) * The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding one year if the convicted person during reintegration custody leaves the dwelling designated for them and the enclosed area belonging thereto or the place of stay according to the daily schedule designated for them in violation of the rules of reintegration custody for the purpose of evading the execution of imprisonment.  
(3b) * If the person subject to proceedings escapes from the custody of the authority during the execution of misdemeanor detention or misdemeanor confinement, they are punishable by confinement for a misdemeanor.  
(4) The punishment of the perpetrator of escape from custody may be reduced without limitation if they voluntarily surrender before their whereabouts become known to the authority.

## 284. §

(1) A detainee who participates in open defiance that seriously endangers the order of detention together with other detainees is punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) The prison riot is punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years for
a) its initiator, organizer or leader,
b) its participant, if he applies violence against a person acting against the prison riot.  
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from five to fifteen years if the prison riot leads to particularly serious consequences.  
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from five to twenty years or life imprisonment if the prison riot causes death.  
(5) Whoever commits preparation for a prison riot is punishable by imprisonment up to three years.  
(6) In the case defined in paragraph (1), the punishment of a person who abandons the defiance voluntarily or upon the request of the authority may be reduced without limitation.

## 285. §

(1) A lawyer who, in order to cause unlawful disadvantage to his client, violates his professional duty is punishable for a felony by imprisonment up to three years.  
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years if the abuse of attorney's office is committed for the purpose of gaining profit.  
(3) For the purposes of this §, a lawyer is also a trainee lawyer and any other person who is entitled to legal representation by virtue of his occupation.

## 286. §

(1) * Whoever unlawfully and on a business-like basis performs the activity of a lawyer, patent attorney or notary public is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment up to two years.  
(2) * The punishment is imprisonment up to three years for a felony if the unauthorized practice is committed by simulating authorization for the activity of a lawyer, patent attorney or notary public.

## 287. § (1) Whoever

a) removes or violates the seal applied at seizure, attachment or sequestration ordered during official proceedings,
b) opens the closed premises serving for the safekeeping of the seized, attached or sequestered thing,
c) * makes the data subject to the obligation to preserve electronic data accessible to an unauthorized person, or withdraws or modifies it from the proceedings,
d) * makes the electronic data temporarily made inaccessible during criminal proceedings accessible to an unauthorized person, or withdraws or modifies it from the proceedings,
is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment up to one year.  
(2) Whoever withdraws a thing seized, attached or sequestered during official proceedings from enforcement is punishable for a felony by imprisonment up to three years.  
(3) * The offender is not punishable for the breaking of seals defined in paragraph (2) if, before the indictment, he returns the thing – without damage to its substance – to the acting authority.

## 288. §

(1) Whoever, during judicial enforcement, after the court has imposed a fine on him with final effect, continues the conduct that gave rise to the imposition of the fine, or still does not fulfill his obligation arising from the enforcement as provided by law – not including the obligation contained in the enforceable instrument – is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment up to one year.  
(2) * The offender is not punishable if, before the indictment, he fulfills his obligation arising from the enforcement as provided by law.

## 289. §

Whoever commits the offense defined in §§ 268–282 during or in connection with proceedings pending before an international criminal court established by an international treaty promulgated by law or by a binding decision of the United Nations Security Council, or before the Court of Justice of the European Union, is punishable according to those sections.

## 290. §

(1) Whoever gives or promises an unlawful advantage to a person performing activities for or in the interest of a business organization, or to another person in consideration of him, in order that he violate his duty, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment up to three years.  
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years if the offense defined in paragraph (1) is committed in connection with a person performing activities for or in the interest of a business organization who is authorized to take independent measures.  
(3) The punishment is
a) in the case of paragraph (1), imprisonment from one to five years,
b) in the case of paragraph (2), imprisonment from two to eight years,
if the bribery is committed in a criminal association or on a business-like basis.  
(4) Whoever commits the bribery in connection with a person performing activities for or in the interest of a foreign business organization is punishable according to paragraphs (1)–(3).  
(5) * The punishment may be reduced without limitation – in a case deserving special consideration it may be waived – against the offender of the offense defined in paragraph (1) if he reports the offense to the authority before it came to the knowledge of the authority and discloses the circumstances of the commission. This provision is not applicable if the offense is committed in a criminal organization.  
(6) * Whoever, in connection with the provision of health services, gives or promises an unlawful advantage to a health worker, a person working in health care or to another person in consideration of them, as defined in the Act on Health Care, if a more serious offense is not committed, is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment up to one year.

## 291. §

(1) * Whoever, in connection with activities performed for or in the interest of a business organization, requests an unlawful advantage, or accepts the unlawful advantage or its promise, or agrees with the requester or acceptor of an unlawful advantage requested by or given or promised to a third person in consideration of him, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment up to three years.  
(2) If the offender
a) violates his duty for the unlawful advantage, he is punishable by imprisonment from one to five years,
b) commits the offense defined in paragraph (1) in a criminal association or on a business-like basis, he is punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(3) If the offender is a person performing activities for or in the interest of a business organization who is authorized to take independent measures, the punishment is
a) in the case defined in paragraph (1), imprisonment from one to five years,
b) in the case defined in paragraph (2) point a), imprisonment from two to eight years,
c) in the case defined in paragraph (2) point b), imprisonment from five to ten years.  
(4) A person performing activities for or in the interest of a foreign business organization who commits the offense defined therein is punishable according to paragraphs (1)–(3).  
(5) * The punishment may be reduced without limitation – in a case deserving special consideration it may be waived – against the offender of the offense defined in paragraph (1) and paragraph (3) point a) if he reports the offense to the authority before it came to the knowledge of the authority, hands over the received unlawful pecuniary advantage or its equivalent value to the authority, and discloses the circumstances of the commission. This provision is not applicable if the offense is committed in a criminal organization.  
(6) * For the purposes of this §, an unlawful advantage in connection with health services is considered to be an unlawful advantage as defined in the Act on Health Care.

## 292. § *

## 293. §

(1) * Whoever seeks to influence an official person in connection with their function by means of an undue advantage given or promised to them or to another person in consideration of them, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) * The briber shall be punishable by imprisonment from one year to five years if they give or promise the undue advantage in order that the official person breach their official duty, exceed their powers, or otherwise abuse their official position.
(3) * Whoever commits the offence defined in paragraphs (1) and (2) in connection with the function of a foreign official person shall be punishable according to paragraphs (1) and (2).
(4) The manager of a business organisation, or a person authorised to control or supervise and performing activities for or in the interest of the business organisation, shall be punishable according to paragraph (1) if a person performing activities for or in the interest of the business organisation commits the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(3) in the interest of the business organisation, and the fulfilment of their supervisory or control obligation could have prevented the commission of the offence.
(5) The manager of a business organisation, or a person authorised to control or supervise and performing activities for or in the interest of the business organisation, who commits the offence defined in paragraph (4) by negligence, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(6) The punishment may be reduced without limitation – in cases deserving special consideration it may be dispensed with – in respect of the perpetrator of the offence defined in paragraphs (1) and (2), if they report the offence to the authority before it comes to the knowledge of the authority and disclose the circumstances of the commission.

## 294. §

(1) * An official person who, in connection with their function, requests an undue advantage, accepts the undue advantage or the promise thereof, or agrees with the person requesting or accepting an undue advantage requested by or given or promised to a third person in consideration of them, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offence is committed by an official person in a leading position.
(3) In the case defined in paragraph (1), the punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years, and in the case defined in paragraph (2), imprisonment from five years to ten years, if the perpetrator
a) * for the undue advantage
aa) breaches their official duty,
ab) exceeds their powers, or
ac) otherwise abuses their official position, or
b) commits the offence in a criminal association or as a business.
(4) A foreign official person who commits the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(3) shall be punishable according to paragraphs (1)–(3).
(5) * The punishment may be reduced without limitation – in cases deserving special consideration it may be dispensed with – in respect of the perpetrator of the offence defined in paragraphs (1) and (2), if they report the offence to the authority before it comes to the knowledge of the authority, hand over the received undue pecuniary advantage or its equivalent value to the authority, and disclose the circumstances of the commission. This provision shall not be applicable if the offence is committed in a criminal organisation.

## 295. §

(1) * Whoever gives or promises an undue advantage to another person or to a person in consideration of them in order that the other person not exercise their legal rights or not fulfil their obligations in judicial, arbitration or administrative proceedings, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) Whoever commits the offence defined in paragraph (1) in the course of or in connection with proceedings before an international criminal court established by an international treaty promulgated by law or by a binding decision of the United Nations Security Council, or before the Court of Justice of the European Union, shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).
(3) * The punishment may be reduced without limitation – in cases deserving special consideration it may be dispensed with – in respect of the perpetrator of the offence defined in paragraphs (1) and (2), if they report the offence to the authority before it comes to the knowledge of the authority and disclose the circumstances of the commission.

## 296. §

(1) * Whoever requests an undue advantage, accepts the undue advantage or the promise thereof, or agrees with the person requesting or accepting an undue advantage requested by or given or promised to a third person in consideration of them, in order that they not exercise their legal rights or not fulfil their obligations in judicial, arbitration or administrative proceedings, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(2) Whoever commits the offence defined in paragraph (1) in the course of or in connection with proceedings before an international criminal court established by an international treaty promulgated by law or by a binding decision of the United Nations Security Council, or before the Court of Justice of the European Union, shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).
(3) * The punishment may be reduced without limitation – in cases deserving special consideration it may be dispensed with – in respect of the perpetrator of the offence defined in paragraphs (1) and (2), if they report the offence to the authority before it comes to the knowledge of the authority, hand over the received undue pecuniary advantage or its equivalent value to the authority, and disclose the circumstances of the commission.

## 297. § *

## 298. § (1) Whoever

a) gives or promises an undue advantage to a person who claims to influence an official person, or
b) * gives or promises an undue advantage in consideration of a person who claims to influence an official person, to another person, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(1a) * Whoever gives or promises an undue advantage to a person impersonating an official person or to another person in consideration of them shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).
(2) * Whoever commits the offence defined in paragraph (1) in connection with a person performing activities for or in the interest of a business organisation shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(3) * Whoever commits the offence defined in paragraphs (1) and (1a) in connection with a foreign official person shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).
(4) * The punishment may be reduced without limitation – in cases deserving special consideration it may be dispensed with – in respect of the perpetrator of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2), if they report the offence to the authority before it comes to the knowledge of the authority and disclose the circumstances of the commission.

## 299. §

* (1) * Whoever, claiming to influence an official person, requests an unlawful advantage for himself or herself or for another, accepts the unlawful advantage or a promise thereof, or agrees with the requester or acceptor of an unlawful advantage requested by or given or promised to a third party in consideration of him or her, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if the perpetrator
a) claims or creates the appearance that he or she is bribing an official person,
b) passes himself or herself off as an official person, or
c) commits the criminal offense as a business.  
(2a) * Whoever, passing himself or herself off as an official person, requests an unlawful advantage in connection with this simulated activity, or accepts the unlawful advantage or a promise thereof, shall be punishable according to paragraph (2).  
(3) Whoever commits the criminal offense defined in paragraph (1) in connection with a person performing activities for or in the interest of a business organization shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(4) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the criminal offense defined in paragraph (3) is committed in connection with a person authorized to take independent measures who performs activities for or in the interest of a business organization.  
(5) Whoever commits the criminal offense defined in paragraphs (1) and (2) in connection with a foreign official person shall be punishable according to paragraphs (1) and (2).  
(6) The punishment may be reduced without limitation – in cases deserving special consideration it may be dispensed with – against the perpetrator of the criminal offense defined in paragraphs (1) and (3) if, before the criminal offense comes to the knowledge of the authority, he or she reports the criminal offense to the authority, hands over the unlawful pecuniary advantage received or its equivalent value to the authority, and discloses the circumstances of the commission.

## 300. §

* (1) An official person who, in this capacity, obtains credible knowledge that bribery, acceptance of bribery, official bribery, acceptance of official bribery, bribery in judicial or official proceedings, acceptance of bribery in judicial or official proceedings, trading in influence or influence peddling has been committed that has not yet been detected, and does not report this to the authority as soon as he or she can, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(2) A relative of the perpetrator shall not be punishable for failure to report a corruption offense.  
300/A. § * (1) For the purposes of this Chapter, breach of duty also includes the performance of a duty tied to the giving of an advantage.  
(2) For the purposes of Sections 290 and 291, a foreign business organization is an organization that has legal personality under its personal law and is entitled to conduct economic activity in the given organizational form.

## 301. §

(1) An official person who physically assaults another during his or her proceedings shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if the criminal offense defined in paragraph (1) is committed by a group.  
(3) Whoever commits preparation for the criminal offense of assault in official proceedings shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(4) * The punishment of a person who discloses the circumstances of the commission of the criminal offense defined in paragraph (2) before the authority prior to the bringing of charges may be reduced without limitation.

## 302. §

(1) A person performing a public duty who physically assaults another in the course of performing his or her public duty shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if the criminal offense defined in paragraph (1) is committed by a group.  
(3) Whoever commits preparation for the criminal offense of assault in proceedings by a person performing a public duty shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(4) * The punishment of a person who discloses the circumstances of the commission of the criminal offense defined in paragraph (2) before the authority prior to the bringing of charges may be reduced without limitation.

## 303. §

(1) An official person who, in order to make another give or not give testimony or a statement, uses violence, threat, or other similar method shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if the criminal offense defined in paragraph (1) is committed by a group.  
(3) Whoever commits preparation for coerced interrogation shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(4) * The punishment of a person who discloses the circumstances of the commission of the criminal offense defined in paragraph (2) before the authority prior to the bringing of charges may be reduced without limitation.

## 304. §

(1) An official person who, during his or her proceedings, unlawfully deprives another of personal liberty shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if the unlawful detention is committed
a) for a base motive or purpose,
b) with the maltreatment of the victim, or
c) causing a serious consequence.

## 305. §

An official person who, in order to cause unlawful detriment or obtain an unlawful advantage,
a) breaches his or her official duty,
b) exceeds his or her official authority, or
c) otherwise abuses his or her official position,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 306. §

A person performing a public duty who, in order to cause unlawful detriment or obtain an unlawful advantage,
a) breaches his or her duty related to the public duty,
b) exceeds his or her authority related to the public duty, or
c) otherwise abuses his or her public duty position,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 307. § (1) * An official person who

a) conducts covert information gathering subject to the authorization of a judge or the minister responsible for justice without authorization, or applies a covert device subject to judicial authorization without authorization, or exceeds the scope of the authorization for these,
b) unlawfully orders or authorizes covert information gathering subject to the authorization of a judge or the minister responsible for justice, or the application of a covert device subject to judicial authorization,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(2) * An official person on the basis of whose false statement of fact covert information gathering subject to the authorization of a judge or the minister responsible for justice, or the application of a covert device subject to judicial authorization, is ordered or authorized shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one to five years if the criminal offense defined in paragraphs (1)–(2) causes significant harm to interests.

## 308. § (1) An official person who

a) * conducts a reliability screening without prosecutorial approval, or exceeds the scope of the approval,
b) unlawfully approves it,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(2) An official person who states a false fact in the decision ordering the reliability screening, and on this basis the person authorized to do so approves the ordering of the reliability screening, shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one to five years if the criminal offense defined in paragraphs (1)–(2) causes significant harm to interests.

## 309. §

The provisions of this Chapter shall also apply mutatis mutandis to a member of an authority of a foreign state performing judicial or law enforcement duties who acts on the territory of Hungary on the basis of law.

## 310. § (1) Whoever

a) obstructs an official or foreign official person in their lawful proceedings by force or threat,
b) compels an official or foreign official person to take action in their lawful proceedings by force or threat, or
c) abuses them during or because of their proceedings,
is punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from two to eight years if the violence against an official person is committed in a group, armed or in an armed manner.  
(3) The organiser or leader of the group defined in paragraph (2) is punishable by imprisonment from five to ten years.  
(4) Whoever participates in a group aimed at committing violence against an official person is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment up to two years, and the organiser and leader of the group is punishable for a felony by imprisonment up to three years.  
(5) Whoever abuses an official person or foreign official person because of their proceedings is punishable according to paragraphs (1)–(4) even if the abused person is no longer an official person or foreign official person at the time of committing the offence.  
(6) Whoever commits preparation for violence against an official person is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment up to one year.  
(7) The participant of the group is not punishable under paragraph (4) if they leave the group voluntarily or upon the authority's request.

## 311. §

Whoever commits the offence defined therein against a person performing a public duty is punishable according to § 310.

## 312. §

Whoever commits the offence defined therein against a person who comes to the support or defence of an official person, foreign official person or person performing a public duty is punishable according to § 310.

## 313. § (1) Whoever

a) abuses,
b) deprives of personal liberty, or
c) endangers
an internationally protected person staying in the country is punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) Whoever threatens to commit the offence defined in paragraph (1) is punishable by imprisonment up to three years.  
(3) Whoever commits preparation for violence against an internationally protected person is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment up to one year.  
(4) *

## 314. § (1) Whoever, for the purpose of

a) compelling a state body, another state or international organisation to do, not do or tolerate something,
b) intimidating the population,
c) changing or disrupting the constitutional, social or economic order of another state, or disrupting the operation of an international organisation,
commits a violent, public danger causing or weapons-related offence against the person defined in paragraph (4), is punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment.  
(2) * Whoever
a) obtains control of significant material assets for the purpose defined in paragraph (1) point a), and makes leaving them intact or returning them conditional on the fulfilment of a demand addressed to a state body or international organisation, or
b) * organises or directs a terrorist group
is punishable according to paragraph (1).  
(3) The punishment of a person may be reduced without limitation if they
a) discontinue the terrorist act defined in paragraph (1) or (2) before serious consequences have arisen from it, and
b) disclose their activity to the authority,
if thereby they cooperate in preventing or mitigating the consequences of the offence, detecting further perpetrators, or preventing further offences.  
(4) For the purposes of this section, violent, public danger causing or weapons-related offences against a person are
a) homicide [§ 160 paragraphs (1)–(2)], bodily harm [§ 164 paragraphs (2)–(6) and (8)], intentional endangerment in the course of an occupation [§ 165 paragraph (3)],
b) kidnapping [§ 190 paragraphs (1)–(4)], violation of personal liberty (§ 194),
c) offence against the safety of transport [§ 232 paragraphs (1)–(2)], endangering rail, air or water transport [§ 233 paragraphs (1)–(2)],
d) abuse of radioactive material [§ 250 paragraphs (1)–(2)],
e) violence against an official person [§ 310 paragraphs (1)–(5)], violence against a person performing a public duty (§ 311), violence against a supporter of an official person or person performing a public duty (§ 312), violence against an internationally protected person [§ 313 paragraph (1)],
f) taking control of a vehicle [§ 320 paragraphs (1)–(2)], causing public danger [§ 322 paragraphs (1)–(3)], disrupting the operation of a public utility [§ 323 paragraphs (1)–(3)], abuse of explosives or explosive devices [§ 324 paragraphs (1)–(2)], abuse of firearms or ammunition [§ 325 paragraphs (1)–(3)],
g) abuse of weapons prohibited by international treaty [§ 326 paragraphs (1)–(5)], abuse of military technology products or services [§ 329 paragraphs (1)–(3)], abuse of dual-use products [§ 330 paragraphs (1)–(2)],
h) robbery [§ 365 paragraphs (1)–(4)] and vandalism [§ 371 paragraphs (1)–(6)],
i) * fraud committed using an information system [§ 375 paragraphs (2)–(4)], violation of an information system or data [§ 423 paragraphs (1)–(4)].

## 315. §

(1) * Whoever incites, offers, undertakes to commit the felony defined in § 314 paragraph (1) or (2), agrees on joint commission, or provides the necessary conditions or facilitating conditions for the purpose of facilitating the commission, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(2) * Whoever carries out the acts defined in paragraph (1) for the purpose of committing the felony defined in § 314 paragraph (1) or (2) in a terrorist group is punishable by imprisonment from five to ten years.  
(3) A person who reports the offence defined in paragraph (1) or (2) to the authority before it comes to the authority's knowledge and discloses the circumstances of the commission is not punishable.

## 316. §

* Whoever threatens to commit a terrorist act is punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
316/A. § * (1) Whoever
a) enters, exits or transits through the territory of Hungary for the purpose of committing the felony defined in § 314 paragraph (1) or (2) or inciting, offering, undertaking to commit it, agreeing on joint commission, or providing the necessary conditions or facilitating conditions for the purpose of facilitating the commission, or
b) joining a terrorist group
is punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(2) Whoever organises the travel defined in paragraph (1), or provides or collects material resources for the purpose of supporting such travel, is punishable according to paragraph (1).

## 317. §

Whoever obtains credible knowledge that the commission of a terrorist act is being prepared and does not report this to the authority as soon as possible is punishable for a felony by imprisonment up to three years.

## 318. § * (1) Whoever

a) provides or collects material resources to ensure the conditions for a terrorist act,
b) supports with material resources a person preparing to commit a terrorist act, or the perpetrator of a terrorist act, or another person in consideration of such persons, or
c) provides or collects material resources for the purpose of supporting the persons specified in point b),
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(2) Whoever commits the offence specified in paragraph (1) in connection with the commission of a terrorist act in a terrorist group or in the interest of a member of a terrorist group, or otherwise supports the activity of a terrorist group, or provides or collects material resources for the purpose of such support of a terrorist group, shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to ten years.  
318/A. § * (1) Whoever
a) provides or collects material resources to ensure the conditions for a terrorist-type offence,
b) supports with material resources a person preparing to commit a terrorist-type offence, or the perpetrator of a terrorist-type offence, or another person in consideration of such persons, or
c) provides or collects material resources for the purpose of supporting the persons specified in point b),
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment up to three years.  
(2) For the purposes of paragraph (1), a terrorist-type offence is:
a) homicide [Section 160 (1), Section 160 (2), if the act is directed against a person present at an airport serving international civil aviation or on board an aircraft in flight or on a ship at sea, or against an internationally protected person],
b) bodily harm (Section 164, if the act is directed against a person present at an airport serving international civil aviation or on board an aircraft in flight or on a ship at sea, or against an internationally protected person),
c) kidnapping [Section 190 (1)–(4)],
d) offence against the safety of transport (Section 232, if the act is directed against an aircraft or a ship at sea).  
e) illicit trafficking in radioactive material (Section 250),
f) sabotage (Section 257),
g) violence against an internationally protected person (Section 313),
h) seizure of control of a vehicle (Section 320),
i) causing public danger [Section 322 (1)–(3)],
j) disruption of the operation of a public utility (Section 323),
k) illicit trafficking in explosives or blasting agents (Section 324, if the act is committed on the premises of a public utility or a public building or structure),
l) illicit trafficking in firearms or ammunition (Section 325, if the act is committed on the premises of a public utility or a public building or structure).  
318/B. § *

## 319. §

* (1) For the purposes of Sections 314–315, Section 316/A and Section 318, a terrorist group is a group consisting of three or more persons, organised for a longer period, operating in a coordinated manner, the purpose of which is to commit a terrorist act.  
(2) For the purposes of Section 316/A, Section 318 and Section 318/A, material resources means the resources, legal documents and instruments defined in Article 1(1) of Council Regulation (EC) No 2580/2001 of 27 December 2001 on specific restrictive measures directed against certain persons and entities with a view to combating terrorism.

## 320. §

(1) Whoever seizes control of an aircraft, a vehicle for mass transport or mass freight transport by violence, threat, or by rendering another person unconscious or incapable of defence, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.  
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment if the offence causes death.  
(3) Whoever commits preparation for the seizure of control of a vehicle shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(4) The punishment of a person who abandons the offence before serious consequences have resulted may be reduced without limitation.

## 321. §

(1) Whoever incites, offers, undertakes to commit an offence in a criminal organisation, agrees on joint commission, or provides the necessary or facilitating conditions for the purpose of facilitating the commission, or otherwise supports the activity of the criminal organisation, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) A person who reports the offence to the authorities and discloses the circumstances of the commission before the offence comes to the knowledge of the authorities shall not be punishable for participation in a criminal organisation.

## 322. §

(1) Whoever causes public danger by triggering the destructive effect of matter or energy, or obstructs the prevention of public danger or the mitigation of its consequences, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from five to ten years if the offence is committed
a) in a group,
b) causing particularly large or greater damage, or
c) in complicity.  
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from five to twenty years or life imprisonment if the offence causes death.  
(4) Whoever commits preparation for causing public danger shall be punishable by imprisonment up to three years.  
(5) Whoever commits the causing of public danger by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment up to three years, in case of particularly large or greater damage from one to five years, in case of causing death from two to eight years.  
(6) The punishment of a person who voluntarily eliminates the public danger before harmful consequences have resulted may be reduced without limitation.  
322/A. § * (1) Whoever obstructs the implementation of
a) epidemiological isolation, observation, quarantine or control ordered to prevent the introduction or spread of an infectious disease subject to quarantine obligation,
b) epidemiological isolation, observation, quarantine or control ordered during an epidemic,
c) plant health or animal health measures ordered to prevent or eliminate the introduction and spread of infectious animal diseases or plant quarantine pests,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment up to three years.  
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years if the offence is committed in a group.  
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from two to eight years if the offence causes death.  
(4) Whoever commits preparation for obstructing epidemiological measures shall be punishable by imprisonment up to one year.

## 323. §

(1) Whoever disrupts the operation of a public utility to a significant extent shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from two to eight years if the offence is committed
a) in a group,
b) in complicity, or
c) causing particularly large damage.  
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from five to ten years if the offence is committed
a) armed,
b) in an armed state, or
c) causing particularly significant damage.  
(4) Whoever commits preparation for disrupting the operation of a public utility shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment up to two years.  
(5) Whoever commits the offence by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment up to three years, in case of causing particularly large or greater damage from one to five years.

## 324. §

(1) Whoever
a) manufactures, acquires, possesses, places on the market, or transfers to a person not authorised to possess explosives, blasting agents or devices for their use without authorisation,
b) brings into, takes out of, or transports through the territory of the country without authorisation or exceeding the limits of authorisation,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from five to ten years if the offence is committed as a business or in complicity.  
(3) Whoever commits preparation for illicit trafficking in explosives or blasting agents shall be punishable by imprisonment up to three years.

## 325. § (1) Whoever

a) * acquires or possesses an operational firearm without authorization,
b) manufactures or places a firearm on the market without authorization,
c) * brings an operational firearm into the territory of the country, takes it out of the country, or transports it through the country without authorization or exceeding the limits of the authorization,
d) * exceeds the limits of the authorization relating to the manufacture of, trade in, or acquisition or possession of an operational firearm,
e) transfers a firearm held with authorization to a person not having authorization,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(2) Whoever shall be punishable pursuant to paragraph (1)
a) acquires or possesses ammunition belonging to a handgun, hunting firearm or sport firearm exceeding a small quantity, or ammunition belonging to another firearm, without authorization,
b) manufactures or places ammunition on the market without authorization,
c) brings ammunition into the territory of the country, takes it out of the country, or transports it through the country without authorization or exceeding the limits of the authorization,
d) exceeds the limits of the authorization relating to the manufacture, acquisition, possession or trade of ammunition,
e) transfers ammunition belonging to a handgun, hunting firearm or sport firearm held with authorization exceeding a small quantity, or ammunition belonging to another firearm, to a person not having authorization.  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to ten years if the offense is committed on a commercial basis or in a criminal organization.  
(4) Whoever brings a handgun, hunting firearm or sport firearm held with authorization, or ammunition belonging thereto, into the territory of the country, takes it out of the country, or transports it through the country without notification, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment up to two years.  
(5) * Whoever commits preparation for an offense defined in paragraphs (1)–(3) shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment up to three years.  
(6) For the purposes of this Section, small quantity means ammunition not exceeding ten pieces.

## 326. § (1) Whoever

a) develops, manufactures,
b) acquires, possesses, uses, unlawfully renders harmless,
c) transfers to a person not authorized to possess it, brings into the territory of the country, takes out of the country, transports through the country, or places on the market,
a weapon prohibited by international treaty shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.  
(2) Whoever, without authorization or in deviation from the authorization, constructs or operates a facility suitable for the production of weapons prohibited by international treaty, or converts an existing facility for the purpose of manufacturing such weapons, shall be punishable pursuant to paragraph (1).  
(3) Whoever
a) provides technical support for the development, manufacture, assembly, quality testing, operation, maintenance or repair of weapons prohibited by international treaty,
b) deceives the authority or person entitled to decide in order to obtain the authorization specified by law for the operation of a facility suitable for the production of weapons prohibited by international treaty,
shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to ten years.  
(4) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to fifteen years if the offense defined in paragraph (3) is committed
a) on a commercial basis,
b) in a criminal organization, or
c) as an official person.  
(5) Whoever
a) commits the offense defined in paragraph (1) on a commercial basis, or
b) commits the offense defined in paragraphs (1)–(2) in a criminal organization or as an official person,
shall be punishable by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment.  
(6) Whoever commits preparation for abuse of weapons prohibited by international treaty shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(7) Whoever commits the offense described in paragraph (2) by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment up to two years.

## 327. §

(1) Whoever violates
a) the obligation to freeze funds or economic resources, or
b) an economic, commercial or financial prohibition
ordered on the basis of Hungary's international legal obligation as promulgated, or in regulations or decisions adopted pursuant to Articles 75 and 215 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union or in regulations or decisions adopted on the basis of authorization by these regulations, as well as in Council decisions adopted pursuant to Article 29 of the Treaty establishing the European Union, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if the violation of the international economic prohibition is committed
a) in connection with trade in goods applicable during the execution of the death penalty, torture or other cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment,
b) with violence, or
c) as an official person.  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to ten years if the violation of the international economic prohibition is committed
a) in connection with trade in firearms, ammunition, explosives, explosive devices, devices for their use or other products intended for military use,
b) while armed, or
c) in a criminal organization.  
(4) Whoever commits preparation for violation of an international economic prohibition shall be punishable by imprisonment up to three years.  
(5) * For the purposes of this Section – unless the legislation promulgating the obligation or prohibition provides otherwise on the basis of the international legal obligation –
a) funds shall mean the assets defined in Article 1(l) of Council Regulation (EU) No 267/2012 of 23 March 2012 concerning restrictive measures against Iran and repealing Regulation (EU) No 961/2010 (hereinafter in this paragraph: Regulation (EU) No 267/2012),
b) freezing of funds shall mean what is defined in Article 1(k) of Regulation (EU) No 267/2012,
c) economic resources shall mean the assets defined in Article 1(h) of Regulation (EU) No 267/2012,
d) freezing of economic resources shall mean the assets defined in Article 1(j) of Regulation (EU) No 267/2012,
e) * goods applicable during the death penalty, torture or other cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment shall mean the goods defined in Annex II of Regulation (EU) 2019/125 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 January 2019 concerning trade in certain goods which could be used for capital punishment, torture or other cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment (hereinafter for the purposes of this Chapter: Regulation (EU) 2019/125 of the European Parliament and of the Council).

## 328. §

(1) Whoever obtains credible knowledge that a violation of an international economic prohibition is being prepared, or that such an offense not yet uncovered has been committed, and does not report it to the authorities as soon as possible, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment up to one year.  
(2) A relative of the perpetrator shall not be punishable for failure to report a violation of an international economic prohibition.

## 329. § (1) Whoever

a) manufactures, places on the market, or provides military technology services without authorisation or exceeding the scope of the authorisation,
b) uses military technology products in a manner different from the authorisation,
c) produces, acquires, uses, possesses, transfers, places on the market, brings into the territory of the country, takes out of it, or transports through it prohibited military technology products,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.  
(2) Whoever shall be punishable pursuant to paragraph (1):
a) provides technical support in connection with the development, manufacture, trade, maintenance, repair, detection, identification or proliferation of chemical, biological or nuclear weapons, other nuclear explosives, or missile technology equipment capable of delivering them,
b) provides technical assistance in connection with other military use, other than that set out in point (a), in relation to a country subject to arms export restrictions binding on Hungary under international commitments.  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to ten years if the offence defined in paragraph (1) is committed in a criminal organisation or on a commercial basis.  
(4) Whoever commits preparation for the abuse of military technology products or services shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(5) For the purposes of this Section:
a) authorisation shall also mean the International Import Certificate and the documents replacing them,
b) * military technology product shall also mean the goods specified in Annex III to Regulation (EU) 2019/125 of the European Parliament and of the Council,
c) * prohibited military technology product means the goods specified in Annex II to Regulation (EU) 2019/125 of the European Parliament and of the Council.

## 330. § (1) Whoever

a) places dual-use items in foreign trade without authorisation or exceeding the scope of the authorisation, including their transfer within the customs territory of the European Union, or
b) uses dual-use items in a manner different from the authorisation,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if the offence defined in paragraph (1) is committed
a) * in connection with chemical substances falling within the scope of Annex 1 to Act LXIII of 2020 on the promulgation of the amendment to the Annex to the Convention on the Prohibition of the Development, Production, Stockpiling and Use of Chemical Weapons and on their Destruction, signed in Paris on 13 January 1993, being brought into the territory of Hungary or transferred from there to the customs territory of the European Union, or
b) in relation to nuclear dual-use items.  
(3) Whoever commits preparation for the abuse of dual-use items shall be punishable by imprisonment for up to three years.  
(4) For the purposes of this Section:
a) authorisation shall also mean the International Import Certificate and the documents replacing them,
b) dual-use item means a product as defined in Article 2(1) of Regulation (EC) No 428/2009 setting up a Community regime for the control of exports, transfer, brokering and transit of dual-use items,
c) * the customs territory of the European Union means the territory defined in Article 4 of Regulation (EU) No 952/2013 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 9 October 2013 laying down the Union Customs Code.

## 331. §

* (1) Whoever incites to war before a large public, or otherwise conducts war propaganda, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) Whoever, if a more serious offence is not committed, incites to the support of terrorism before a large public, or otherwise conducts propaganda supporting terrorism, shall be punishable pursuant to paragraph (1).

## 332. § * Whoever before a large public

a) against the Hungarian nation,
b) against any national, ethnic, racial, religious group or a member thereof, or
c) against certain groups of the population or their members – in particular on the grounds of disability, gender identity, sexual orientation –
incites to violence or hatred, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment for up to three years.

## 333. §

* Whoever before a large public denies, questions, presents in an insignificant light, or seeks to justify the fact of genocide or other crimes against humanity committed by the National Socialist or Communist regimes, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment for up to three years.

## 334. §

Whoever before a large public uses an insulting or degrading expression concerning the anthem, flag, coat of arms or the Holy Crown of Hungary, or otherwise desecrates them, if a more serious offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment for up to one year.

## 335. §

* Whoever disseminates, uses before a large public, or displays publicly a swastika, SS insignia, arrow cross, hammer and sickle, five-pointed red star or symbols depicting them in a manner capable of disturbing public order – in particular in a manner violating the human dignity or funeral rights of the victims of totalitarian regimes –
a) disseminates,
b) uses before a large public, or
c) displays publicly,
if a more serious offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by confinement.

## 336. §

Whoever before a large public, in a manner capable of disturbing public order, incites to general disobedience against an order of an authority, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment for up to three years.

## 337. §

* (1) Whoever at the scene of a public danger, before a large public, in connection with the public danger, asserts or spreads a false fact or distorts a true fact in such a manner that is capable of causing confusion or unrest among a larger group of people at the scene of the public danger, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment for up to three years.  
(2) Whoever during a special legal order, before a large public, asserts or spreads a false fact or distorts a true fact in such a manner that is capable of hindering or frustrating the effectiveness of defence, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.

## 338. §

(1) Whoever asserts or spreads a false fact capable of disturbing public order, or creates the appearance that an event involving public danger is threatening to occur, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment for up to three years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one to five years if the threat of public danger has seriously disturbed public order.

## 339. §

(1) Whoever engages in provocatively anti-social, violent conduct capable of causing outrage or alarm in others, if a more serious offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment for up to two years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment for up to three years for a felony if the disorderly conduct is committed
a) in a group,
b) seriously disturbing public order,
c) while armed,
d) while equipped with weapons, or
e) * at a public event or assembly.

## 340. §

(1) * Whoever resists with violence or threat the lawful measures taken by the organiser or steward of a public event or assembly for the purpose of maintaining order, if a more serious criminal offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) If a more serious criminal offence is not committed, whoever at a sports event unlawfully enters or stays in an area of the facility that is closed off from spectators or from a specified group of spectators, or throws into such area an object endangering the holding of the sports event or the physical integrity of others, shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the disturbance of order is committed
a) in a group,
b) while armed,
c) while equipped with weapons, or
d) as a special recidivist,
e) * in violation of the restrictions ensuring the peaceful nature of the assembly under the Act on the right of assembly.
(4) For the purposes of special recidivism, criminal offences of a similar nature are
a) bodily harm [Section 164 paragraphs (3)–(6) and (8)],
b) violence against an official [Section 310 paragraphs (1)–(5)], violence against a person performing a public duty (Section 311), violence against a supporter of an official or a person performing a public duty (Section 312),
c) hooliganism (Section 339),
d) vandalism [Section 371 paragraphs (1)–(6)].

## 341. §

The perpetrator of hooliganism and disturbance of order may also be subject to a ban.

## 342. § (1) Whoever

a) produces a false public document or falsifies the content of a public document,
b) uses a false, falsified or genuine public document issued in another person's name,
c) participates in having false data, facts or statements concerning the existence, modification or termination of a right or obligation recorded in a public document,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(1a) * The criminal offence under paragraph (1) point c) shall not be committed in relation to the registration of an address in the register containing the personal and address data of citizens, if it is done with the consent of the accommodation provider or by registration at a property owned by the person.
(2) Whoever commits preparation for forgery of public documents as defined in paragraph (1) points a) or b) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(3) Whoever commits the forgery of public documents as defined in paragraph (1) point c) by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by confinement.

## 343. § (1) An official who, abusing his or her official authority,

a) produces a false public document,
b) falsifies the content of a public document, or
c) falsely records a material fact in a public document,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(2) The provisions of this Section shall also apply accordingly to a member of a foreign state's authority performing judicial or law enforcement tasks who acts on the territory of Hungary on the basis of law.
343/A. § * (1) Whoever
a) issues a document that can be confused with a diploma certifying qualifications obtained in higher education or a certificate certifying qualifications obtained in non-formal education or in school-based vocational training, or
b) unlawfully confers a
ba) diploma certifying qualifications,
bb) academic degree,
bc) title, or
bd) abbreviation
as defined in the Act on national higher education or that can be confused with such,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) Whoever commits the criminal offence defined in paragraph (1) in a criminal organisation or on a commercial basis shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 344. § Whoever

a) produces a false security document authorising entry as defined in separate legislation,
b) falsifies the content of a security document authorising entry,
c) uses a false, falsified or genuine security document authorising entry issued in another person's name,
if a more serious criminal offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

## 345. §

Whoever uses a false, falsified or false-content private document to prove the existence, modification or termination of a right or obligation shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

## 346. §

* (1) Whoever, in relation to one or more public documents that are not or not exclusively his or her own,
a) unlawfully obtains them from another person without that person's consent,
b) unlawfully destroys or damages them, or
c) unlawfully takes them from another person or unlawfully conceals them from the person entitled,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) If another criminal offence is not committed, whoever transfers or receives a public document for the purpose of unlawful gain shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).
(3) Whoever commits the criminal offence defined in paragraph (1) in relation to a private document in order to obtain an unlawful advantage or cause an unlawful disadvantage shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

## 347. § (1) Whoever

a) removes or falsifies a unique identification mark,
b) obtains, uses or disposes of an object whose unique identification mark is false, falsified, or whose unique identification mark has been removed,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if the criminal offence defined in paragraph (1) is committed on a commercial basis or in a criminal organisation.
(3) For the purposes of this Section, unique identification mark means: the marking applied by the manufacturer or the authority on an object, or on a component thereof, the possession or intended use of which is subject to official authorisation by law, serving for unique identification.

## 348. §

Whoever, for the purpose of unlawful gain, falsifies the value indicated by the odometer of a road motor vehicle by influencing the instrument or by affecting the measurement process, or prevents the operation of the instrument, if a more serious criminal offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

## 349. § * (1) Whoever

a) for the purpose of acquiring ownership of agricultural and forestry land,
b) for the purpose of establishing a usufruct right or right of use over agricultural and forestry land
concludes a void contract by circumventing the statutory prohibition or restriction relating to these, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(2) Whoever
a) for the acquisition of ownership of agricultural and forestry land,
b) for the establishment of a usufruct right or right of use over agricultural and forestry land
uses agricultural and forestry land or collects its benefits on the basis of an agreement created to conceal a void contract concluded by him or her by circumventing a statutory prohibition or restriction, if a more serious criminal offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(3) * A lawyer, chamber legal counsel or notary public who participates in the conclusion of a contract under paragraph (1) shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).
(4) The punishment of the perpetrator of the criminal offence under paragraphs (1) and (2) may be reduced without limitation if he or she reports the criminal offence to the authority before it comes to the authority's knowledge and discloses the circumstances of the commission.
