(2) A person who, for the purpose of selling goods, states a false fact or presents a true fact in a manner capable of deception before a large public or in respect of an essential characteristic of goods in significant quantity or value, or provides information capable of deception, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the criminal offence defined in paragraph (2) is committed in connection with characteristics relating to the effect of the goods on health or the environment, its dangerousness, risks or safety.  
(4) For the purposes of this Section
a) an essential characteristic of goods means:
aa) the composition, technical characteristics and suitability of the goods for the given purpose,
ab) * the origin, place of origin and method of production of the goods,
ac) the testing, verification or result thereof of the goods;  
b) organised product presentation means: retail activity carried out during a trip or event organised for the purpose of marketing a product.

## 418. §

A person who, for the purpose of obtaining an unlawful advantage or causing financial detriment to another, unlawfully acquires, uses, makes accessible to another person or discloses to the public a trade secret shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 419. §

(1) A person who, without the consent of a competitor, manufactures goods with a distinctive appearance, packaging, marking or designation by which the competitor or its goods with distinctive characteristics can be recognised, or acquires, keeps or places on the market such goods for the purpose of placing them on the market, if no other criminal offence is committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the criminal offence is committed in respect of imitation goods in significant quantity or value.

## 420. §

(1) A person who, for the purpose of influencing the outcome of a public procurement procedure or an open or restricted tender announced in respect of an activity subject to concession, concludes an agreement or engages in other concerted practice aimed at fixing prices, fees, other contractual conditions or dividing the market, and thereby restricts competition, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(2) A person who, for the purpose of influencing the outcome of a public procurement procedure or an open or restricted tender announced in respect of an activity subject to concession, participates in the adoption of a decision by an association of undertakings, a public body, an association or other similar organisation which restricts competition shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanour if the criminal offence defined in paragraph (1) or (2) is committed in respect of a public procurement value not exceeding a significant value.  
(4) * The perpetrator of the criminal offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(3) shall not be punishable if he reports the act to the authority before it comes to the knowledge of the authority acting in criminal matters and discloses the circumstances of the commission.  
(5) The perpetrator of the criminal offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(3) shall not be punishable if, at the time of committing the act, he is an executive officer, member, member of the supervisory board, employee or agent thereof of an undertaking which – before the authority acting in competition supervision matters has initiated an investigation in the case – has submitted an application giving grounds for the waiver of a fine under the law on the prohibition of restriction of competition covering the act, and discloses the circumstances of the commission.  
(6) The punishment may be reduced without limit, and in cases deserving special consideration may be waived, in respect of a person who, at the time of committing the criminal offence, is an executive officer, member, member of the supervisory board, employee or agent thereof of an undertaking which has submitted to the authority acting in competition supervision matters an application giving grounds for the waiver or reduction of a fine under the law on the prohibition of restriction of competition covering the act, and discloses the circumstances of the commission.

## 421. § For the purposes of this Chapter

a) product means: any tangible, marketable movable thing and natural forces that can be exploited in the manner of a thing,
b) goods means: a product, immovable property, a service and a right of pecuniary value.

## 422. §

(1) Anyone who, for the purpose of unlawfully obtaining personal data, private secrets, economic secrets or business secrets,
a) * secretly searches another person's dwelling, other premises belonging thereto, or the enclosed area belonging thereto,
b) * secretly observes or records events occurring in another person's dwelling, other premises belonging thereto, or the enclosed area belonging thereto, by using technical means,
c) * secretly opens or obtains another person's postal consignment or other closed consignment, and records its content by technical means,
d) * secretly intercepts the content of communication conducted via electronic communications network or device, or on an information system, and records what is perceived by technical means,
e) * secretly intercepts data processed in an information system, and records what is perceived by technical means,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(1a) * Anyone who, for the purpose of unlawfully obtaining personal data, private secrets, economic secrets or business secrets,
a) * secretly searches premises or areas other than public places or places open to the public, vehicles – with the exception of public transport vehicles – or objects in another person's use,
b) secretly observes or records events occurring in premises or areas other than public places or places open to the public, or in vehicles – with the exception of public transport vehicles – by using technical means,
shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).  
(2) * Anyone who collects information beyond that specified in paragraph (1) for the purpose of establishing the identity or activity of an undercover investigator or a person secretly cooperating with an authority authorized to conduct covert intelligence gathering or to use covert means shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).  
(3) Anyone who transmits or uses personal data, private secrets, economic secrets or business secrets obtained in the manner specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).  
(4) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if the unlawful data acquisition specified in paragraphs (1)–(3) is committed
a) by simulating official proceedings,
b) as a business,
c) in a criminal organization, or
d) causing significant harm to interests.  
422/A. § * (1) Anyone who, by unauthorized use of an unmanned aerial vehicle, observes another person's dwelling, other premises, or the enclosed area belonging thereto, and records the events occurring there, if no other criminal offense is committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by confinement.  
(2) Anyone who, if no other criminal offense is committed, makes audio or video recordings made during the observation specified in paragraph (1) accessible to the general public shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(3) The criminal offenses specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) shall be punishable only upon private motion.

## 423. §

* (1) Anyone who unlawfully enters an information system by breaching or circumventing the technical measures ensuring the protection of the information system, or remains therein beyond or in breach of their access authorization, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(2) Anyone who
a) unlawfully or in breach of the limits of their authorization obstructs the operation of an information system, or
b) unlawfully or in breach of the limits of their authorization alters, deletes or makes inaccessible data in an information system,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(3) * The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony if the criminal offense specified in paragraph (2) affects a significant number of information systems or causes significant harm to interests.  
(4) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years if the criminal offense is committed against a public utility.  
(5) For the purposes of this section, data means: any representation of facts, information or concepts stored, processed, handled or transmitted in an information system in any form suitable for processing by an information system, including any program that enables the performance of a function by an information system.

## 424. §

(1) * Anyone who, for the purpose of committing the criminal offense specified in § 375, in point d) of § 422 paragraph (1), or in § 423,
a) prepares, transfers, makes accessible, obtains, or places into circulation a password or computer program necessary for or facilitating this, or
b) places at another person's disposal their economic, technical, or organizational knowledge relating to the preparation of a password or computer program,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(2) * The perpetrator of the criminal offense specified in point a) of paragraph (1) shall not be punishable if – before the preparation of the password or computer program necessary for or facilitating the commission of the criminal offense came to the knowledge of the authority acting in criminal matters – they disclose their activity to the authority, hand over the prepared item to the authority, and enable the establishment of the identity of other persons participating in the preparation.  
(3) For the purposes of this section, password means: any identifier consisting of numbers, letters, characters, biometric data or a combination thereof, enabling entry into an information system or a part thereof.

## 425. §

(1) A person liable for military service who fails to fulfill their military call-up obligation shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(2) Anyone who commits the criminal offense by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 426. §

A person liable for military service who, for the purpose of evading military service,
a) fails to fulfill their obligation to appear or be called up, or
b) mutilates their body, harms their health, or engages in deceptive conduct,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five years to ten years.

## 427. §

A person liable for military service who refuses to perform their military service shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five years to fifteen years.

## 428. §

(1) Anyone who commits an act aimed at preventing a person liable for military service from fulfilling their call-up obligation shall be punishable for a felony according to the provisions of § 425.  
(2) * Anyone who commits an act aimed at evading a person liable for military service from military service
a) in the manner specified in point a) of § 426, or
b) in the manner specified in point b) of § 426,
shall be punishable according to the provisions therein.

## 429. §

(1) * Anyone who fails to fulfill their civil defense obligation shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if the criminal offense causes serious danger.  
(3) Anyone who commits the criminal offense specified in paragraph (2) by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.

## 430. §

A person obligated to perform defense work who seriously breaches this obligation by absence or in another manner shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 431. §

* Anyone who seriously breaches or evades their defense and security obligation consisting of economic and material services shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.

## 432. §

The punishment may be mitigated without limitation if the perpetrator of a criminal offense specified in this chapter voluntarily fulfills the obligation they failed to perform.

## 433. § (1) * The provisions of this chapter shall be applied in a state of war.

(1a) * Point b) of § 426 and point b) of paragraph (2) of § 428 shall also be applied during the period following the Government's initiation of a state of war.  
(2) * Breach of civil defense obligation may be committed, in addition to those specified in paragraph (1), in a state of emergency and in a state of danger, and breach of service obligation may be committed in a state of danger as well.  
(3) * Breach of defense work obligation may be committed only in a state of war.

## 434. §

(1) Anyone who, for the purpose of permanently evading the performance of their military service, arbitrarily leaves their place of service or stays away from it, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if the desertion is committed
a) while armed,
b) in a group,
c) during the performance of important service or by exploiting the service,
d) by using violence against a person, or
e) to a foreign country.  
(3) * Anyone who deserts to a foreign country in the manner specified in points a)–c) of paragraph (2), or who commits the desertion in time of war, is punishable by imprisonment from five years to fifteen years.  
(4) Anyone who commits preparation for desertion as specified in paragraph (2) is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year, or in the case specified in paragraph (3) for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(5) The punishment of the perpetrator of desertion may be reduced without limitation if they voluntarily report to the authorities within thirty days. The day of desertion is not included in the time limit.

## 435. §

(1) Anyone who arbitrarily leaves their place of service or stays away from it, and their absence exceeds two days, is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(2) If the duration of the unauthorized absence exceeds nine days, the punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony.  
(3) * If the unauthorized absence is committed in time of war, during humanitarian activity or peacekeeping in a foreign theater of operations, the punishment is
a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony,
b) in the case specified in paragraph (2), imprisonment from two years to eight years.

## 436. §

(1) Anyone who, for the purpose of permanently evading the performance of their military service, mutilates their body, damages their health, or engages in deceptive conduct, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(2) Anyone who commits the offense specified in paragraph (1) in order to temporarily evade the performance of their military service is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(3) If the duration of the temporary evasion exceeds six days, the punishment is imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(4) * If the offense is committed in time of war, the punishment is
a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment from five years to fifteen years,
b) in the case specified in paragraph (2), imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony,
c) in the case specified in paragraph (3), imprisonment from two years to eight years.

## 437. §

(1) Anyone who expressly refuses to perform military service is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(1a) * The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if the refusal of service is committed during a state of national defense crisis.  
(2) * The punishment is imprisonment from five years to fifteen years if the refusal of service is committed in time of war.

## 438. §

(1) Anyone who, while on guard, watch or other standby duty – in violation of the service regulations applicable to the given service – falls asleep, or during the service consumes alcoholic beverages, or narcotics or intoxicating substances or agents not classified as narcotics, leaves their designated place, or otherwise seriously violates the regulations on the performance of the service, is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(2) If the offense carries the risk of significant detriment to the service, the punishment is for a felony
a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment not exceeding three years,
b) * in time of war, imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to fifteen years for a felony if the offense is committed in a combat situation or during humanitarian activity or peacekeeping in a foreign theater of operations, and particularly great detriment results from it.  
(4) Anyone who commits the offense by negligence is punishable for a misdemeanor in the case specified in paragraph (2), according to the distinction made therein, by confinement or imprisonment not exceeding one year, respectively, and in the case of paragraph (3) by imprisonment not exceeding three years.

## 439. §

(1) Anyone who evades an important service duty by deception or absence, or renders themselves incapable of performing it, commits a misdemeanor and is punishable by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(2) If the offense specified in paragraph (1) carries the risk of significant detriment to the service or is committed during humanitarian activity or peacekeeping in a foreign theater of operations, the punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony.  
(3) * If the offense specified in paragraph (2) is committed in time of war, the punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years.

## 440. §

(1) Anyone who fails to make a report in a timely manner in an important service matter, or makes a false report, if the offense carries the risk of significant detriment to the service, is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(2) * The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony if the offense specified in paragraph (1) is committed in time of war, during humanitarian activity or peacekeeping in a foreign theater of operations.

## 441. §

(1) Anyone who, in order to cause unlawful detriment or obtain unlawful advantage, abuses their service authority or position, if a more serious offense is not committed, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony if significant detriment results from the offense.

## 442. §

(1) Anyone who participates in collective, open resistance directed against service order and discipline, which significantly disrupts the performance of service duties, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(2) The following are punishable by imprisonment from two years to eight years for mutiny:
a) the initiator, organizer or leader,
b) a participant who uses violence against a superior or against someone acting against the mutiny.  
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to fifteen years if the mutiny results in particularly serious consequences.  
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to twenty years or life imprisonment if the mutiny causes death.  
(5) * The punishment is
a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment from two years to eight years,
b) in the case specified in paragraph (2), imprisonment from five years to fifteen years,
if the mutiny is committed in time of war.  
(6) If the mutiny specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) is committed in a combat situation, the punishment is imprisonment from five years to fifteen years.  
(7) * Anyone who commits preparation for mutiny is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years, or in time of war or in a combat situation by imprisonment from one year to five years.  
(8) In the case specified in paragraph (1), the punishment of anyone who abandons the mutiny before it results in more serious consequences or after being called upon to do so may be reduced without limitation.

## 443. §

(1) Anyone who does not prevent, to the extent possible, a mutiny or its preparation that has come to their knowledge, or does not report it without delay, is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(2) * The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony if the offense specified in paragraph (1) is committed in time of war or in a combat situation.

## 444. § (1) Anyone who fails to obey an order shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment

(2) If the disobedience to an order is committed in a group, the punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding two years.  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the disobedience to an order
a) occurs in the presence of other subordinates or otherwise publicly, whether by express refusal to obey the order or in another offensive manner,
b) entails a risk of significant detriment to the service or to discipline.  
(4) * The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if the offense defined in subsection (3) is committed in time of war.  
(5) Anyone who fails to obey a combat order in war, or an order concerning the use of weapons during humanitarian activity or peacekeeping in a foreign theater of operations, shall be punishable by imprisonment from five years to fifteen years.

## 445. § (1) Anyone who

a) against a superior officer,
b) against a senior, a guard or other service agent
uses violence, threatens with it, or offers physical resistance during or because of the performance of their service, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if
a) the offense is committed while armed, having armed oneself or in a group,
b) the offense is also disobedience to an order,
c) the offense entails serious bodily harm or a risk of significant detriment to the service or to discipline.  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offense causes permanent disability, serious deterioration of health or danger to life.  
(4) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to fifteen years if the offense causes the death of the victim.  
(5) The punishment shall be imprisonment from ten years to twenty years or life imprisonment if
a) the offense also constitutes intentional homicide, or
b) the offense is committed in a combat situation.  
(6) * The punishment shall be
a) in the case defined in subsection (1), imprisonment from one year to five years,
b) in the case defined in subsection (2), imprisonment from two years to eight years,
c) in the case defined in subsection (3), imprisonment from five years to fifteen years
if the offense is committed in time of war.

## 446. §

Anyone who commits the offense defined in § 445 against a person who has come to the defense of or has been assigned to protect a superior officer or service agent shall be punishable according to § 445.

## 447. § (1) Anyone who

a) the superior officer,
b) a senior, guard or other service agent performing their service
grossly insults the authority of, in the presence of others or in a conspicuously coarse manner, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the offense is committed in the presence of several soldiers or otherwise publicly.

## 448. §

(1) Anyone who incites discontent among soldiers toward a superior officer, an order, the service order or discipline shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the incitement results in significant detriment to the service or to discipline.

## 449. §

(1) Anyone who insults their subordinate in their human dignity in the presence of others or in a conspicuously coarse manner shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the offense is committed
a) from base motives,
b) causing severe physical or mental suffering,
c) in the presence of several soldiers,
d) to the detriment of several subordinates.  
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if the offense causes serious bodily harm or significant detriment to the service.

## 450. § Anyone who, by abusing their superior authority,

a) subjects their subordinate to disciplinary punishment,
b) restricts them in the exercise of their right to complain,
c) shortchanges them in their entitlements or burdens them financially,
d) uses them for private purposes,
e) treats them more favorably or less favorably compared to others,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.

## 451. §

(1) Anyone who breaches their superior duty by failing to take the necessary measures for the material provision of their subordinate, for protecting them from imminent danger or for their rescue, if a more serious offense is not committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(2) If the offense entails significant detriment to the service or to discipline, the punishment shall be for a felony
a) in the case defined in subsection (1), imprisonment from one year to five years,
b) * in time of war, imprisonment from two years to eight years.  
(3) Anyone who commits the offense defined in subsection (2) by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor – according to the distinction made therein – by imprisonment not exceeding one year or three years, respectively.

## 452. § (1) Anyone who, in breach of their superior duty,

a) fails to take the necessary measures to prevent or bring to account the breach of duty or offense of a subordinate,
b) fails to take the necessary measures to overcome a disturbance threatening the service order, discipline or public safety,
if a crime against humanity or war crime is not committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(2) If the offense entails significant detriment to the service, to discipline or to public safety, the punishment shall be for a felony
a) in the case defined in subsection (1), imprisonment from one year to five years,
b) * in time of war, imprisonment from two years to eight years.  
(3) Anyone who commits the offense defined in subsection (2) by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor – according to the distinction made therein – by imprisonment not exceeding one year or three years, respectively.

## 453. §

(1) Anyone who breaches their superior duty by failing to supervise their subordinate in the performance of their service, and this entails significant detriment to the service or to discipline, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.  
(2) If the offense entails particularly great detriment to the service or to discipline, the punishment shall be for a felony
a) in the case defined in subsection (1), imprisonment from one year to five years,
b) * in time of war, imprisonment from two years to eight years.  
(3) Anyone who commits the offense defined in subsection (2) by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor – according to the distinction made therein – by imprisonment not exceeding one year or three years, respectively.

## 454. §

(1) Anyone who directly endangers the increase of the unit's readiness by, in breach of their service duty,
a) failing to ensure the necessary armament, combat equipment or other war materiel or to protect its stock,
b) destroying, rendering unusable or otherwise diverting from its purpose important armament, combat equipment or other important war materiel,
commits a felony and shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years, or in time of war by imprisonment from two years to eight years.  
(2) * If the offense entails particularly great detriment to the service, the punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years, or in time of war imprisonment from five years to fifteen years.  
(3) Anyone who commits the offense by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor in the case of subsection (1) – according to the distinction made therein – by imprisonment not exceeding one year or three years, respectively, and in the case of subsection (2) – according to the distinction made therein – by imprisonment not exceeding two years or from one year to five years, respectively.

## 455. § Anyone who, in a combat situation, in breach of their command duty,

a) surrenders the subordinate soldiers to the enemy or allows them to be captured,
b) destroys without compelling necessity the important combat position, installation, combat equipment or other war materiel entrusted to them, or hands it over to the enemy in usable condition,
c) fails to put up against the enemy the resistance of which they are capable,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five years to fifteen years.

## 456. § Anyone who evades the performance of their combat duty

a) by unauthorized abandonment of their post, by hiding or by fleeing,
b) by deliberately causing incapacity for combat or by deceptive conduct,
c) by abandoning, damaging or failing to use their weapon,
d) by voluntary surrender to the enemy, or
e) by other serious breach of their service duty,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.

## 457. §

(1) * Anyone who, in time of war, sows discontent among soldiers or creates despondency shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.  
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to fifteen years if the criminal offence
a) causes discontent or other breach of duty among soldiers in a combat situation, or
b) results in other significant detriment to the service.

## 458. § For the purposes of this Chapter

a) military service means service performed by the persons specified in Section 127(1),
b) combat situation means the conduct of actual combat operations.

## 459. § (1) For the purposes of this Act

1. * criminal organisation: a group consisting of at least three persons, organised hierarchically for a longer period of time, operating conspiratorially, the purpose of which is to commit intentional criminal offences punishable by imprisonment of five years or more;
2. criminal association is established when two or more persons commit criminal offences in an organised manner, or agree to do so, and attempt to commit at least one criminal offence, but a criminal organisation is not established;
3. the criminal offence is committed by a group if at least three persons participate in its commission;
4. violent conduct also includes aggressive physical action exerted on another person, even if it is not capable of causing bodily injury;
5. commits the criminal offence while armed if the person
a) * has in their possession a functioning firearm,
b) explosive material,
c) explosive substance,
d) a device for the use of explosive material or explosive substance
or commits the criminal offence by threatening with an imitation of those specified in points a)-d);
6. commits the criminal offence while equipped with weapons if the person has in their possession an instrument capable of taking life in order to overcome or prevent resistance;
7. threat: in the absence of a provision to the contrary, the prospect of serious harm which is capable of causing serious fear in the person threatened;
8. * business organisation: in addition to the business organisation as defined in the Code of Civil Procedure, also an organisation whose civil law relations in connection with its business activity are subject to the provisions applicable to business organisations under the Code of Civil Procedure;
9. * authority also includes the court and the prosecution service;
10. war:
a) the situations defined in common Articles 2 and 3 of the international conventions concluded in Geneva on 12 August 1949 for the protection of the victims of war, and in paragraph 4 of Article 1 of Additional Protocol I to those conventions,
b) the situations defined in Article 1 of Additional Protocol II to the conventions referred to in point a),
c) * a state of war,
d) a state of emergency,
e) in the case of war crimes and military offences, also the deployment of the Hungarian Defence Forces abroad;
11. official person:
a) the President of the Republic,
b) * a Member of Parliament, a nationality spokesperson and a Member of the European Parliament elected in Hungary,
c) a judge of the Constitutional Court,
d) * the Prime Minister, a minister, a state secretary, an administrative state secretary, a deputy state secretary and a lord-lieutenant,
e) a judge, a prosecutor and an arbitrator,
f) the Commissioner for Fundamental Rights and their deputy,
g) a notary public and a deputy notary public,
h) an independent judicial enforcement officer, a deputy independent judicial enforcement officer and an independent judicial enforcement officer candidate authorised for enforcement service,
i) a member of the representative body of a local government and a nationality self-government,
j) * a commander of the Hungarian Defence Forces with jurisdiction over the investigating authority, and the commander of a floating facility or an aircraft, if authorised to apply the provisions relating to the investigating authority,
k) * a person performing public authority tasks or service at the Constitutional Court, the Sándor Palace, the Office of the National Assembly, the Office of the Commissioner for Fundamental Rights, the National Bank of Hungary, the State Audit Office, a court, a prosecution service, a central state administrative body, the National Assembly Guard, a metropolitan or county government office, a local government administrative body or a public body, whose activity forms part of the proper functioning of the body,
l) * a member of an electoral commission;
12. person performing a public duty:
a) * a member of the defence forces performing service duties,
b) a person assigned to a civil defence organisation and performing civil defence service,
c) a neighbourhood watch member in the performance of their activities as defined in the Act on neighbourhood watch and the rules of neighbourhood watch activities,
d) * a religious person and a member of a religious association who professionally performs religious rites,
e) in court or other official proceedings, a defence counsel, a legal representative, an expert, and a service enforcement officer who does not qualify as an official person,
f) * a healthcare worker, another person in an employment relationship with a healthcare provider and a patient rights representative in the cases specified in the Act on Healthcare,
g) a member of the state ambulance service and another organisation authorised to provide rescue services in connection with rescue and patient transport,
h) * a member of a municipal and facility fire brigade and a voluntary fire brigade association in the performance of their firefighting and technical rescue duties,
i) * in the cases specified in the Act on National Public Education, a teacher and an employee directly assisting educational and teaching work, in the cases specified in the Act on Vocational Training, an instructor, and in the cases specified in the Act on National Higher Education, a teacher, instructor and scientific researcher of a higher education institution,
j) a person employed in a position specified in the Act on the Protection of Children and Guardianship Administration and in the Act on Social Administration and Social Benefits in the performance of this activity,
k) a member of forestry professional staff and authorised forestry professional staff in the scope of their activities specified in the Act on Forests, the Protection of Forests and Forest Management,
l) a professional hunter in the scope of their activities specified in the Act on the Protection of Game, Game Management and Hunting,
m) * a fishery guard in the scope of their activities specified in the Act on Fisheries Management and the Protection of Fish,
n) a person performing enforcement duties at a business organisation operating public transport vehicles in the course of this activity,
o) a person performing customer relations duties at a universal postal service provider in the course of this activity,
p) * a school guard in the scope of their activities specified in the Act on the Police,
q) * an armed security guard in the performance of their service,
r) * a person performing the monitoring of the proper operation of parking spaces and the proper use of parking spaces at an organisation providing parking public services aimed at ensuring parking of road vehicles on national and local public roads, on private roads not closed to public traffic owned by the state or a local government, and on squares, parks and other public areas, in accordance with the Act on Road Traffic, in the course of this activity,
s) * a road inspector and an operator inspector in the scope of their activities specified in the Act on Road Traffic;
13. foreign official person:
a) * a person performing legislative, judicial, administrative or law enforcement duties in a foreign state, or a person performing public authority tasks or service in a foreign state, including a person performing such tasks or service in a public body or in a state or municipal enterprise,
b) a person performing service at an international organisation established by an international treaty promulgated by law, whose activity forms part of the proper functioning of the organisation,
c) a person elected to the assembly or body of an international organisation established by an international treaty promulgated by law, including a Member of the European Parliament elected abroad,
d) a member of an international court having jurisdiction over the territory of Hungary or its nationals, a person performing service at an international court, whose activity forms part of the proper functioning of the court;
14. relative:
a) a lineal relative and their spouse or cohabitant,
b) an adoptive parent and a foster parent (including a cohabiting step-parent), an adopted child and a foster child (including a cohabiting step-child),
c) a sibling and the sibling's spouse or cohabitant,
d) a spouse, a cohabitant,
e) a lineal relative and sibling of a spouse or cohabitant;
15. information system: equipment ensuring the automatic processing, handling, storage and transmission of data, or a set of such equipment in connection with each other;
16. damage: in the absence of a provision to the contrary in this Act, the decrease in value caused in property by the criminal offence;
17. pecuniary disadvantage: in the absence of a provision to the contrary in this Act, the damage caused in property and the loss of pecuniary advantage;
18. * narcotic drug: a substance listed in the narcotic drugs lists 1 and 2 in Annex 1 and in the psychotropic substances lists 1 and 2 in Annex 2 of Decree 78/2022 (XII. 28.) of the Minister of the Interior;
19. * cash substitute payment instrument means a cash substitute payment instrument as defined in the Act on Credit Institutions and a negotiable order, a treasury card, a traveller's cheque, a voucher issued on the basis of an Act for the purpose of settling the consideration for goods or services of limited scope that may be given with or without tax payable by the payer, and a bill of exchange, provided that it is protected against copying, counterfeiting or unauthorised use by its design, coding or the signature on it;
20. electronic cash substitute payment instrument means, in addition to the cash substitute payment instrument as defined in the Act on Credit Institutions, a treasury card and an electronic voucher issued under the authorisation of the Act on Personal Income Tax, provided that they are used through an information system;
21. public utility:
a) a public utility,
b) a public transport undertaking,
c) an electronic communications network,
d) logistical, financial and IT centres and plants operated by the universal postal service provider for the performance of its public interest tasks,
e) a plant producing war material or military equipment, a plant producing energy or raw materials for industrial use;
22. in public on a large scale shall also be understood to mean the commission of a criminal offence through publication in a press product, media service, reproduction or on an electronic communications network;
23. weapon prohibited by international treaty:
a) the asphyxiating, poisonous and other similar gases and bacteriological means of warfare referred to in the Protocol signed at Geneva on 17 June 1925 for the Prohibition of the Use in War of Asphyxiating, Poisonous or Other Gases, and of Bacteriological Methods of Warfare, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 20 of 1955, the asphyxiating, poisonous and other similar gas, the bacteriological weapon,
b) the bacteriological (biological) and toxin weapon as defined in Article 1 of the Convention on the Prohibition of the Development, Production and Stockpiling of Bacteriological (Biological) and Toxin Weapons and on their Destruction, adopted at the twenty-sixth session of the United Nations on 10 December 1971, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 11 of 1975,
c) the Convention on Prohibitions or Restrictions on the Use of Certain Conventional Weapons Which May be Deemed to be Excessively Injurious or to Have Indiscriminate Effects, signed at Geneva on 15 October 1980, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 2 of 1984, and the
ca) weapon causing injury by fragments which are not detectable by X-rays as defined in Protocol I,
cb) mine, remotely delivered mine, anti-personnel mine, booby-trap and other device as defined in Article 2, paragraphs 1-5 of Amended Protocol II promulgated by Act CXXXIII of 1997,
cc) incendiary weapon as defined in Article 1, paragraph 1 of Protocol III,
cd) blinding laser weapon as defined in Article I of Protocol IV,
d) chemical weapon or chemical riot control agent as defined in Article II, paragraphs 1 and 7 of the Convention on the Prohibition of the Development, Production, Stockpiling and Use of Chemical Weapons and on their Destruction, signed in Paris on 13 January 1993, promulgated by Act CIV of 1997,
e) anti-personnel mine as defined in Article 2, paragraph 1 of the Convention on the Prohibition of the Use, Stockpiling, Production and Transfer of Anti-Personnel Mines and on their Destruction, adopted in Oslo on 18 September 1997, promulgated by Act X of 1998,
f) cluster munition as defined in Article 2, paragraph 2 of the Convention on Cluster Munitions promulgated by Act XI of 2012, and explosive bomblet as defined in Article 2, paragraph 13;
24. * assembly: an assembly falling within the scope of the Act on the Right of Assembly;
24a. * public event: an event, other than an assembly, which is open to everyone on the same conditions;
25. prostitution means the performance of sexual acts for the purpose of regular gain;
26. violent criminal offence against a person:
a) genocide [Section 142(1)], crime against humanity [Section 143(1)], apartheid [Section 144(1)-(3)],
b) violence against a war envoy (Section 148), violence against protected persons [Section 149(1)-(2) and (4)], other war crime (Section 158),
c) homicide [Section 160(1)-(3) and (5)], homicide committed in a state of strong agitation (Section 161), bodily harm [Section 164(3)-(6) and (8)],
d) * kidnapping [Section 190(1)-(4)], trafficking in human beings and forced labour [Section 192(1)-(6)], violation of personal liberty (Section 194), coercion (Section 195),
e) * sexual coercion (Section 196), sexual violence [Section 197(1)-(4a)], relationship violence (Section 212/A),
f) * violation of freedom of conscience and religion (Section 215), violence against a member of a community [Section 216(2)-(3)], violation of freedom of association and assembly [Section 217(1)],
g) violent change of the constitutional order [Section 254(1)], rebellion [Section 256(1)-(2)],
h) abuse in official proceedings [Section 301(1)-(2)], abuse in proceedings of a person performing a public duty [Section 302(1)-(2)], torture [Section 303(1)-(2)], unlawful detention (Section 304),
i) violence against an official person [Section 310(1)-(3) and (5)], violence against a person performing a public duty (Section 311), violence against a supporter of an official person or a person performing a public duty (Section 312), violence against an internationally protected person [Section 313(1)],
j) act of terrorism [Section 314(1)-(2)], seizure of a vehicle [Section 320(1)-(2)],
k) robbery [Section 365(1)-(4)], extortion (Section 367), taking the law into one's own hands [Section 368(1)-(2)],
l) qualified cases of mutiny [Section 442(2)-(6)] and violence against a superior or service agent [Section 445];
27. sexual act: sexual intercourse and any act seriously offending decency which is capable of, or is aimed at, arousing, maintaining or satisfying sexual desire;
28. commits the criminal offence for profit if the person seeks to obtain regular gain through the commission of criminal offences of the same or similar nature;
29. * a person shall also be considered incapable of defence if, by reason of their situation or condition, they are temporarily or permanently incapable of offering resistance;
30. protected cultural property shall also be understood to mean cultural property declared protected;
31. * a recidivist is the perpetrator of an intentional criminal offence if they were previously sentenced to imprisonment to be executed for an intentional criminal offence, and three years have not yet elapsed between the completion of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability and the commission of the new criminal offence;
a) * a special recidivist is a recidivist who commits a criminal offence of the same or similar nature on both occasions;
b) a multiple recidivist is a person who was sentenced to imprisonment to be executed as a recidivist before the commission of the intentional criminal offence, and three years have not yet elapsed between the completion of the last sentence or the termination of its enforceability and the commission of the new criminal offence punishable by imprisonment;
c) * a violent multiple recidivist is a multiple recidivist who commits a violent criminal offence against a person on all three occasions;
32. * internationally protected person: a foreign official person who enjoys immunity based on international law under the international treaty applicable to them;
33. * new psychoactive substance: a substance specified in Annex 3 of Decree 78/2022 (XII. 28.) of the Minister of the Interior;
34. * cultural heritage element protected abroad: in accordance with the Council of Europe Convention on Offences relating to Cultural Property, concluded in Nicosia on 19 May 2017, promulgated by Act CXXV of 2021,
a) movable cultural heritage element classified, defined or designated in accordance with Article 2, paragraph 2, point a), and
b) immovable cultural heritage element defined or designated in accordance with Article 2, paragraph 2, point b);
35. * humanitarian operation: assistance activity carried out with the consent of the host country or with the authorisation of a competent international organisation, with the substantive participation of the Defence Forces, aimed at eliminating and reducing the consequences of a situation caused by armed conflict, natural or civilisation disaster which seriously endangers or damages the lives, health, material values, basic supply of people, or the natural environment;
36. * humanitarian activity carried out in a foreign theatre of operations: participation of the Defence Forces in a territory endangered by armed clashes between opposing parties, for the purpose of ensuring the lives, physical integrity, health and basic goods necessary for the sustenance of the civilian population;
37. * prohibited foreign support: support originating from abroad the acceptance or use of which is prohibited by the Act on the Operation and Management of Political Parties or the Act on Electoral Procedure;
38. * responsible person: the responsible person as defined in the Act on Civil Organisations.
(2) Where this Act refers to a cohabitant, it shall also be understood to mean a registered cohabitant.
(3) * Where this Act refers to a member of the defence forces, a theatre of operations, allied armed forces or peacekeeping, it shall be understood to mean the concepts defined in the Act on National Defence and the Hungarian Defence Forces.
(3a) * Where this Act refers to rank, it shall be understood to mean, in the case of a member of the defence forces, the rank held, and in the case of a professional member of the police, the National Assembly Guard, the prison service, the professional disaster management body and the civilian national security services, the rank determined on the basis of the classification and pay grade of the service position held.
(4) Where this Act refers to data classified as top secret, secret, confidential or restricted, it shall be understood to mean national or foreign classified data as defined in the Act on the Protection of Classified Data.
(5) * For the purposes of this Act
a) a third-country national shall be understood to mean the concept defined in the Act on the general rules on the entry and residence of third-country nationals,
b) permit issued for the purpose of employment:
ba) the National Card,
bb) the Corporate Card issued under Section 48(2), point b) or c) of Act XC of 2023 on the general rules on the entry and residence of third-country nationals,
bc) residence permit for the purpose of seasonal employment,
bd) residence permit for employment purposes issued for the purpose of implementing an investment,
be) residence permit for employment purposes,
bf) guest worker residence permit,
bg) Hungarian Card,
bh) EU Blue Card,
bi) residence permit issued for the purpose of intra-corporate transfer (including the long-term mobility permit),
bj) residence permit for research purposes (including the long-term residence permit for researchers and the short-term mobility certificate for researchers),
bk) residence permit for study purposes (including the student mobility residence permit and the student mobility certificate),
bl) residence permit issued for the purpose of ensuring family unity in the framework of a combined application procedure,
bm) residence permit issued for humanitarian purposes,
bn) residence permit for the purpose of posting.
(6) For the purposes of this Act, value, damage and pecuniary disadvantage
a) between fifty thousand and one and five hundred thousand forints is minor,
b) between five hundred thousand and one and five million forints is major,
c) between five million and one and fifty million forints is significant,
d) between fifty million and one and five hundred million forints is particularly large,
e) above five hundred million forints is particularly significant.


This excerpt sits under: The state of the legislation in force today (14.02.2024).

## 460. §

(1) For the purposes of Chapter XIV, a protected person is one who does not take a direct part in hostilities, in particular
a) a member of the armed forces who has laid down his or her arms and, having withdrawn from hostilities, has surrendered;  
b) a person who has clearly become incapable of fighting as a result of illness, injury, capture or other cause, or has clearly withdrawn from hostilities;  
c) personnel of a humanitarian aid or peacekeeping mission operating under the Charter of the United Nations, if they are entitled to the protection afforded to civilians or civilian objects under the international law of armed conflict;d) a civilian, to the extent and for as long as he or she does not take a direct part in hostilities.  
(2) For the purposes of Chapter XXVI
a) a disciplinary matter shall be considered to be a procedure for which an act of law establishes the conduct constituting a disciplinary offence, the detailed rules of the procedure and the disciplinary penalties that may be imposed;  
b) a civil matter shall also include a case pending before an arbitral tribunal.

## 461. §

(1) For the purposes of §§ 176–180, a narcotic drug is of a small quantity if
a) its pure active substance content expressed in base form
aa) in the case of LSD, does not exceed 0.001 gram,
ab) in the case of psilocybin, does not exceed 0.1 gram,
ac) in the case of psilocin, does not exceed 0.2 gram,
ad) * in the case of amphetamine, methamphetamine, MDPV and alpha-PVP, does not exceed 0.5 gram,
ae) in the case of dihydrocodeine, does not exceed 0.8 gram,
af) in the case of heroin, does not exceed 0.6 gram,
ag) in the case of morphine, does not exceed 0.9 gram,
ah) in the case of ketamine, codeine, MDA, MDMA, N-ethyl-MDA (MDE), MBDB, 1-PEA, N-methyl-1-PEA, mCPP, methadone, 4-fluoroamphetamine and pethidine, does not exceed 1 gram,
ai) in the case of mephedrone, methylone and 4-MEC, does not exceed 1.5 grams,
aj) in the case of cocaine, does not exceed 2 grams,
ak) in the case of BZP, does not exceed 3 grams,
al) * in the case of pentedrone, does not exceed 0.4 gram,
b) in the case of GHB, its pure active substance content expressed in acid form does not exceed 7.5 grams,
c) in the case of tetrahydrocannabinol (THC), the pure and acid form THC content present together (total THC) does not exceed 6 grams,
d) * its pure active substance content
da) in the case of GBL, does not exceed 6.2 grams,
db) in the case of AB–CHMINACA, MDMB–CHMICA, AM–2201, AB–PINACA, AB–FUBINACA, and ADB–FUBINACA, does not exceed 0.05 gram.  
(2) For the purposes of §§ 176–180, a narcotic drug is of a small quantity if, in the case of a cannabis plant, the number of plant individuals does not exceed five.  
(3) For the purposes of §§ 176–180, the narcotic drug referred to in paragraphs (1)–(2)
a) is of a significant quantity if it exceeds twenty times the upper limit of the small quantity specified for the given narcotic drug,
b) is of a particularly significant quantity if it exceeds two hundred times the upper limit of the small quantity specified for the given narcotic drug.  
(4) For the purposes of §§ 176–180, in the case of a narcotic drug not listed in paragraphs (1)–(2), the narcotic drug
a) is of a small quantity if its pure active substance content does not exceed seven times the average effective dose for a non-habituated consumer,
b) is of a significant quantity if its pure active substance content exceeds one hundred and forty times the average effective dose for a non-habituated consumer,
c) is of a particularly significant quantity if its pure active substance content exceeds one thousand four hundred times the average effective dose for a non-habituated consumer.  
(5) * For the purposes of §§ 184–184/C, a new psychoactive substance is of a small quantity if its pure active substance content in the list contained in Annex 3 of Decree 78/2022 (XII. 28.) BM *
a) in the case of the group of compounds under point 1, does not exceed 0.5 gram,
b) in the case of the group of compounds under point 2, does not exceed 1.5 grams,
c) in the case of the group of compounds under point 3, does not exceed 2 grams,
d) in the case of the group of compounds under point 4, does not exceed 1 gram,
e) in the case of the group of compounds under point 5, does not exceed 0.1 gram,
f) in the case of the substances under point 6, does not exceed 2 grams.  
(6) * For the purposes of §§ 184–184/C, a new psychoactive substance is of a significant quantity if its pure active substance content exceeds twenty times the upper limit of the small quantity.  
(7) * For the purposes of §§ 184/B and 184/C, a new psychoactive substance is of a particularly significant quantity if its pure active substance content exceeds two hundred times the upper limit of the small quantity.  
(8) * In the case of compounds occurring in salt form, pure active substance content shall mean the active substance content expressed in base form.

## 462. § (1) No criminal offence is committed if

a) negligent handling is committed causing pecuniary disadvantage not exceeding one hundred thousand forints,
b) abuse of social security, social or other welfare benefits is committed causing damage not exceeding fifty thousand forints.  
(2) Not a criminal offence but a petty offence is committed if
a) vandalism and fraud are committed causing damage not exceeding fifty thousand forints,
b) * theft, embezzlement and misappropriation are committed for a value not exceeding fifty thousand forints,
c) breach of trust is committed causing pecuniary disadvantage not exceeding fifty thousand forints,
d) infringement of copyright or related rights is committed not exceeding one hundred thousand forints
da) in respect of blank media fees or reprographic fees,
db) causing pecuniary disadvantage,
e) infringement of industrial property rights is committed causing pecuniary disadvantage not exceeding one hundred thousand forints,
f) * placing a product of poor quality on the market or imitation of a competitor is committed for a value not exceeding one hundred thousand forints,
g) * money laundering is committed for a value not exceeding fifty thousand forints, in accordance with § 177 (1a) of Act II of 2012 on Petty Offences, Petty Offence Proceedings and the Petty Offence Registration System.  
(3) * No criminal offence is committed, or a customs petty offence is committed, if the pecuniary disadvantage caused by budgetary fraud does not exceed five hundred thousand forints.  
(4) * A criminal offence is committed if, in the case of
a) * the same property petty offence committed by the same perpetrator within one year and adjudicated jointly, the value of the thing, the damage caused or the pecuniary disadvantage caused exceeds the amount specified in points a)–c) or g) of paragraph (2),
b) infringement of copyright or related rights, the blank media fee or the reprographic fee exceeds the amount specified in point d) of paragraph (2),
c) infringement of industrial property rights, the pecuniary disadvantage exceeds the amount specified in point e) of paragraph (2),
d) placing a product of poor quality on the market or imitation of a competitor, the value of the product or goods exceeds the amount specified in point f) of paragraph (2),
e) a customs petty offence, the pecuniary disadvantage exceeds the amount specified in paragraph (3)
as a result of aggregation of values.  
(5) * A criminal offence is committed in the case of infringement of copyright or related rights petty offence committed by the same perpetrator and adjudicated jointly, if the individual acts infringe the copyright or related right or rights of another or others under the Act on Copyright, causing pecuniary disadvantage exceeding the amount specified in point d) of paragraph (2).

## 463. §

This Act shall enter into force on 1 July 2013.
